# Decisions

## 2026-11-27: Automatic worktree backups and a scratch-build cleanup (1.1.3 maintenance)

- **[Fact] The problem:** worker restarts and usage limits have ended sessions mid-task, and agent worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` live only in the container; backing one up to `caw-backup-<name>` was a step to remember (`.claude/README.md`). Scratch builds are about 2 GB each and fill the disk.
- **Chosen:** `scripts/worktree-tidy.js`. `backup` pushes each worktree whose commits `origin/Main` lacks to `caw-backup-<folder name>` when that branch is missing or behind; it never commits, and warns about uncommitted changes. A `SubagentStop` hook in `.claude/settings.json` runs it whenever an agent finishes, so a finished agent's work is on GitHub before the main session reads its report. `clean` lists by default; `--apply` removes a worktree only when it has no uncommitted changes and Main or its backup branch holds its tip (its branch stays: deleting branches asks first), and a CMake build folder (`engine/build*`, or a scratchpad folder when `CLAUDE_SCRATCHPAD` is set) whose top-level entries are older than `--days` (default 1).
- **Checked:** `scripts/worktree-tidy.test.js` (which worktrees are pushed or removed, branch names, when a build is stale) runs in the *App* workflow's script tests.
- **Not chosen:** committing a worktree's uncommitted changes in the hook (it would put a half-done edit into the agent's history), and removing worktrees whose commits were squash-merged without a backup (their tips aren't on Main, so the tool can't tell; back them up first, then clean).

## 2026-11-27: CHANGELOG entries as one file per change, joined at release (1.1.3 maintenance)

- **[Fact] The problem:** each pull request added its lines to the next version's entry in `CHANGELOG.md` and its copy `app/assets/CHANGELOG.md`, so two of them conflicted on the same lines (`.claude/README.md` listed both CHANGELOGs among the files parallel branches collide in), and a release PR that wrote the entry had to recall every change by hand.
- **Chosen:** each change adds `changes/<short-name>.md`: a `### New`, `### Changed` or `### Fixed` line and one `- ` bullet in the changelog's voice, with continuation lines and bullets nested by two spaces (at most 40 lines, 4000 characters; [how to write one](../changes/README.md)). The version pull request runs `node scripts/changelog-join.js <version> <date> --summary "<line>"`: it puts `## <version> (<date>)`, the summary, then the groups in the order New, Changed, Fixed (files by name within a group) above the newest entry in both copies, and deletes the joined files. It writes nothing when a file doesn't parse, the copies differ, the version already has an entry or the entry would fail `changelog-notes.js` (the release job's own read, run on the result first).
- **Checked:** `scripts/changelog-join.test.js` parses every file in `changes/` and runs in the *App* workflow's script tests, whose path filters now include `changes/**`, so a pull request that only adds a change file still runs it.
- **Unchanged:** the What's new screen (it reads the joined entry, the same format as before), the bundled copy and its test, and the release job (`changelog-notes.js` still fails a release with no entry, so a version PR that forgot the join is caught there).
- **Not chosen:** joining in the release job (the bundled copy would then differ from the release's notes, and the app ships the copy), a front-matter format (the file reads as the changelog lines it becomes), and an "unreleased" entry kept in CHANGELOG.md (it is the shared line two PRs collide on).

## 2026-11-27: Bench modes and C API versions come from one list each (1.1.3 maintenance)

- **[Fact] The failure (git log, 2026-11-27):** parallel branches picked the same next number and the merge kept both. C API: 24 twice (`7d2b5e5` speed-parallel and `2aeb885`, fixed by `83b8474` "C API 25 instead of 24"), 40 twice (`b91a05d` MIDI effects and `bf38c8e` Play status, fixed at the merge `175460c`, which left `engine.md` saying `cawApiVersion` 40 for API 41), and the engine bumped ahead of the app (`468ffef` caw.h 42 with the app at 41, `0603ba4` 44 with the app at 43, `9542aa7` 37 with the app at 36) while the app test carried a third copy (`b91a05d` left it at 39). Bench: Timber (`e4fcff9`) and the sampler (`382c219`) both took mode 308 and Rook (`a29818d`) took 321, renumbered at their merges (`1aad35b` Timber 321, `d75c635` Rook 323); a kept duplicate `if (only == N)` compiles, the second branch is dead and its ctest silently times the other device. An unlisted mode number played scenario 5 and passed.
- **Chosen:** the bench modes are one `constexpr` table, `engine/bench/bench_modes.h` (number, name, ctest, ctest arguments, what): a `static_assert` fails the build on a number, name or ctest used twice, `caw_bench.cpp` selects by name through `consteval bench::mode("...")` (an unknown name doesn't compile), CMake makes the cost ctests from the rows (re-run when the header changes), and an unlisted number exits 2 with the list. The C API's list stays `caw.h`'s history comment (a C++ table of it would be a second copy); `scripts/number-lists.js`, run by `docs-test.js` (so on every pull request) and tested in the *Dart tests* job, fails two entries for one version, a gap, a `#define` that isn't the last entry, the app's `cawApiVersion` apart from it, a note's "`CAW_API_VERSION` N" or "`cawApiVersion` N" that is neither current nor the version its "C API N" line is about, and a `caw_bench S N` in the notes, workflows or CMake that names no listed mode. The Dart constant must stay a literal (no compile-time read of the header), so a Dart test compares it with `caw.h` instead of a hard-coded number.
- **Not chosen:** a new CI job (both checks ride ones that already run), generating the Dart constant from the header (another build step for one number), and changing a number or a public signature.

## 2026-11-27: The long CI checks split into jobs that name their failing test (owner, 1.1.3 maintenance)

- **Owner (2026-11-24):** "break down the big checks: split the long CI jobs (Dart tests, engine shards, the sanitizer build) into smaller ones whose failure names its test."
- **[Fact] Before (step timestamps, Engine runs 45904645556 and 45933498369, App run 45933567534, 2026-11-26/27):** *Tests (Linux sanitizers)* took 77-109 minutes: its build 1-1.5 minutes with the compiler cache, its CTest step 74-107, waiting on `caw_tests_shard_1` (4,466-6,424 s; the other shards 960-2,170 s). *Dart tests*: 14 minutes, 12 of them `flutter test`. Windows engine tests: 15-17 minutes, 7.5-8.5 of them the build (no compiler cache).
- **[Fact] Where the sanitizer time goes (measured here 2026-11-27, GCC 13 ASan/UBSan Debug, 4 cores, one process per case group):** `rook_every_control_of_each_kinds_default_kit_is_audible` takes 1,738 s alone, `drums_audit_every_pad_setting_changes_the_sound` about 1,030 s and every other case under 440 s; all cases about 8,000 s. At 4 or 8 shards the two slowest share a shard. **Decision:** the sanitizer build registers 14 shards (`CAW_TEST_SHARDS`, a CMake cache variable; 4 stays the default, and the count changes only the split, never which cases run), one CI job per shard runs it on one thread and on the mix pool and compares that shard's render digests (`CompareDigests.cmake` takes the shards to compare; every render belongs to one shard, so this is as strict as the one comparison over all), and a fifteenth job runs the other CTest tests. The slowest shard is then about 1,930 s, so the sanitizers take about 35 minutes (the slowest case and 3 minutes of setup and build), not 75-110. A *Tests (Linux sanitizers)* job fails unless all fifteen passed.
- **Each sanitizer job builds for itself, not one build passed on as an artifact:** with the shared compiler cache the build takes 1-1.5 minutes, while the build directory a test job needs holds a 578 MB `caw_tests` and a 306 MB `libcaw.so` (measured here). One build then N downloads would put a whole job (setup, build, upload) before every test job, and the downloads cost about what the cached build does. Only the other-tests job, which builds everything, saves the cache.
- **The Dart tests:** four jobs, *Dart tests (1/4)* to *(4/4)*, each running a quarter of the test files (first every fourth file of the sorted list, then by measured time, below). Not `flutter test --total-shards`: package:test (test_core 0.6.18, `runner.dart`'s `_shardSuite`) splits each file's tests by index, so every job would still compile and load all 222 files. Each job writes a JSON report beside the default reporter, and `scripts/dart-test-summary.js` puts each failing test's file, line, name and first error line in the job's summary and as an annotation. A *Dart tests* job keeps the old check's name, runs the scripts' Node tests and fails unless all four passed; the GitHub Release still needs it.
- **Windows engine tests stay one job:** most of the job is its uncached build, which each part would repeat. Every engine test job's summary now names its failing cases (`scripts/ctest-summary.js`: CTest's failed entries, each `FAIL` line, sanitizer reports).
- **[Fact] After (PR #282's own runs, Engine 45952907456 and App 45952907435, 2026-11-27):** the runners took the extra jobs without queueing (an Engine run starts 20 jobs, an App pull request run 6). *Tests (Linux sanitizers)* went green 28 minutes after the push (was 77-109): the slowest shard, 10/14, took 23 minutes and most 3-13. *Dart tests* took 11 minutes (was 14), but unevenly: shard 1/4 10.7 minutes, the other three 2-4.
- **The Dart files are balanced by measured time (2026-11-27), not every fourth file:** `scripts/dart-test-times.js` turns a full run's JSON report into `app/test/test_times.json` (each file's seconds, sorted by path), and `scripts/dart-test-shard.js` gives each job its files: heaviest first, each to the job with the least time so far, ties by path, every file in exactly one job, an error for an empty job; a file missing from the table weighs the table's median, and entries for files that are gone are ignored. A file's time is its wall-clock span from its suite event (loading, which includes compiling it) to its last test's end, not the sum of its tests' times: a job pays for the compile and the gaps between tests too (over this run the two differ by 2 s in 2,031 s, so either would do). Each job passes its files heaviest first, since `flutter test` starts them in the order given (**[Assumption]**: read from the run's order, not the runner's source).
- **[Fact] The measurement (here, 2026-11-27, 4 cores, `flutter test --concurrency=4`, without `CAW_LIB`, so the 71 files that use the engine library skipped those parts):** 222 files, 2,031 s of file time, 13 minutes 18 s in all. `use_like_owner_test.dart` takes 458 s alone; next are `device_uis_test.dart` 88 s, `instrument_pages_test.dart` 69, `perf_knobs_bench_test.dart` 65 and `drums_ui_test.dart` 53, and the median file 2.8 s. Every fourth file put 809, 273, 571 and 379 s in the four jobs (about 543, 83, 149 and 112 s of wall time at four at once: job 1 matches its 10.7 minutes with setup); by time each job gets 508 s, but the one holding `use_like_owner_test.dart` still runs about 458 s (7.6 minutes) and the others about 127. **`use_like_owner_test.dart` is split (2026-11-27), no test changed or lost:** its big group, *every control of every device answers the owner's finger*, runs in `use_like_owner_1_test.dart` to `use_like_owner_4_test.dart` under the same group and test names, with the harness in `test/use_like_owner/harness.dart`; the MIDI-effect test, the up-and-down group and a new guard (the four parts run every device exactly once) stay in `use_like_owner_test.dart`. Devices go to the parts greedily by each one's measured time (`parts`, heaviest first to the lightest part; a device not in the harness's table weighs the median), not every fourth one. **[Fact] Measured here 2026-11-27, without `CAW_LIB`, the files one at a time:** before, the file took 458 s (99 devices 412 s, Drums alone 122.5 s, Flagship 41.5); after, the parts take 149.7 s (Drums alone, which sets the floor), 107.8, 101.9 and 100.5 s and `use_like_owner_test.dart` 44.3, 504 s in all (each file compiles and loads the harness). The sorted test names of the two runs match but for the guard. With these times in `test_times.json` each job gets about 520 s of file time, and no file holds a job past about 150 s. Re-measure (`node scripts/dart-test-times.js <report.json>`, which writes only the report's files: merge a partial report into the table by hand) when files change a lot, and the harness's device times when a device's page grows.

## 2026-11-26: Curlew's four new winds and their page (owner)

- **Owner (2026-11-26):** "Use your recommendations for the questions you ask me." Curlew adds Piccolo, Oboe and Tuba (winds VSCO 2 CE has CC0 sustains of; the alto flute and other saxophones wait for recordings), appended as variants 6-8 so saved projects keep their meaning. Its page shows the cards in **two rows**, woodwinds (Flute, Piccolo, Clarinet, Oboe, Sax) over brass (Trumpet, Trombone, Horn, Tuba), and each new instrument is drawn in the **existing style**: a thin outline of its bore with the standing wave inside (the piccolo a short straight tube, the oboe a narrow cone with a small bell, the tuba a big coil with an upturned bell).
- **Bassoon not built (2026-11-26):** on the sax's cone its notes under C4 took 0.4-0.9 s to reach half their level at every steady setting tried (reed slope, a stiffer reed, a tongue pulse into the bore), so they sounded late and failed `instrument_no_stray_peaks_on_any_key`. It comes as variant 9 once a bassoon model speaks in time (a real one takes about 50-100 ms; assumption, not measured here).
- **Standing (owner, 2026-11-26):** when the owner is asked a question, Claude's recommended option is the answer unless the owner says otherwise.

## 2026-11-26: Claude merges and releases on its own (owner)

- **Owner (2026-11-26):** "You don't need my permission to merge and release. Just continue on your way and merge and release when required." Claude merges its pull request to Main once CI is green and the release check passes, and cuts a release (app.yml `release`) when a set of finished fixes is ready for the owner to try. The earlier "don't merge PR #269 without the owner's ask" is superseded. What is checked before a release is unchanged (2026-11-25 below), and UI looks are still asked first.

## 2026-11-25: Bittern is the Salt Lake Tabernacle organ's divisions, on one keyboard (Claude, owner's request)

- **Owner:** "put a lot of work in ... the organ ... it shouldn't just be a bunch of instruments bundled together it should be a genuine organ, use the one that the Mormon Tabernacle choir uses at Temple square."
- **Decision:** Bittern models that organ's structure ([organ research](organ-research-tabernacle.md), section 6): 26 of its stops by their names and pitches in the Great, Swell, Choir, Antiphonal and Pedal, built on the measured pipes of the rebuilt-from-recordings organ ([engine](engine.md), Bittern). The open question "should the swell box enclose only the Swell" is settled by the real organ (a fact, not a preference): the Swell and the Choir are enclosed, so the box and the tremulant act on those only.
- **One keyboard plays manuals and pedal:** the Pedal's stops play the lowest key held (or every key, the Pedal choice), and every manual division sounds on every key, as if coupled to the Great; separate couplers would do nothing with one keyboard. **[Assumption]** what a one-keyboard player wants; the owner can ask for a split point instead.
- **Ids kept:** the alpha's stop ids stay on the stop of the same kind of pipe, so alpha projects load; `celeste8` is now part of the Viole Céleste II. Alpha projects sound different (the alpha compatibility policy: similar, not the same).
- **Silent speech clips:** 13 of the speech asset's 64 clips are empty (the generator kept clips with near-zero residuals and large scales). The organ marks them when the bank loads and never plays one; regenerating the asset is left for when its source recordings are re-fetched.
- **Not built:** the Antiphonal behind the listener (a voice is mono before the pan), the Solo, Positiv and Bombarde divisions, and the 32' Montre extension.

## 2026-11-25: MIDI effects leave the device library until MIDI routing (Claude, in the 1.1.3-alpha.2 knob fix)

- **Fact:** a MIDI effect (role `event_processor`: Hop, Clutch, Perch, Mock, Ruffle) plays only from a track's MIDI components, which the app has no UI for; placed on an effect chain, the engine leaves it out, so its knobs did nothing (part of "the knobs do nothing", 1.1.3-alpha.1).
- **Decision:** the library no longer offers them for chains (`app/lib/model/files_catalog.dart`, test "the library offers no MIDI effect for a chain"). They return with the MIDI routing work (roadmap 1.1.4). Old projects keep them in their files.

## 2026-11-25: How work is checked before the owner gets it (owner's feedback on 1.1.3-alpha.1, with the advisor session's review)

The owner found that in 1.1.3-alpha.1 most knobs and all effects seemed to do nothing, Felt and Upright sounded like the grand, several knobs were dead, Bittern sounded like a synth, Curlew's growl was wrong, Nightingale had stray peaks, noises came at the wrong moments, the new instruments had no pages, and hours went into Timber, which they replace. These are standing rules from now on; nobody needs to restate them:

- **"No listening gates" means Claude verifies, not less checking.** Nothing ships that the owner cannot use on the phone as they use it. A small complete release beats a big half-done one.
- **Every control does something audible:** every parameter of every built-in, every variant and every preset differs measurably (an automated test sweeps each one; a dead knob is a failing test). Variants of one instrument must differ spectrally (Felt, Upright and Grand).
- **Never work on what is being replaced:** before starting, ask whether it is the end state. Timber was being replaced, so tuning it was wasted; the new instruments stand alone.
- **A headline feature ships with its UI** in the same release (never the generic knob page for a new instrument; "resemble and upgrade" is the look approval for instrument pages).
- **A global interaction change is tested on every control type** before it ships: knobs, sliders, XY pads, envelope handles, choices and effect pages, inside scroll views and sideways panels, with touch and mouse, as the owner holds the phone.
- **Before a release, render every preset to audio and analyse it:** peak against RMS (stray peaks), the spectral distance between variants (identical sounds), noise events against note onsets (noises at the wrong moment), and each instrument's spectrum against a CC0 recording of the real one (a synth-like organ). Add a "use it like the owner" widget test: a new project, add the instrument, open its page at phone size, turn every control with the shipped gesture, play, and check the engine got the values; and check it can be found from a fresh project.
- **CI:** run the full local suite (and the Arm64 qemu build for engine changes) before pushing; push once per complete fix, not per tweak.
## 2026-11-25: Timber removed; its projects load as the new instruments, once (owner's 1.1.3 step, Claude building it)

- **Owner (2026-11-25):** remove Timber (`org.caw.timber`) under the alpha compatibility policy (Hub decisions 2026-11-24: old projects sound similar, not the same): no Classic DSP or legacy paths kept, no DSP in two copies; `org.caw.timber` stays only as a load-time alias; each old model and its knobs map once, on load, to the new instrument's nearest preset plus knobs mapped by meaning; no old project may crash. Piano → Starling; Bowed → Nightingale; Winds → Curlew; Mallets → Woodpecker; Plucked → Starling's guitar and harp variants (being added; Starling's piano until they exist); a pipe-like organ → Bittern; the electric piano → Kestrel ("it's just a synthesized piano"); the tonewheel organ → Kestrel too, **[Open question, awaiting the owner]**: kept behind one flag (`kTonewheelToKestrel`, `engine/src/timber_migrate.cpp`). Searching "Timber" still finds the new instruments; a project that had Timber says so once, in the open-notice line.
- **Built:** the table is in [engine](engine.md#timber-removed-in-113). The electric piano and tonewheel organ are Kestrel presets, "Electric piano (tine)", "Electric piano (reed)" and "Tonewheel organ" (FM, and three sines at the drawbar footages), not Timber's DSP moved: [Assumption] credible with presets alone, judged from the synthesis, not by ear; the owner's ear decides, and moving the DSP in remains the fallback. A Timber organ counts as pipe-like with its rotary off, no percussion and drive at most 0.2 (Claude's call: those are the tonewheel's own marks); its percussion and key click are not carried to Kestrel.
- **Classic pruned from the library (lead, 2026-11-25):** `timber::Piano` keeps only the Simulated path (the commuted Classic loop, its excitation tables, `timber_tables.cpp` and its sympathetic bank gone; the parameter list loses `sim`), `timber::Wind` only the Simulated winds (the Classic excitation, its pitch tables and the pad clicks Curlew had off gone; it loses `sim` and `noises`). Starling's and Curlew's sound is meant to be unchanged; their reference renders say whether it is.
- **Each old project keeps its own Space** (the new instruments default dry): the room it had is the room it plays in. Lanes move only where the old knob means the same in the same units; the others are dropped, as for the retired synths.
- **Tests:** Timber's own tests, its compat hash tables (and their per-build tables) and references are gone; the piano model's physics checks were ported to Starling (`starling_model_test.cpp`); `timber_migrate_test.cpp` loads every model, Classic and Simulated knobs, NaN, out-of-range and unknown model indexes.
## 2026-11-25: Starling's classical guitar and harp: recorded plucks carried on by a string filled from their own past (Claude, building the owner's 1.1.3 step 6)

- **The instruments as built:** Starling's `variant` 1 (Classical guitar) and 2 (Harp), the piano untouched at 0 (its own file and compile flags, so its references hold bit for bit) ([engine](engine.md#starlings-classical-guitar-and-harp-113)). The string is Timber's extended Karplus-Strong loop from the shared `timber_*.h` library (not Timber's device), with measured dispersion and decays, a linear-phase loss and fitted bodies.
- **In phase by filling the loop with the recording:** at the end of each clip's hold the delay line holds the recording's own last samples (its body taken off by the bands' exact inverse) and the loop's filters have run over them, so the string continues the recording rather than being aligned to it (Woodpecker fits modes; a string has too many partials for that). Each clip's pitch is measured over its crossfade and its first, faster decay too, so the handover neither drifts nor swells.
- **No steel-string guitar:** **[Fact]** VCSL has no guitar; FreePats' steel guitar is GPL-3 with an exception (not permissive); Karoryfer's CC0 guitars (Emilyguitar, Shinyguitar, Black and Green) are electric; Malaclypse's Ella Gitauru steel acoustic is MIT but shipped as a RAR of unclear recording method (read 2026-11-25). Built what the recordings support: the nylon guitar and the harp. A steel guitar can be a later variant (index 3) once a CC0 or CC-BY source is found.
- **Per-variant labels, one model:** the guitar's Finger / nail, Palm mute and Sustain pedal are the harp's Finger, Damping and Let ring (on by default: harp strings ring until damped).
- **Strict floating point for `starling_strings.cpp`** (model and frame), so the references are Stable on every build.
- **Dependencies:** none new (miniaudio's FLAC decoder). The script uses numpy, scipy and soundfile (BSD, development only); unpacking FreePats' 7z used py7zr locally (LGPL, a tool, never shipped or committed).
- **Recordings** (CC0 1.0, both): FreePats' *Spanish Classical Guitar* SFZ+FLAC 2019-06-18 by Roberto for the FreePats project (<https://freepats.zenvoid.org/Guitar/acoustic-guitar.html>, read 2026-11-25; the archive 4,713,892 bytes, sha256 903916921a21662d2237ade7f0e98e55de93cb7b86da219e4e10f4ad385b8f5e), embedded as `engine/assets/starling/guitar.flac` (147,978 bytes: 16 clips of 134 ms and their histories); and VCSL's concert harp (<https://github.com/sgossner/VCSL>, commit c1ea7bcc, read 2026-11-25), embedded as `engine/assets/starling/harp.flac` (418,830 bytes: 46 clips of 149 ms and their histories). Credited in the app's licences and `engine/assets/starling/CREDITS.md`. Per file (source size):
  - FreePats `samples/` (16 files, 1.29 MB): E2.flac 122,883, G2.flac 122,348, A2.flac 122,471, C3.flac 97,009, E3.flac 109,663, G3.flac 109,718, A#3.flac 65,413, C#4.flac 76,764, E4.flac 98,031, G4.flac 71,824, A#4.flac 67,526, C#5.flac 54,098, E5.flac 47,804, G5.flac 49,878, A#5.flac 44,256, C6.flac 34,382.
  - VCSL `Chordophones/Composite Chordophones/Concert Harp` (45 files used, 82.6 MB; kB): KSHarp_E1_f1 3729, KSHarp_G1_f1 2786, KSHarp_B1_mf1 2284, KSHarp_B1_f1 4472, KSHarp_D2_mf1 1741, KSHarp_D2_f1 2452, KSHarp_F2_mf1 3086, KSHarp_F2_f1 4239, KSHarp_A2_mf1 2007, KSHarp_A2_f1 1956, KSHarp_C3_mf3 1886, KSHarp_C3_f2 2788, KSHarp_E3_mf1 2461, KSHarp_E3_f1 2811, KSHarp_G3_mf1 1806, KSHarp_G3_f1 2463, KSHarp_B3_mf1 1981, KSHarp_B3_f1 2095, KSHarp_D4_mf1 1921, KSHarp_D4_f1 1992, KSHarp_F4_mf1 1913, KSHarp_F4_f1 1873, KSHarp_A4_mf1 2143, KSHarp_A4_f1 2057, KSHarp_C5_mf1 1237, KSHarp_C5_f1 2238, KSHarp_E5_mf1 1171, KSHarp_E5_f1 1728, KSHarp_G5_mf1 1049, KSHarp_G5_f1 938, KSHarp_B5_mf1 779, KSHarp_B5_f1 739, KSHarp_D6_mf1 687, KSHarp_D6_f1 1314, KSHarp_F6_mf1 413, KSHarp_F6_f1 775, KSHarp_A6_mf1 636, KSHarp_A6_f1 544, KSHarp_B6_mf1 483, KSHarp_B6_f1 551, KSHarp_D7_p1 200, KSHarp_D7_f1 498, KSHarp_F7_p1 230, KSHarp_F7_f1 332 (all .wav; KSHarp_G1_mp1, 0.12 periodic over its crossfade, is replaced by G1_f1).
## 2026-11-25: Rook's realistic kit: recorded parts, the kinds up front, Randomness (Claude, on the owner's feedback after 1.1.2)

**Owner (after 1.1.2):** "Rook: I see no difference." A new Rook opened on the old 808 kit; the Synth, Produced and Acoustic kinds were reached only through 24 presets listed last, uncategorised, or a minimal Build a kit card; the first realistic kit is a dry studio rock/pop kit with synthesized heads and recorded transients, shells, wires and cymbals (CC0 kits), at most about 3 MB; Rook keeps two modes, Synthesized and Simulated, realistic meaning close-miked, almost dry, subtle noises only where the real kit makes them, with a Noise knob; Randomize gets a Randomness knob and keeps each pad its type. **Built** ([engine](engine.md#recorded-parts-113), [app](app.md)).
- **What was wrong (found):** nothing in the engine told the kinds apart for the owner: `kit_kind` was metadata, a new instrument took the descriptor's defaults (the 808 kit), and Randomize on a Simulated pad replaced it with a synthesized sound. Measured now by character, never level (per pad: centroid, third-octave shares, attack, T60, each independent of loudness): Synth and Acoustic differ on all 5 pads (mean band distance 20.0 dB), Produced and Acoustic on all 5 (19.0 dB), Synth and Produced on 4 of 5 (10.1 dB; their crashes alike) ([engine](engine.md#recorded-parts-113)).
- **Recordings on the model, per part (Claude, the owner's split):** a drum's clip is high-passed over its head's lowest modes and layered on the simulated head, so the head keeps its tuning, glide, damping and sympathy while the stick, shell, rim and wires are real; a hat's or cymbal's recording replaces its plate (no model runs at Recorded 1) and its own knobs (tuning, decay, damping, alloy, swell) act on the recording. Chosen over Woodpecker's in-phase crossfade: a struck drum's attack is mostly stick and shell, not head modes, so the transient can sit on the model without aligning partials.
- **Recorded** (`pc<n>_rec`, appended; 0 keeps 1.1.2's sound) and **Randomness** (`randomness`, appended, 0.5: Randomize's amount, no sound of its own) are new parameters (14,280 in all); the engine choice is labelled Synthesized / Simulated (values unchanged). Old projects load with both at their defaults: their kits sound as before, except that the Acoustic kit presets (and projects naming one as their `preset`) now play the recordings in their stick styles: similar, not identical (the owner's rule for this change).
- **A new Rook opens on "Acoustic kit: Studio"** (`LayerDoc.newInstrumentPresets`): the realistic studio kit is what most songs start from, its pads sit on General MIDI keys, and it is the kind the owner asked to hear first; the engine's defaults stay the 808 kit so older projects keep their sound. The kinds' best kits (the kind cards' targets): Synth kit: 808-style, Produced kit: Pop, Acoustic kit: Studio. **[Assumption]** Pop and 808-style were picked as each kind's most typical style; the owner may prefer others.
- **A Simulated pad's own settings need Synthesized** (the needs table): a Simulated pad strikes its piece, so its pan, tuning, layers, effects and most variation did nothing; the app now dims them with that reason. Making them act on a piece (per-pad routing of the kit's mics) is left for the Acoustic screens the owner will design.
- **Randomize keeps the type:** whatever a draw or its starting sound holds, each layer's decay ends within the type's length (a closed hat 0.12 s, a crash 3 s, a kick 1.8 s), no holds or gated decays, one-shot, drive at most 2 on its gentle curves, no clipper, the compressor at most 0.35 (`keepType`, `builtin_drums_sounds.cpp`; `rook_randomize_keeps_each_pad_clearly_its_type`). A Simulated pad's Randomize varies its piece within its kind (`randomizeAcousticJson`).
- **The kit tools stay** in a KIT TOOLS card; building a new kit moved to the kind cards (a tap on the playing kind lists its kits and "New … kit from a random seed").
- **Dependencies:** none new (miniaudio decodes the FLAC; the script uses numpy, scipy and soundfile, BSD, development only).
- **Recordings** ([engine](engine.md#recorded-parts-113)): Karoryfer Samples' **Unruly Drums** (<https://github.com/sfzinstruments/karoryfer.unruly-drums>, commit 9bf75c2) and **Big Rusty Drums** (<https://github.com/sfzinstruments/karoryfer.big-rusty-drums>, commit f07ce00). **[Fact, read 2026-11-25]** each repository's `LICENSE` is "CC0 1.0 Universal", and Karoryfer's free-samples page (karoryfer-samples.myshopify.com/pages/free-samples) says "All our free sample libraries are under a Creative Commons Zero license". Swirly Drums (brushes) was not used. Embedded as `engine/assets/rook/kit.flac` (1,177,302 bytes; 36 clips, 28.5 s, 16-bit mono at 44.1 kHz), credited in the app's licences and `engine/assets/rook/CREDITS.md`. Per file (source, CC0 1.0, source size):
  - Unruly Drums, kick 20" inside mic (`Samples/k20/in`): rr1/kick_clean_vl2_rr1.flac 42,172 B, rr1/kick_clean_vl5_rr1.flac 44,114 B, rr1/kick_clean_vl8_rr1.flac 44,112 B, rr2/kick_clean_vl8_rr2.flac 44,107 B.
  - Unruly Drums, snare 14" top mic (`Samples/s14/top`): rr1/s14_center_vl2_rr1.flac 43,191 B, rr1/s14_center_vl5_rr1.flac 45,260 B, rr1/s14_center_vl8_rr1.flac 46,448 B, rr2/s14_center_vl8_rr2.flac 47,648 B, rr1/s14_edge_vl3_rr1.flac 41,627 B, rr1/s14_edge_vl7_rr1.flac 45,495 B, rr1/s14_rimshot_vl3_rr1.flac 51,134 B, rr1/s14_rimshot_vl6_rr1.flac 54,336 B, rr1/s14_sstick_vl3_rr1.flac 36,931 B, rr1/s14_sstick_vl6_rr1.flac 39,319 B.

## 2026-11-24: Woodpecker, the tuned percussion: measured modes in phase with recorded attacks (Claude, building the owner's 1.1.3 step 5)

- **The instrument as built:** `org.caw.woodpecker`, one merged device with six instruments (Marimba, Xylophone, Glockenspiel, Vibraphone, Tubular bells, Timpani), each a modal model (up to 12 decaying modes a voice) whose modes, decays and tuning are measured from VCSL recordings, with that recording's first 30 ms crossfading into the model over 100 ms (the timpani 80 ms and 200 ms) ([engine](engine.md#woodpecker-tuned-percussion-113)). New code, not Timber's Mallet: Timber's tables are typical values chosen by ear.
- **The crossfade is in phase, not aligned by a lag:** the asset script fits the zone's modes to each clip over its crossfade (least squares, each mode decaying at its T60) and the model starts every mode at that complex amplitude carried back to the strike, so recording and model are the same partials in the same phase; the fades are complementary (smoothstep, summing to 1). Starling's lag-and-sign alignment was not needed: a modal model can match each partial's phase exactly. What the fitted modes leave out (the timpani's 47 % median, a bar's 1–5 %) is given to the modes as level (at most +6 dB) so the loudness carries on.
- **Every instrument plays in equal temperament at A440:** the recordings are played at the ratio that puts their reference mode there (VCSL's xylophone is 12–21 cents sharp, its glockenspiel 8–24, its chimes 2–11; measured), not at their own tuning as Starling's piano is: these instruments are tuned to a pitch standard, a piano is stretched.
- **Per-instrument knobs:** each variant's descriptor has only the knobs its instrument uses (the resonator on the marimba, xylophone and vibraphone; the motor on the vibraphone; the damper pedal on the vibraphone and the chimes), mapped onto one model's full set. **Eco** keeps Normal's 8 modes (they carry the crossfade) and plays 12 voices instead of 24; **High** plays 12 modes.
- **Strict floating point for `builtin_woodpecker.cpp`** (model and frame), so its reference render is Stable on every build (no fused multiply-adds on Arm64); there are no compat tables for it to break.
- **Recordings:** VCSL (CC0 1.0, Versilian Studios; <https://github.com/sgossner/VCSL>, read 2026-11-24), embedded as `engine/assets/woodpecker/mallets.flac` (1,225,596 bytes; 152 clips, 28.3 s of 16-bit mono at 44.1 kHz), loaded on first use like Starling's. **No new dependency:** the decoder is miniaudio's; the script uses numpy, scipy and soundfile (BSD, development only). Credited in the app's licences and `engine/assets/woodpecker/CREDITS.md`. Two substitutions (measured): Xylo_Hard_G3_pp sounds F#4 −87 ct, so the medium mallet's G3 pp stands in; the marimba's soft F5 and C6 sit at the noise floor (45 dB under loud), so its "med" layer is the soft layer throughout. Per file (source folder, CC0 1.0, source size):
  - `Idiophones/Struck Idiophones/Marimba` (20 files, 21.7 MB): Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F1_med_01.wav 1027 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F1_loud_01.wav 972 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C2_med_01.wav 1785 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C2_loud_01.wav 2236 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_G2_med_01.wav 1513 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_G2_loud_01.wav 1855 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_B2_med_01.wav 1496 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_B2_loud_01.wav 1586 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F3_med_01.wav 1383 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F3_loud_01.wav 1590 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C4_med_01.wav 655 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C4_loud_01.wav 1017 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_G4_med_01.wav 580 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_G4_loud_01.wav 800 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_B4_med_01.wav 434 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_B4_loud_01.wav 694 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F5_med_01.wav 567 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_F5_loud_01.wav 551 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C6_med_01.wav 452 kB, Marimba_hit_Outrigger_C6_loud_01.wav 472 kB.

## 2026-11-24: Nightingale's bow measured on the model; Starling's budget (Claude, building the owner's 1.1.3)

- **Nightingale:** the research's Schelleng force (fmax over beta, fmin over beta squared) did not fit this model, whose window was measured instead, as were the bow's pitch pull and the bodies (the recordings over the model, VSCO 2 CE, CC0). The tables are generated by tests run under `--update-references`, so a model change regenerates them ([engine](engine.md#nightingale-the-bowed-strings-113)).
- **Cost first, then the extras:** one Newton step at Normal (Timber's; the second cost 30 % and changed nothing measured), allpass delay reads (Lagrange's four taps cost more for the same flat gain), the body as 8 peaking filters (a convolution cost twice the strings): Nightingale sits within about 4 % of Timber's bowed strings.
- **Starling's ceiling is heavy plus 10 %** (26,500 µs/s at 16 voices; 20,000 at Eco): its recordings add about 10 % in the Benchmark's case of 32 notes struck a second. Halving that meant dropping the second velocity layer, which brings back audible steps in tone between layers; the owner's realism comes first, and the budget can be revisited.

## 2026-11-24: Starling's piano: recordings set the levels, aligned to the model, embedded and loaded on use (Claude, building the owner's hybrid)

- **The hybrid as built:** the Salamander Grand's first 200 ms per zone and layer, crossfading into Timber's Simulated piano over 40..160 ms, plus its key-release and pedal noises ([engine](engine.md#starling-the-hybrid-piano-113)). Chosen over a residual overlay (the recording with its partials removed): removing partials needs a spectral split that fails in the bass, where they lie closer than an analysis window resolves.
- **The recordings set the levels, not the model:** first matched the other way, the treble's strike was lifted 20 dB (to +5 dBFS), because the model's treble tone is far louder than a real one. Now each voice's model is scaled to its recording, so the Salamander's balance carries over.
- **Aligned crossfade:** recording and model share partials and cancelled in places; each clip's lag and polarity are calibrated against the model (generated, checked in CI with build tolerance).
- **The Salamander's own tuning** for the model and the recordings (its measured stretch, not equal temperament), so the crossfade does not beat.
- **Dependencies:** no new library: the FLAC decoder is miniaudio's, already used. The recordings are CC-BY 3.0 (permissive, attribution): credited in the app's licences and `engine/assets/starling/CREDITS.md`. The preparation script uses Python with numpy and soundfile (BSD, development only, never shipped).
- **Embedded, not app assets:** the engine (CLI, tests, every platform) has the bank without a file path; `recorded::acquire` decodes it only when an instrument uses it and frees it after (the owner's load-on-use rule).

## 2026-11-24: Playback 1.1.2: the phone's own mechanisms, no heat action, load never silences the song; the Benchmark plays every built-in (Claude, on the owner's 1.1.1 report)

**Owner (2026-11-24, S22+ on 1.1.1):** dropouts at a 20-30 % meter; playback stops or stalls when the phone warms or takes load; large spikes. "Look at how demanding applications work on phones; we're probably creating systems that already exist in the phone." "I don't care if the phone gets hot. Games don't get worse because the phone is hot; the system throttles, not the app." The smart-freeze hint must come only after real trouble; the Benchmark must play every device under its shown name ([performance](performance.md#playback-on-the-phone-112-2026-11-24)).
- **No heat action anywhere (owner):** the load stabilizer's heat backoff (1.1.1, below) and its "spin less on light projects" rule are gone; `caw_audio_set_thermal` acts on nothing (kept in the API); the offline worker plan no longer drops workers at thermal warm, hot or critical (`execution.cpp`; fewer workers on cores already slowed only piles the work onto fewer of them). The thermal state and the headroom (read every 10 s from launch) are information for the Performance sheet, never an action. Heat no longer stops a Benchmark either (owner, 2026-11-24, below).
- **The busy-spin is retired (Claude, on Google's guidance and the owner's Benchmark of 2026-11-24):** off unless `CAW_LOAD_STABILIZER=1` is in the environment when the device opens (a hidden comparison switch); on, it spins its full 0.8 share whatever the heat. **[Fact, source.android.com "Performance Hint API", read 2026-11-24]** a busy loop to raise the clock is "a bad practice as it wastes power and increases the thermal load"; **[Fact, the owner's Benchmark]** with it on, the callback's core ran at 0.5-0.6 of its top clock. The ADPF hint session stays as it is (the S22+ reports none: `hint_diag` in `caw_audio_threads` now shows the API level, `isFeatureSupported` and the raw preferred rate).
- **The buffer is the headroom (Claude, Oboe's LatencyTuner way):** the tuner is on by default (Settings key `buffer_tuning_on`, so the old default's saved "off" does not carry over); an output opened without a size (Low latency, Auto while recording) is tuned from two bursts; a fixed size (Auto's and Battery's 1024 frames, a chosen size) only grows, a burst at each AAudio underrun (`AAudioStream_getXRunCount`), never below its opened size. **Auto keeps its 1024 frames** against the advice not to ask a callback size: **[Fact, the owner's Benchmark]** that open ran the callback at SCHED_FIFO (`callback_policy` 1), the no-size Low-latency open came up on the normal mixer (960-frame bursts, policy 0). A Low-latency open on the normal mixer is opened once more (`fast_path_retry`); the status and the Benchmark's live rows show the path (`mmap`, `fast`, `normal`), MMAP, buffer, burst and capacity, the granted rate and modes. The live pool reads the callback's scheduling again every 256 callbacks (a fast track's real-time grant can come after the first callback).
- **Workers are not pinned on Android (Claude):** **[Fact, source.android.com "Performance Hint API", read 2026-11-24]** "Your app shouldn't try to set the CPU core affinity ... devices often ignore an app's processor affinity"; the owner's Benchmark had the workers on the fast cores and the callback on cores 4-6. Their nice stays. Hybrid desktops keep the mask.
- **Load never stops playback (Claude, on the owner's rule):** overload shedding never sheds the last layer still sounding, and sheds only while the meter itself is over 0.75 (a past stretch no longer takes a layer); a device the system stopped under the playing transport (miniaudio gives up on a stream whose reroute after an AAudio error failed: **[Fact, AOSP AudioStreamInternalPlay.cpp, read 2026-11-24]** a callback write that times out ends the callback loop with an error) is opened again within a second, also when the app had marked it closed. Kept: the shedding's 0.3 s response (a 1 s one changed which layers a test project sheds and when, and with it the restored drums' exact match).
- **Sustained Performance Mode is not asked for (Claude, after the audio review of 1.1.2):** it is an app-requested cap that trades peak clocks for steady ones, against the owner's rule that the system throttles, not the app. The Benchmark's device block still reports whether the phone has it (`isSustainedPerformanceModeSupported()`) and the API level. **[Assumption]** false on the S22+.
- **The every_instrument reference leaves out Timber's bowed strings (owner, 2026-11-24: "It doesn't necessarily need to sound identical every time just close enough. If it's repeatable and doesn't change the sound so much that it's distracting it's okay. You're also welcome to narrow the test"):** **[Fact, measured 2026-11-24 under qemu and with Clang]** the bowed string's stick-slip friction (Classic and Simulated) moves by up to 1.2 dB between compilers (GCC Arm64, Clang x86-64), which no floating-point flag fixed without changing other pinned bits. The Benchmark still plays both; the stress scenario's cross-build mix match (`stress_test.cpp`, `withoutBowedStrings`) renders without them, and Timber's own references keep checking their sound on every build.
- **The Performance sheet's look is approved (owner, 2026-11-24: "The performance menu it's fine"):** the CPU readout opens it, and a small dot beside the readout marks something new ([app](app.md)).
- **Heat is a plain reading (owner, 2026-11-24: "Just because something is hot doesn't mean it will stutter"):** the Performance sheet shows the heat level with no warning or cool-down tip, heat never marks its dot, and heat no longer stops a Benchmark (battery saver still does).
- **The fast-path retry is for Low latency only (Claude, after the audio review of 1.1.2):** Auto's 1024 frames never get the fast path, so retrying them would cut a playing stream on every open; a retry that fails opens as before rather than leave the device closed. The grow-only tuner counts from the opened size rounded up to whole bursts, so its first growth is a full burst.
- **The smart-freeze hint (owner):** never on the CPU figure (it came at about 40 % with no dropout, on an overload shed: 1.1.1 counted any shed at once); now 3 dropouts (the platform's underruns) within 10 s at two moments or more, or a track shed for 4 s.
- **The Benchmark plays every built-in (owner):** `every_instrument` and `every_effect` are generated from the engine's list (each instrument in each model, Timber Classic and Simulated, Rook's three kit kinds; every effect in each variant, keyed where it takes a key; retired ids left out); a test fails when a registered built-in is missing. Their hashes and references change (intended). Scenario names use the shown names (Raven, Rook, Magpie; the ids stay); each cost carries a `name`. A ramp the engine's component cap ends says so in the engine's words.
- **Rejected or left:** opening the live stream at the device's native rate (the owner's phone ran 48 kHz everywhere, so the rate was not the cause; resampling 44.1 kHz projects live is the owner's call); profiling the live parts (a profile runs the mix on one thread); Game Mode and Samsung's GameSDK (games only, deprecated); smoothing the CPU figure (a UI question: with no spin the clock no longer swings with it).

## 2026-11-20: Timber 1.1.2: the other models simulated, appended behind `sim`; the patent and literature checks (Claude, within the owner's 1.1.2 answers)

**Owner (2026-11-20):** every Timber model ships in 1.1.2; the mechanical noises subtle by default, with a Noise knob to raise them; check that each patent actually applies. **Built** ([engine](engine.md#timber-removed-in-113), "1.1.2 Simulated").
- **Append-only (Claude):** each model but the piano gets `sim` (Classic, Simulated; Classic plays as 1.1.0) and `noises` (0..1, 0.3: a listening position; 1 close-miked) after its own knobs, before `variant`; the organ gets `sim` only, its `click` being its contacts' hardness (its own-knob count stays at 16). The 36 non-piano 1.1.0 presets are hashed against their 1.1.0 renders (`timber_compat_test.cpp`, recorded from Main 2a9934c on the x86-64 optimised build). **[Fact]** the engine builds with `-fassociative-math`, so a Simulated path written inline beside a 1.1.0 loop changed that loop's bits (the mallets' did); each Simulated path is kept out of line (`CAW_TIMBER_NOINLINE`), which brought them back. **[Open]** the Arm64, Windows and Debug tables are not recorded (no such build here): on those builds the test prints its table instead of checking; record them on CI from 2a9934c as Rook's were.
- **New presets start Simulated; a fresh Timber does not (Claude):** `sim`'s default stays Classic so a 1.1.0 project keeps its sound; the 37 new presets (six or more per model, most named "Real ..." for the job) set it. [Open question for the owner] whether a newly added Timber should open on a Simulated preset for these models, as the piano does.
- **What was read before building (2026-11-20), each the design's [Assumption] otherwise:** Serafin, Avanzini and Rocchesso, SMAC 2003 (full text, air.unimi.it): the elasto-plastic bristle law z' = v (1 - alpha(v, z) z / zss(v)), alpha's elastic-mixed-plastic map with breakaway zba, zss from the Stribeck curve with fs = mu_s Fn, fc = mu_d Fn (mu_s 0.4-0.5, mu_d 0.2 for rosin), its Newton solution of the delay-free loop and its note that the curves' shapes may deviate "without affecting the model behavior significantly", and its citation of Dupont et al. as the elasto-plastic source; Verge, Hirschberg and Causse, JASA 101(5), 2925-2939, 1997 (abstract and record): a jet-drive pressure jump at the mouth modified from Fletcher's 1976 model, turbulence as a further pressure jump scaling with the jet velocity squared, the transient set by the drive's rise; Hirschberg, Gilbert, Msallam and Wijnands, JASA 99, 1754-1758, 1996 (abstract and HAL record): shock waves at fortissimo, brightness from nonlinear propagation, which matters for the radiated sound and can be neglected for the oscillation; Adachi and Sato, JASA 99, 1200-1209, 1996 (ATR report and records): lips that swing and stretch; Hunt and Crossley, J. Appl. Mech. 42(2), 440-445, 1975 (records): F = K x^n + lambda x^n x'. Not re-read (still [Assumption] where used): Fletcher 1976, McIntyre, Schumacher and Woodhouse 1983, Smith 1986, Scavone 1997, Schelleng 1973, Evangelista and Eckerholm 2010, Chafe 1990.
- **Where the build departs from the design (Claude):** the jet's travel delay goes as 1 / sqrt(p) around the 1.1.0 jet length, so its tuning holds; the sax's cone is the reed at a truncated cone's closed end, a first-order allpass that is -1 low and +1 high, not two waveguides (the two-line version overblew to the octave); brassiness acts on the radiated wave, not inside the loop (Hirschberg et al.); the Stribeck curve is a Lorentzian (no exponential, no divisions on the sample's critical path) and Newton takes 1 step at Normal, 2 at High, from the elastic solution that is exact while sticking (the paper's 7 iterations were 4x over budget); a released bow lifts and the string is damped (the bristles against a resting bow handed energy back: the original model is not passive, as Matusiak, Chatziioannou and Van Walstijn 2025 note); the plucked string's fret buzz is a bounce off a gap in the loop at Normal and High (the design had it at High only); the High tiers are the design's dynamic reed and more Newton steps, not the tone-hole lattice or two bow points.
- **Patents (our reading, not legal advice; Google Patents, read 2026-11-20).** One search per model's main technique (flute jet and breath noise; reed tonguing; brass lips and brassiness; bowed friction; string slide noise and fret collision; mallet and hammer contact; tine hammer and pickup; organ key click and contacts):
  - **Chafe (Stanford) US5508473A** (period-synchronous noise in wind instruments, a jet delay): **expired** (fee-related lapse 2008; anticipated 2014-05-10). It does not restrict the flute's pulsed breath.
  - **Casio US10347222B2** (Active, to about 2037-09-29): claim 1 needs a blowing pressure taken from a blowing pressure sensor, run through a weight-on-a-spring model to a second blowing pressure that makes the sound. Timber has no blowing pressure sensor and runs no breath through a mass-spring: its mass-springs are the lips and (High) the reed, valves on the flow. Kept outside it by not smoothing the breath through a mass-spring.
  - **Casio US10297239B2** (Active, to about 2038-05-23): touch sensors on a wind instrument judging tonguing. Not applicable: Timber's tongue is a modelled contact started by the note, with no sensor.
  - **Yamaha US7872190B2 / EP2079080B1** (lip and reed coupled motion equations): **expired** (fee-related, US) and not in force (EP). Timber's tonguing is a tongue-reed contact, not a lip-reed equilibrium.
  - **Yamaha US5459280** (a tongue simulator in a reed model, 1990s), **US5408042** (breath noise convolved, 1995), **US5272275** (brass waveguide loop) and **Staccato US6284965B1** (filtered delay loop and nonlinear junction): expired by their 20-year terms (US6284965B1 "Expired - Lifetime", 2019-05-19). [Assumption] US5459280's status was not opened; its 1990s filing puts it past 20 years.
  - **No patent found** on elasto-plastic bow friction, tonewheel key-click contact timing (US10199024B1, Active, is modal tonewheel effects: drawbar routing, pickup distortion, crosstalk; no key click), guitar slide handling noise (US6011213A, plucked-string synthesis with hand squeak: [Assumption] expired, 1990s filing, status not opened), fret collision in a waveguide, or Hunt-Crossley mallet and tine hammer contacts.
  - **[Assumption]** these were single searches; other active patents may exist. The piano's checks are the piano's own entry.
- **Cost (owner, 2026-11-24: optimise rather than raise ceilings):** the Simulated models' voices run four at a time (`GroupedModel` in `instrument_core.h`; each lane computes what its voice would alone), so the electric piano, bowed, sax and brass cases, which read 8,100-11,000 µs per second of audio against the 9,600 ceiling, now read 6,000-8,300 on this box (idle, min of three runs, [engine](engine.md#timber-removed-in-113)), and their separate 11,200 ceiling (`kSim`) is gone: every polyphonic case takes 9,600. Normal keeps one Newton step for the bow (High two); no approximation was needed.
- **[Fact]** No new dependency; original code.

## 2026-11-20: Timber's Simulated piano (1.1.2): what is simulated, what it is measured against, and the patent reading (Claude, building the owner's approved design)

**Owner (2026-11-20):** build the piano as designed; every Timber model ships in 1.1.2; check that each patent's claims actually apply and keep the design outside them; the mechanical noises natural (subtle) by default with a Noise knob; the action delay off by default; new pianos open on Simulated at Normal on the phone.
- **Decision (Claude): Simulated is a second mode beside Classic,** its knobs appended (`sim`, `tier`, `noise`, `touch`, `action`, `glide`, `sympathy`, `phantom`); `sim` defaults to Classic, so every 1.1.0 preset and project renders bit for bit as before (hashed per build: `timber_piano_compat_test.cpp`). New pianos open on Simulated because the app starts a new Timber with `sim` on (`LayerDoc.newInstrumentValues`); `tier` "Setting" follows Settings › Sound › Quality, which is Normal on the phone by default.
- **Decision (Claude): Simulated's per-sample code is compiled with strict floating point** (`starling_piano_sim.cpp`), as the clip stretcher and Flagship are: under the engine's reassociating flags its contacts and banks gave a different last bit at different block sizes. Classic keeps the fast flags and its bits.
- **Decision (Claude): only silently held keys listen to the bridge** (held-key sympathy): struck strings drive it, unstruck held ones take a share of it. Coupling every voice to every other through the 16-frame-late bridge sum was not passive and grew without bound with 16 keys held and the pedal down (tested), so no string both drives and listens.
- **Departures from the design (Claude):** the pedal-down register bank is 24 fixed modes on the stretched fundamentals of C2..B3, not Zambon, Lehtonen and Bank's fitted filter (no measured register response was available); High has no live hammer (every tier plays the table hammer) and no duplex strings; the soundboard has no FIR part; a single bass string runs two slightly detuned lanes (its two polarisations), which give its two-stage decay. A silently held key still ends after about 0.3 s if nothing has made it sound (the frame's rule for silent held voices).
- **[Fact, measured 2026-11-20]** The test targets are numbers measured from the Salamander Grand (a Yamaha C5, by Alexander Holm, CC-BY 3.0; archive.org item SalamanderGrandPianoV3, the Ogg set): inharmonicity from partials 2–15 at A0..A3 (the bass asymptote of the B curve), broadband T60 per octave (0.1–1 s and 2–6 s after the peak, the median of C, D#, F# and A at velocity layer 8), and the attack's brightening and level from layer 2 to 16 at C2 and C4. Only the numbers are in the repository, with this credit; no audio ships (owner, 2026-11-20).
- **[Fact, DAFx-11 paper, read 2026-11-20]** The B curve's form and its treble asymptote are Rigaud, David and Daudet's ("A parametric model of piano tuning": sT = 9.26e-2, yT = −13.64).
- **[Assumption]** The hammer and string anchors of the build-time strikes (hammer masses, felt K and p, lengths, tension, strike point) are typical values interpolated through Chaigne and Askenfelt's C2, C4 and C7 as commonly cited; their part II table was not re-read. The contact constants and the noise levels are set by the tests' calibration, not measured.

**The patent check (our reading of each claim against what is built; not legal advice).** Claims read on Google Patents on 2026-11-20; their wording is paraphrased here.
- **Yamaha US10978031B2** (Active, priority 2018-10-05, expected expiry 2039-10-02). **[Fact]** Independent claims 1 (device), 9 (method) and 17 (medium) each need an instructor for a key-depression sound from key operation information *and* one for a key-release sound "according to a manner of a key release"; claim 18 needs a key-release sound "that changes depending on a speed of the key release". Dependent claims 2–8 and 10–16 define the manner by the key's speed between positions. **[Assumption] Reading:** the key-bottom thump is a depression sound, so the first element is met; the second is not. Simulated makes no release sound of its own: when a damper lands, its felt meets the string, and the buzz is that contact with the string's own motion, its level set by the string's amplitude. Nothing depends on how the key was released: a note-off's velocity is ignored throughout Timber (bit-identical renders at note-off velocities 0 and 1, tested), there is no key-up thump, and the same landing follows a pedal release. Outside claims 1, 9, 17 and 18, as we read them. The open point is how widely "manner" would be read (the timing alone of a release?): counsel's question.
- **Yamaha US12039960B2** (Active, priority 2019-09-05, expected expiry 2041-04-27). **[Fact]** Independent claims 1 (method), 9 (device) and 12 (medium) need a resonance sound produced, on a multiple damper (pedal) operation, based on an additional sound relating to that operation, with the resonance's envelope waveform produced from that additional sound, the additional sound made by waveform processing of random noise, and a simulated damper sound output with it. **[Assumption] Reading:** the pedal's noise (the trapwork's wooden contact and the dampers' seeded felt contacts) goes to the output only; the register bank and the strings are fed by the sounding strings alone, scaled by the pedal's depth; no noise shapes any envelope. The resonance input is bit-identical with and without the pedal noise (tested). The envelope element is not met: outside every independent claim, as we read them.
- **Casio US11094307B2** (Active, priority 2018-10-04, expected expiry 2039-10-02). **[Fact]** Independent claims 1 and 17 (instrument) and 18 (method) need stored excitation waveform data made in advance by joining portions of waveforms of one pitch at different intensities, and a sound source that applies a window function to a part of it chosen by pitch and volume. **[Assumption] Reading:** every excitation is computed by our own simulation (a force pulse per key zone and hammer speed), stored whole, and read from its start; two neighbouring speeds and zones are blended sample by sample, never joined in sequence; no window is applied (each pulse starts and ends at rest, tested). Outside, as we read them.
- **Modartt US7915515B2** (Active, priority 2006-07-28, expected expiry 2027-10-20). **[Fact]** Independent claims 1 (device) and 24 (medium) need a presynthesis module that produces timbre coefficients representing the damping and frequency (of the partials, claim 10) from soundboard and string parameters, feeding a real-time module. **[Assumption] Reading:** the app holds no such module. The dispersion is one allpass coefficient per key, fitted at prepare time to a B curve that is itself a fit to measured partials; the loss follows target decay curves; the soundboard's sections sit on a fixed pole grid with their weights fitted once, offline, to a synthesised board; the build-time hammer simulation uses string parameters, but its output is force pulses, not damping or frequency coefficients, and it is not part of the app. No coefficient is per partial and the output is never a sum of partials. Outside, as we read them, until it expires.
- **[Assumption]** Only these four patents were read for 1.1.2's piano. This is our reading, to be confirmed by a patent attorney; it is not legal advice.

## 2026-11-20: Rook 1.1.2: Synth, Produced and Acoustic kits; the drum patents read (Claude, building the owner's approved design)

**Owner (2026-11-20):** build Rook's three kinds of kit as designed; acoustic pieces keep ringing when re-hit; mechanical noises subtle by default; tests may use numbers from CC0/CC-BY recordings (no audio shipped); e-drum maps for General MIDI, Roland V-Drums and Alesis, checked against their published note charts; check that each patent applies before relying on a design-around. What was built: [engine reference](engine.md#kits-of-three-kinds-112).

- **Append only (Claude):** the pad gains `engine`, `acou_piece`, `acou_artic` and `acou_layer`; the Acoustic kit (`kit_kind`, `acou_*`, 16 × `pc<n>_*`) follows the pads. Every default reproduces the old sound: the pre-1.1.0 tables still pass on every build, and a new table holds the 32 presets of 1.1.0 and 1.1.1 (x86-64 Release, recorded from 2a9934c; **[Assumption]** the Arm64, Windows and Debug tables are recorded on CI from the same commit, as the old ones were).
- **A piece is one object (owner's answer, built so):** no voices: a pad names a piece and an articulation; re-hits add to its motion. A Layers pad may strike a piece as a layer (`acou_layer`).
- **Choices made within the design (Claude):**
  - The contacts are linearly implicit (the force solved with the response it causes) rather than the design's SAV scheme; the guard resets a piece on a non-finite or runaway output. The tests (extreme settings, every articulation) stay finite.
  - **[Assumption]** a stick's tip strikes with about a quarter of its mass (it pivots in the hand); with it, contact on a snare is 3.3-3.5 ms and on a 12" tom 4.5-5.0 ms (the design asked 0.5-5).
  - The rim is a fixed surface for the contact (a hoop far stiffer than a stick); its contact then follows Hertz's v^-0.2 (measured v^-0.19).
  - The cymbal's coupling drives each high mode by one product of two lower modes (the design's "about two products a mode" as a cascade over the whole plate, without the energy-conserving back-reaction, which cost 6 times as much); a soft hit stays within 1 dB of the linear plate.
  - Hi-hat: `acou_hat_pedal` 0 open .. 1 closed, as CC 4 runs on Roland's modules; Closed and Open hold the hat until the pedal moves; a plain stroke plays it where the pedal holds it.
  - Mechanical noise (`acou_noise`) defaults to 0.25: the pedal's mechanism, a hand's grab, a brush's swish, the hat's foot; the stick's own click is part of the contact.
  - The cost ceilings in caw_bench are regression ceilings (about 1.2 times what was measured, 2026-11-24). After that date's optimisation (owner: optimise rather than raise ceilings; the same model, only its rounding moved) the Acoustic engine meets the design's heavy ceiling for a Normal full kit and medium for an Eco groove, but its typical Normal groove over a bar is about 1.25 times the design's estimate and its Eco full kit about 1.3 times medium ([performance](engine.md#performance)): the rest is the frame-by-frame contacts (closed hat, snare wires) and the ride's cascade, which a cheaper model would change (a cascade at half rate moved the ride by -23 dB, about half of the cascade's own effect, so it was not taken). The kit's room (1.1.0's reverb) is priced on its own, as the design does.
- **E-drum maps (facts checked 2026-11-20):** General MIDI from the GM 1 percussion keys; Roland from Roland's TD-27/TD-07/TD-17 default note maps (search-quoted) and the TD-50 MIDI implementation (CC 4 open to closed, choke as polyphonic pressure); Alesis from the Nitro module's user guide appendix. Some Roland tom-rim, crash-2 and ride notes are **[Assumption]** (the long-standing defaults; Roland's support pages refused automated reads). A pad's pressure of half or more chokes its cymbal, as an e-drum's choke sends.
- **Patents (our reading, not legal advice; Google Patents and web searches, 2026-11-20):**
  - Searched: drum modal synthesis with snare-wire collision, cymbal synthesis with nonlinear mode coupling, hi-hat pedal sound generation. No patent was found claiming a modal membrane with colliding snare wires, or cymbal modes coupled nonlinearly.
  - **[Fact]** **Korg US10482855B2** (Active, priority 2017-11-22, to about 2038-11-20) and **Korg US10475430B2** (Active, priority 2017-12-07, to about 2038-12-04) claim a hi-hat sound apparatus with a **recorder storing recorded sound data** (hit sounds for each of a set number of pedal states, foot close and foot open sounds) whose playback volumes follow the state and the change of the pads' distance. Our hat stores and plays no recorded or pre-rendered sounds and has no states: two plates are simulated and their gap moves continuously, so the claims' recorder and per-state sounds are absent. Kept outside them: Rook never builds a hat from per-state stored samples crossfaded by the pedal.
  - **[Fact]** US5748513 (Stanford, coupled-mode digital filter for inharmonic tones, gongs and cymbals; priority 1996) is **[Assumption]** expired by its 20-year term (status not opened). EP3012832 (Potsdam, modelling a recorded sound by a fitted differential equation) is shown not in force, and Rook fits nothing to recordings. US7772481B2 (convolving recorded drum sounds with a stick sensor) does not apply: nothing is convolved with recordings.
  - **[Assumption]** only these searches were made; counsel should confirm before a commercial release.
- **The app (Claude, within CLAUDE.md's rule to ask before UI):** Rook's page gains only a kit-kind and style picker and the builders' actions in its existing kit sheet, from existing components; anything more is for the owner ([app](app.md)).

## 2026-11-20: Device motion at 60 frames a second; the stabilizer follows the clock's need and the heat; a real-world Benchmark (Claude, on the owner's 1.1.1 report)

**Owner (2026-11-20):** fix the new playback spikes and UI slowdowns; the app and the Benchmark should run without thermal trouble; the Benchmark measures real-world performance: no cool-down pauses, the measured numbers the headline, the top-clock figure off the front ([performance](performance.md#playback-spikes-and-ui-load-111-2026-11-20)).
- **Device motion is held to 60 frames a second (Claude):** the beta plan's B3 drew it on every frame the screen ran (up to 240 Hz); each motion frame repaints the whole visual and keeps the raster and GPU threads busy on the cores the audio shares. The playhead, scrolling, drags and the Frame rate setting are unchanged. Meters read 30 times a second without asking for the frames between, the crow loader draws 30 and Parrot 20. **[Assumption]** 60 is smooth enough for these pictures; the owner may want it to follow the Frame rate setting instead.
- **The load stabilizer spins only as far as the clock needs (Claude; superseded 2026-11-24: retired, no heat action):** to its 0.8 share while the recent peak callback takes half its period or more, proportionally less below (never under 0.48), with the target raised to 0.6 and 0.7 at thermal status warm and hot and no spin at critical; the app passes the thermal state (`caw_audio_set_thermal`, API 45). Rejected: switching it off at warm (the owner's phone sits there, and it is what took the live rows to 0 xruns) and a lower fixed share (by schedutil's 1.25 b f it lets the clock fall on heavy projects too).
- **The Benchmark:** the cool-down pauses are removed and every number on screen and in the shared text is the measured one; `clock_share` and the `*_at_top_clock` figures stay in the JSON as context (the format only gains fields).

## 2026-11-19: The bird theme and personality in the app (Claude, building the owner's sound plan)

**Owner (2026-11-19):** "a little bit of that theme", never distracting; personality that stays professional; the built-in themes renamed Raven, Jackdaw, Magpie and Jay; Parrot as a joke theme ([App](app.md)).
- **Five families became four (Claude):** Neon's palette is Raven, Flat's is Jackdaw; Magpie (high contrast) and Jay (blue accent, light first) are new palettes. Dusk, Dawn and Amber are gone: a saved Dusk opens as Jackdaw, Dawn as Jay and Amber as Magpie (`migrateThemeName`), and settings.json writes `theme_families: 2` so a later bird name is never migrated twice. [Assumption] the owner wanted four families, not the old five renamed.
- **Icons as a font (Claude):** the family hints are glyphs of a small icon font made by `scripts/icon-font.js` (plain Node, no package), so device headers and the browser show them through their existing `IconData` without changes there. **[Fact]** No new dependency.
- **Sound:** the one sound the surprises make is synthesised in the app and played through the sample preview voice; nothing is downloaded or recorded.
- **Left out:** the logo designs (each needs the owner's yes) and the restored-after-crash line (there is no such screen).

## 2026-11-19: Flock's mips read at most at rate 1; its sample is read in place (Claude, within the sound plan)

**Context:** the sound design (grain engine, Flock) asks for half-band mip copies for reads more than an octave up (+50 % RAM) and a sample read from the sample store, read-only and shared.
- **Decision (Claude):** a grain over rate 2 reads the copy that brings its rate to at most 1 (not 2), so no content folds over; reaching 48 st takes four copies, about 94 % more RAM than the sample, and a sample over 120 s gets none. The sample is read in place from the asset store (no copy), its first frame kept for the cubic read. The filter defaults to Off (presets that want it turn it on), which keeps the design point inside the medium budget.
- **[Fact]** No new dependency.

## 2026-11-19: Drums 1.1.0 (Rook): settings that say when they are heard, appended layers, groove and the one-screen editor (Claude, building the owner's plan)

**Owner (2026-11-19):** the drums were "finicky, some settings don't work… one big list"; plug and play with the advanced controls kept, and Raven's one-screen layout. **Built** ([engine](engine.md#drums-orgcawdrums), [app](app.md)):
- **The audit found one setting that did nothing:** Noise mode Snappy without the Body (fixed: a shell of its own). Every other setting changed the sound, but many only in a context (a filter's resonance on LP 6 dB, Clap's bursts outside Clap, glide without Mono and tracking, Room send with the kit's room off). Choice: the engine publishes those contexts (`needs`) and the editor dims each such setting with what it needs, instead of hiding it; a test checks every rule both ways. Out 2..9 stay a known limit (one stereo output), said beside the choice.
- **Append-only:** 51 pad and 6 kit-wide parameters after the old ones; every old preset renders bit-identically (hashes recorded from 20bcb8b).
- **Groove only delays:** a hit can't play before its note arrives, so swing, the groove tables, humanize and flams move hits late (no look-ahead latency added). [Open question for the owner] a small look-ahead would let a groove push hits early too.
- **No new dependencies.** The FM, waveguide and texture layers, the ring modulator and the echo are original code.
- **Dawn chorus kit: left for later.** It needs real bird and nature recordings under CC0 or CC-BY with their licences checked; none were verified and fetched in this session, so no kit ships with them yet.

## 2026-11-19: Timber's piano excitation is synthesised; Timber is one merged instrument (Claude, within the owner's Timber decisions)

**Context:** the owner settled (2026-11-19) the commuted-waveguide piano designed around Modartt's US7915515B2 claims, and allowed a soundboard excitation derived from a CC-BY recording to ship built in, with credit.
- **Decision (Claude):** this build ships **no recorded material**. The 24 soundboard excitations (Grand, Upright, Felt × 8 zones) are designed by Timber itself at registration (`engine/src/timber_tables.cpp`: decaying band noise, longer in the low bands, and six low body resonances), so no credit or NOTICE line is needed. Extracting a residual from the Salamander Grand (CC-BY 3.0) was not attempted in this session: it needs the samples fetched and an inverse-filtering pass whose ringing partials are the design's named risk. It stays the open option the owner allowed; if it is taken, its source, licence and credit go here and in NOTICE.
- **Decision (Claude):** `org.caw.timber` is one merged device (`fx_merged.h`) whose `variant`, shown as "Model", picks one of seven classes, so only the chosen model's voices and memory exist (the design's "fx_merged's variant pattern").
- **Departures from the design (Claude):** the bowed body is five resonators after the voices' sum, not a 2,048-tap convolution (no latency, a fraction of the cost); the sax is the clarinet's reed with its even harmonics folded in (an assumption, by ear); the brass model speaks from about C3 up; the organ's leakage is not modelled; phantom partials and sostenuto are not built.
- **[Fact]** No new dependency: everything is the engine's own code and its Library (`lib::Reverb`, `org.caw.rotary`'s class).

## 2026-11-19: The brand is protected; the code stays Apache 2.0 (owner)

**Decision (owner):** the code, engine and SDK stay open under Apache 2.0. The brand belongs to Kmandevr, doing business as AutoStacc, and is not licensed with the code ([brand and trademark policy](../TRADEMARKS.md)).
- **The brand covers:** the names, logos and logo designs, the bird product names, and the mascot and theme art. The code's internal ids stay plain and free to use for compatibility.
- **Forks:** forking is welcome. A non-commercial fork may keep the brand if it says clearly that it is unofficial. Selling or monetising anything under the brand needs AutoStacc's permission, so a commercial fork must rebrand.
- **No unbranded build:** C.A.W won't provide one, so a rebranded clone isn't made easy (owner).
- **Hosted content:** what AutoStacc hosts (packs, presets, kits) is AutoStacc's commercial content, outside this repository and outside Apache.

**[Fact]** Apache-2.0 §6 grants no trademark rights, and released code and content keep their Apache licence. **Owner's own check (2026-11-19):**
- the owner found no business in the field trading as "CAW";
- C.A.W is the acronym of the full name "Code Auditioning Workspace", for which the owner found no trademark;
- the owner considers Raven unclaimed in this field.

**[Assumption]** The policy states AutoStacc's claim; registering the marks is what makes them enforceable. That is the owner's call.

## 2026-11-19: The audio callback keeps its core busy where Android gives no performance hint (Claude, owner-approved performance work)

**Superseded 2026-11-24:** retired, off unless `CAW_LOAD_STABILIZER=1` (above). **Decision (as it was):** on Android, while something plays, the app is in front, the buffer mode is not Battery and no ADPF hint session is open, the device callback spins after its work until 0.8 of its period from its ideal start (`engine/src/load_stabilizer.h`, `stabilize` in `engine/platform/audio_miniaudio.cpp`). The S22+'s Benchmark (2026-11-19) had the hint `unsupported` and the callback's big core at 634 MHz of 2.5 GHz, its live p99 79–139 %. The arithmetic is Oboe's `StabilizedCallback` (Apache-2.0, google/oboe `src/common/StabilizedCallback.cpp`, read 2026-11-19), **reimplemented**, not copied and not a dependency: Oboe is not linked (miniaudio opens the stream), and a wrapper class of 60 lines is not worth vendoring a library for. The share is Oboe's 0.8, not the 50–70 % first suggested: under schedutil's documented `f = 1.25 u f_max` (docs.kernel.org, read 2026-11-19) a lower share lets the clock fall back ([performance](performance.md#live-callback-clock-and-the-benchmark)). No setting: Battery is the off switch, and the cost (one big core about 80 % busy while playing) is described in [performance](performance.md). Measured only on the phone; on Linux the rules are unit-tested and nothing else changes.

## 2026-11-18: Merged effects open old projects; files that use them are version 7 (Claude, owner's FX plan)

**Decision:**
- **Old ids are retired, not removed:** the 30 old effect ids no longer list or create, but every project, preset, rack, lane, link and MIDI mapping that names one opens as its merged device's variant ([engine](engine.md), [app](app.md)).
- **File version 7** is written only when a project names a merged-only id, any `variant`, or any rack (racks always save `rack_layout`); otherwise v6 or v5 exactly as before. Builds before 1.0.8 refuse v7, so most projects saved by 1.0.8 don't open in 1.0.7. Accepted: a newer file must not play wrong in an older build.
- **Changing a variant** keeps only knobs the new variant declares identically; the rest reset to defaults, and their automation lanes, modulation links and MIDI mappings are dropped with them, because their meaning changed. One undo step.
- **Until the device UI kit,** a plain "Variant" pill opens a menu of variants on today's device pages (an assumption the approved device UI plan replaces).

## 2026-11-18: The Hub goes into use with 1.2.0; APKs are installed by hand until then (owner)

**Decision (owner):** 1.0.7 lays the Hub's groundwork only: C.A.W's side of the link, the Hub's first build and its update channels and notifications, all inert while no Hub certificate is pinned. Using the Hub (signing it, pinning each app's certificate, installing updates through it) comes with 1.2.0 (renumbered 2026-11-19; it was 1.1.0). Until then the owner installs APKs by hand.

## 2026-11-18: Declared capabilities: strict default, embedded in the binary, older components warned until rebuilt (owner, architect)

**Decision:**
- **Strict default (owner):** with nothing declared, a C.A.W component may use only the host API, maths, memory and the C++ runtime. Anything more, background threads and reading or writing files included, is declared with a plain reason, or the component is blocked; there is no Trusted override for undeclared use. CLAP and VST3 binaries can't declare, so their findings only warn.
- **Where (architect):** in the component's `caw-component.json` (`capabilities`: id, reason, and for `internet` its sites), embedded by the build in an allocated data section of the binary (`caw_caps` on ELF, `.cawcap` on PE) that the scan reads without loading it. So the declaration is covered by the binary's SHA-256, which the trust store and the Hub's lists key on, and can't be swapped on its own. The component ABI and descriptor are unchanged. Ids are the scan's list (`internet`, not `network`).
- **Older components (owner):** a component trusted before 1.0.7, with no declaration, keeps loading with its findings shown as warnings until it is rebuilt or updated; then an undeclared use blocks it.
- **The scan's rules (Claude, 2026-11-18; [engine](engine.md#pre-load-scan)):** blocking is on for C.A.W components.
