What the C++ runtime itself pulls in is no finding (`__cxa_*`, `operator new`, pthread mutexes, once, keys and condition variables, stdio and locale calls, `std::filesystem::path`'s text members); `open`, `open64`, `openat`, `__open_2` and `syscall`, which static libstdc++ imports for `std::random_device` and futexes, are hints that never block (a determined author could hide behind them; the hub's review and reputation lists are the backstop). `fopen`, file streams, `std::filesystem` operations, `pthread_create` and `std::thread` are certain. Registry imports count as input spying, `ptrace` and cross-process calls as starting programs. Mach-O, ELF32 and PE32 are refused.
- **Declaring (owner):** a "Needs" form in the component's settings in the C++ Builder: a capability from a picker, its reason on one line and, for Internet, its sites; it writes the manifest.

## 2026-11-18: The Hub switches update channels and shows download notifications; scan findings go in the trust sheet (owner)

**Decision (owner):**
- **Update channels:** the Hub installs C.A.W updates from the channel the user chose in C.A.W (Stable, Beta, Every change), not only Stable. C.A.W tells the Hub the channel in its install request; an older C.A.W that sends none gets Stable. Downgrades stay refused.
- **Notifications:** the Hub runs long downloads as a foreground service with a progress notification, so Android doesn't stop them.
- **Scan findings:** what the pre-load scan finds in a plug-in is listed in the existing Isolated / Trusted / Blocked sheet, one row per finding with a plain sentence and a "Why might it need this?" fold; a blocked C.A.W component says why and offers no Trusted.

## 2026-11-18: General mixer routing in the engine and the model, silent in 1.0.7; renders need only be repeatable (owner)

**Decision (owner):** the mixer's routes become general (a track's output to any bus or straight to the master, sends to any bus or track, feedback sends, sidechain keys from any track), built in the engine and the app's model and shipped silently in 1.0.7: no screens until the 1.1.0 visual router's look is designed ("Engine only for now"). Claude's choices within it, following the architect's review:
- **Extend the mixer, not a second graph:** `Engine::MixGraph` already runs on `GraphExecutor`; its pulled route tables (with their fixed summing order) become general routes. Stereo only for now: multichannel buses and multi-out need the component format extended.
- **A send into a track** is summed into its input before its effects, and the track keeps its instrument. A return is a bus or track fed by sends; no new kind of track.
- **Feedback** only on a send marked `feedback`: it reads the previous 64-frame chunk, with the limiter on unless switched off (the owner's settled choices).
- **Format:** new fields are optional and written only when used; an app project that uses them is saved as file version 6, otherwise 5, so older builds refuse what they can't play instead of dropping routes.
- **Renders (owner):** existing renders need not stay bit-identical, as long as every render is repeatable (the same bits on every run, at any buffer size, with or without the mix pool) and sounds the same; a changed reference is re-recorded with the reason.

## 2026-11-18: The Hub's package is com.autostacc.hub; its pinned certificate (owner)

**Decision (owner):** the companion's package is `com.autostacc.hub` (not the assumed `com.kmandevr.caw.companion`). C.A.W accepts a companion only when its signing certificate's SHA-256 digest matches a pinned value from the build (`HUB_CERT_SHA256`, a repository variable, since a digest is public); while it is empty, no companion is accepted. The Hub's first build is the service C.A.W calls and the site window (autostacc.com).

## 2026-11-18: Plans and the vision move to the private AutoStacc-Hub now; a curated public roadmap; the code stays public (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-18):** C.A.W's vision, roadmap, feature plans, proposals for unbuilt work, owner questions and decisions about unbuilt features move now, not at 1.1.0, into the private repository `Kmandevr/AutoStacc-Hub` (docs/caw/), "the private repository that holds everything we don't want people to know, like feature plans".
- **Public (this repository):** what is shipped and how the code works, the contracts the shipped code implements (the companion protocol, the C.A.W-Telemetry contract), security rules, licences and process, with short pointers such as "planned work is tracked privately".
- **[Fact]** Git history still shows the old plans; only future changes are hidden. Hidden features stay readable in the public code (Apache-2.0, neutral commit messages): the surprise rests on the plans and the announcements, not on secret code.
- **The public roadmap** ([roadmap](roadmap.md)) lists only what the owner chooses to reveal: for now the promised work: hosting VST3 and CLAP plug-ins, iOS and macOS releases, Google Play, and the AutoStacc store and cloud library.
- **Issues** (owner, the same day): this repository's Issues are for bugs that are fine to show the public. Features, plans and other tasks are tracked in the private repository's Issues; the 26 open feature and plan issues were copied there and closed here with a note (closing doesn't hide their text or edit history). The task template is gone; `bug.yml` stays.
- **Replaces** the 2026-11-17 decision that plans would go private at 1.1.0.

## 2026-11-18: The companion's groundwork in the main app: a services package, one link, two flavours (#239, for 1.0.7)

**Decision (Claude, following the architect's design and the owner's groundwork rule):** what needs no owner input is built, with no change for anyone without a companion.
- **`caw_services`:** the platform-free update, relay, schema and SHA-256 code moved, unchanged, into a pure-Dart package in the app (`app/packages/caw_services/`, Apache-2.0), so the companion can share it. Small updates read the install through a narrow `InstalledBuild` interface instead of the Flutter-bound `UpdateInstaller`. Not a new third-party dependency: its only dependency is `path` 1.9.1, already recorded.
- **One link, chosen at startup:** `CompanionLink` with an Android implementation and none, and adapters at the existing interfaces (`UpdateSource`, `UpdateInstaller`, `RelayClient`, a new `TrustSource`). Absent or older companion: the in-app path. A timeout on a read falls back too; a timeout on a send never repeats it in-app, since the companion may have sent it. The relay adapter sits at `RelayClient`, not `RelayHttp`, because `RelayHttp` takes any URL.
- **Protocol** ([companion protocol](companion-protocol.md)): a bound Messenger service, ten kinds, version 1, 64 KiB JSON bodies, no URL or method from the caller, and the caller check by `Message.sendingUid` on every message both ways, before the message is read. The pinned certificate is read from the app's own signature at run time, so no digest sits in the source. The known-bad list isn't enforced: the trust source isn't consulted by the loader yet, until the list has a source.
- **No custom permission in the main app (security review, the same day):** a hostile app declaring `com.kmandevr.caw.permission.COMPANION` first would block C.A.W's own install or update (Android lets only the first declarer's key declare a name), so the main app declares and asks for none, and the per-message uid and signer check is the boundary. Whether the companion declares one is open (plan (private), question 10). The play flavour's `caw/updates` answers `notImplemented`, and `update-delta.js` checks `--prefix`.
- **Flavours:** `github` (today's APK, still what CI publishes as `caw-android.apk`) and `play`, with the same `applicationId`, minus both install permissions and the self-updater. CI checks that the play APK carries neither permission.
- **Why:** the owner's 1.0.7 direction (least privilege, the companion holding updates, the network and trust) needs these seams first. Building them now, behind today's behaviour, leaves the companion itself, its look and the permission drop to the owner's choices.
- **Not checked here:** no Android SDK in the session, so the Kotlin, the Gradle flavours and the merged manifests are first built by CI.

## 2026-11-18: Settings in five sections of grouped cards, one place per setting, and eight new settings (owner, #244)

**Decision:** Settings has five sections ordered by use, Sound, Editing, Appearance and feel, Projects and export, App; each page is a column of rounded glass cards, one per group of related settings, with a small header and a More fold for the rarer ones (search opens it). Sound's cards are Audio devices (output, input, channels, rate together), Quality and buffer, Latency and Bluetooth, Recording and metronome, MIDI and controllers. The registry (`settings_registry.dart`) gives every setting one id, one card and its fold flag; duplicates are gone: the two "Recording offset" rows (the audio round trip and the MIDI note offset, two settings) sit together under Latency and Bluetooth as Audio take offset and MIDI note offset, the Privacy Policy and Terms live on the Privacy page only and Send feedback beside the Privacy row only. Engine diagnostics stays in App › About (#139's open placement, settled). New: pause when unplugged, a safety limiter, a mono check, keep the screen awake, new-track defaults, record mode and new audio tracks' monitoring, remember zoom per project, a left-handed layout, and the buffer tuner's switch.
**Why:** twelve sections with split recording settings and duplicate rows read as several screens; the owner wants one cohesive screen that groups what belongs together.
**Choices made here, for the owner to confirm:** the safety limiter is live-only (`caw_engine_set_output_safety`, C API 35; a stage after the mix in the device callback, never in a render), zero-latency, transparent under -4 dBFS and held under -1 dBFS, on by default, after the master's own -0.2 dBFS limiter that every render keeps; so live and export differ only when it acts or the mono check is on, which the owner chose over the rule that they sound the same. There is no "limit the export" choice yet. The mono check is never saved. Keep the screen awake defaults to While playing. The record mode is the existing app-wide `midi_record_mode`, not a per-track default (tracks have none); the new-track instrument applies to the MIDI track Record makes (an Empty track stays empty). Remembered views live in settings.json by project id, not in the project file, so they are no edit and no undo step. Left-handed mirrors the transports and the rail only. Card and fold placement of each setting is in the registry.

## 2026-11-18: A set latency keeps its total until a like figure moves (#241, the owner's tap-along report)

**Decision:** every latency the user sets (typed, slid, tapped, pinged, accepted from takes; output and input) is saved as what it adds on top of the system's figure then, with that figure's kind ("timestamp" once 3 reads agree, "settling" before, "buffers", "none"). Read over a figure of another kind it keeps its total; it rides only on a figure of the same kind, which is a real change (a new buffer size or codec), and the re-check card asks only when a settled figure set over a settled one moved.
**Why:** a saved offset that rides on a better figure counts the system's part twice (the slider in 1.0.5-alpha.2, tap-along after it); keeping every total fixed instead would ignore a real buffer change, on Windows the only figure there is.
**[Assumption]:** 3 agreeing reads (6 to 9 s of playing) are enough for a Bluetooth link's timestamps to settle; a phone run can check it with the status's figures.

## 2026-11-18: Real device choice on Android: by AudioManager's list, kept by type and name, opened by today's id (owner's request, #242)

**Decision:** on Android the Audio page's output and input drop-downs offer the devices `AudioManager.getDevices` lists, named by type plus the product name for display. The settings keep `android:<type>:<product name>[#n]`, because an `AudioDeviceInfo` id is given per connection ([Assumption] from the API's description of ids as identifying a device while it is connected; not checked on a primary page). The app sends the engine `aaudio:<id>` with the id the device has now, and AAudio's `setDeviceId` opens it (miniaudio 0.11.25 passes `ma_device_id.aaudio`). A chosen device that is not connected plays or records on the default, the page says so, and the app opens it again when it reconnects. A stream that runs on another device than the one chosen, because Android routed it elsewhere or miniaudio reopened the default after a disconnect, shows in the status as a fallback.
**Why:** the owner wants a DAW's device choice: the speaker while Bluetooth is connected, a chosen microphone. miniaudio lists only the default on AAudio, and the platform's list was already read for latency.
**Open, for a phone run:** whether Android honours a chosen speaker while Bluetooth is connected. The status's `routed_device_id` and fallback will show it either way.
## 2026-11-17: Each platform's low-latency path; ASIO and rtkit stay out (owner's request, #237)

**Decision:** Low latency on Windows asks the endpoint's smallest period through `IAudioClient3` (shared) or the device's minimum (exclusive), never under 128 frames, falling back to miniaudio's 10 ms when that does not open; Auto, Battery and a chosen size are unchanged, so the default path is as before (Claude's choice: Low latency is the user's explicit request for the least delay). Exclusive mode stays off by default on Windows and Android (the earlier decision). `CAW_AUDIO_BACKEND` puts one miniaudio backend first for the CLI and tests (unset by default). On Android the stream already suits LE Audio (48 kHz, stereo, usage GAME); the route model reports LE Audio or A2DP and its label in the Latency page waits for the owner's look. BLE MIDI needs nothing: Android's own MIDI service asks for high connection priority. **No new dependency:** ASIO is proprietary or GPLv3, libdbus (for rtkit) AFL-2.1 or GPL-2.0+, neither clearly permissive; both are left to the owner. Facts and sources in [engine](engine.md) ("Each platform's low-latency path").

## 2026-11-17: Latency: lean early, an instant UI on Bluetooth, a slider while listening, learned compensation (owner, #236)

**Owner (2026-11-17, final):** what follows the audio (playhead, meters, clip highlights, the piano-roll cursor, the music video preview) errs slightly early rather than late, 0 to 10 ms; everything else responds at once, even on Bluetooth; an offset slider in ms with ±1 ms buttons, reachable without opening Settings, used while the song plays, saved per output device; type-in, tap-along and the loopback stay; offsets per device by route kind and a stable non-identifying key, re-checked on a device change; the ping runs several times and an unreliable result is reported, not saved; takes measured against the beat **suggest** a correction, never applied silently; when Bluetooth is the output the UI says plainly that it adds a delay no app can remove, with a help card (LE Audio, game or low-latency modes) and a button to the system's Bluetooth settings. C.A.W does not try to choose the codec (owner's brief: a privileged system API since Android 14).

**Why playback looked late (found 2026-11-17, before this change):** the output latency was already subtracted (`heardStep`); what was late was the rest. (1) Meters, clip and track highlights showed the newest meter read taken at or before *now − output latency*; a read stands for the ~33 ms rendered before it, so they were on average a whole read interval late (0 to 66 ms), plus the bar's 15 ms rise. (2) Nothing allowed for the display: a frame built for *now* lights up about two refreshes later (**[Assumption]**: 17 ms at 120 Hz, 33 ms at 60 Hz). (3) The music video preview drew the last read's playhead each frame (held up to 33 ms, about 16 ms late on average, stepping at 30 Hz), as did the piano roll under Smooth follow. (4) On-screen keys were recorded at the last read (up to 33 ms early).

**Chosen (Claude):**
- **Early bias 5 ms** (`earlyBiasUs`), on top of the display latency: it covers what stays uncertain after the compensation (the output latency is read at a random point of the callback's cycle, half a burst either way; the display latency is whole refreshes, half a 120 Hz frame is 4.2 ms), and a picture ahead of its sound is the side people forgive (**[Fact]** ITU-R BT.1359-1: detectable at about 45 ms of sound ahead of the picture but 125 ms behind; secondary sources, read 2026-11-17). The lead is 21.7 ms at 120 Hz.
- **Meters by span:** the read whose span holds the heard moment (`MeterTimeline.covering`), not the newest before it; the playhead clock evaluated at now plus the lead (its run-on limit raised from 100 to 150 ms for Lightweight's 66 ms reads); what moves only on reads drawn half an interval on.
- **Ping series:** 5 runs, at least 4 heard, all within 3 ms or 3 % of the median (**[Assumption]**); the median places takes (the engine's own measurement is only its last run).
- **Learning:** MIDI takes only (note starts within 60 ms of a quarter-note beat); a suggestion after 3 takes and 24 notes with a median shift of at least 8 ms and 70 % agreement (**[Assumption]**); it changes the output's total, because a note played on what is heard lands late by exactly the output latency's error. Audio takes would need onset analysis of the take: not built.
- **Per device:** the existing key (route kind and product name, local only; telemetry has only the kind), now with the system's figure at the time an offset was set: a move of more than 15 ms since shows a re-check card; the route is read again 2 s after a change. Offsets may go below the system's figure (to −500 ms) where it over-reports.
- **Look choices for the owner to confirm:** the sheet is the app's glass sheet with a drag handle; the total in large monospace figures, yellow past 50 ms; outlined −/+ buttons either side of the slider (filled tonal ones came out loud magenta); Play/Pause and a Click chip under it; one dim line of instructions; cards with a faint tint and a coloured outline (green: suggestion, yellow: re-check, blue: Bluetooth); *Type in, tap along or measure* at the bottom. The entry is *Latency: N ms…* in Record's long-press menu (the transport has no other hold menu) and the latency badge's tap.

**Audit (instant UI):** nothing but the playhead, meters and highlights, the count-in countdown and the place a take lands reads the output latency; buttons, selection, edits and knob drags never did. On-screen keys and pads already play on the finger's down (`Listener`).

## 2026-11-17: Comping in take lanes, Consolidate, a take per loop pass and Record that just works (owner's decisions; Claude's build, #209)

**Owner (2026-11-17, final):** a track's takes show as lanes under it, like the automation lanes (header, height, hold menu); a swipe across a take lane picks that part into the comp, which plays with short crossfades at the joins; Flatten turns the comp into ordinary clips and removes the unused takes, one undo step; MIDI and audio; the gesture rules of [UI](ui.md) (tap selects, double tap the quick action, hold the menu, hold-and-move moves); the comp plays exactly what is shown. Consolidate as #209 says ("merge selected clips on a track into one clip"), in the clip-selection hold menu next to Bounce to audio, one undo step. Frozen tracks ask "Unfreeze to edit?". Every loop pass, MIDI or audio, is a take. Record with nothing armed arms the selected track if it can take the input, else makes an audio track (the microphone) or a MIDI track (a keyboard connected), then counts in and records; a tip shown once explains the arm button; the microphone permission flow stays.

**Built ([app](app.md): take lanes and comping, Consolidate, Record):**
- **The comp is parts, not cut clips:** `[{take, from}]` on the clip, each part playing its take until the next. A MIDI comp counts in whole steps of the pattern (so it loops with the clip) and is played as a pattern of its own built from the parts (notes that start in a part, cut at its end), so the engine, the piano roll, export, bounce and freeze need nothing new. An audio comp counts in seconds of the file as the clip reads it (unwrapped past a loop), so a trim, a stretch or a tempo change keeps each part on its audio; a split and a reverse move it with the audio.
- **Exactly what is shown, with no engine change:** the app sends an audio comp as a clip per part, each overlapping its neighbours by a 10 ms equal-power crossfade centred on the join (at most half the shorter part), through the existing clip player (fades, offsets, up to 16 overlapping voices; nothing new in the audio callback, nothing allocated there). A render test with the engine (`test/comping_test.dart`) checks the comp is each take bit for bit outside the crossfades and their equal-power sum inside, and that Flatten (the same parts as clips) renders identically. No C API change (33); no engine change, so no golden hash changed.
- **Consolidate:** MIDI clips are joined as Glue joins them (their notes as they play, in one new pattern); audio clips go through the bounce path (`caw_render_song`, `stem_tap: "pre"`) from a copy of the project where the track plays only those clips with no instrument, effects or their lanes and links, over their span in whole steps with no tail, into one fixed clip; with the engine it renders as the clips did within 1e-6.
- **Record:** `RecordStart.planRecord` and `pressRecord` (`lib/model/record_start.dart`); the new MIDI track plays the Flagship (the default synth). A MIDI loop recording now makes a take of every pass, an empty one included, but not a last pass recording stopped in before anything was played; audio already did (the engine ends a take at each loop wrap).
- **Project format:** `comp` (MIDI and audio clips) and `show_takes` (a layer) are part of format v5 (no further bump; an older C.A.W refuses the file whole); settings.json gains `record_tip_shown`.
- **Claude's choices for the owner to confirm (looks and feel):** take lanes sit above the automation lanes, 56 px rows (not resizable yet), headers *Take n* in the track's colour with a dot while some comp plays part of that take; lane n shows take n of every clip with takes; the comp's parts lit (the track colour at 30 %) and the rest dimmed (black at 42 %); joins as thin lines, with a small cross on an audio clip's crossfade; the take chip reads **C** for a comp; hold-and-move moves the nearest join (the lanes have nothing else to move); double tap plays the take whole; the lane menu has Use this take, Flatten, Hide take lanes, the header's Use take n everywhere, Flatten all takes, Hide take lanes; the crossfade is 10 ms equal power; Record with no keyboard on an instrument track makes a new audio track (the on-screen keys open only for a MIDI track armed from its menu); the tip is a snack bar for 6 s.
- **Open:** take lane heights are fixed at 56 px (the automation lanes drag theirs); a comp can't be moved to a new clip or saved as a named comp (REAPER's Save as new comp); Overdub and Replace record modes still merge a loop recording's passes, as those modes mean; Flatten of an audio comp keeps the take files on disk until the project is deleted.

## 2026-11-17: Better track automation: tools, curves, transforms, Automate from any control (owner's decisions; Claude's build, #230)

**Owner (2026-11-17, final):** editing stays in the lanes under the track, made bigger, with a tool row (select, pencil, line, shapes at the grid rate, eraser); Automate from any automatable control's hold menu; curved segments (linear, slow, fast, S-curve, step) chosen in the segment's menu and bent by a sideways drag; box select with move, scale and tilt, copy and paste at the playhead, grid snap, "Automation follows clips" per project; one searchable picker grouped by device with recent parameters first; every edit one automation undo step.

**Built ([app](app.md), automation lanes under tracks; [engine](engine.md), Curved segments):**
- **Curve maths:** b = 0.4375 (1 + tension); Slow and Fast are the rational ease t(1∓b) / (t(1∓b) + (1−t)(1±b)), S-curve joins them at the middle, Step holds. Only `+ − × ÷`, dyadic constants, so every platform gives the same bits under the engine's strict floating point (no `pow`; nothing to allocate). Tension −1 is straight, so a sideways bend grows from a line without a jump. Linear keeps the original expression, so existing projects are bit-identical (no reference renders changed). Stored as `curve` (a name) and `tension` on the earlier point, omitted for linear, so a project without curves saves as before.
- **No C API change** (curves are project JSON only; the batch's version comes from the sample library, 33, and the buffer tuner, 34). The new saved fields (point `curve` and `tension`, lane `height`, `recent_automation`, `automation_follows_clips`) are part of project format v5 (merged with the MIDI storage change in batch 1.0.5), which an older C.A.W refuses whole.
- **Claude's choices for the owner to confirm (looks and feel):** lanes 56 px by default (32–240 by dragging the header's bottom edge); the tool row under the lanes (where + Lane was), with "+ Automate" first; at header widths under 236 px the tools fold into one button with a ▾ (a phone's headers can't hold five); drawn tool glyphs in the lanes' stroke style, the chosen tool in a blue glow; the selection box and its handles in yellow (the selected points' colour), edge handles as bars and corner handles as dots; previews in the text colour, the eraser's sweep in pink; a curved segment's handle a little wider and brighter; sideways right bends toward Fast, left toward Slow (80 px for a full bend; Alt or Ctrl with an up or down drag bends up or down); a sine drawn with four curved points a cycle (Fast to each peak, Slow back), squares and random as steps; random seeded by the project seed, the lane and the start; one cycle per grid step (the arranger's 1/16 by default gives fast shapes; a coarser grid slows them); the pencil unsnapped, the other tools snapped (Shift free); a drawn span replaces the points under it; points moved onto others make them give way, as sections do; copy holds heights so it pastes into any lane; the picker a glass sheet with Recent (the project's last 8) first; Automate adds a point at the playhead on a new lane and only reveals an existing one; on a device editor knob Automate is a button on the knob's hold sheet and value dialog beside Learn MIDI CC (the hold already opens those), elsewhere a one-item hold menu; "Automation follows clips" moves points only for moves along the same track.
- **Open:** on a phone the device editor is a full-screen page, so Automate there opens the lane behind it (close the page to see it); the master's chain and modulators have no lanes under a track and don't offer Automate; the focus view's Automation tab keeps its own + for volume and pan.

## 2026-11-17: MIDI clip playback and the piano roll: chasing, eight-octave bends, the sampler's chase (Claude, owner bugs for 1.0.5, #226, #228)

- **Bends reach ±96 semitones** (owner: "I would like to be able to bend as much as I want to"). Eight octaves either way, bend plus vibrato depth, in the pattern text (`kMaxBend`), the app's model (`NoteExpression.maxBend`), the roll's Curve tool and the note extras' curve, and MIDI import. Why 96 and not more: MIDI's bend range (RPN 0's data entry, 0..127) holds it, MPE's default member range is 48 and 96 is the next common step, and past eight octaves a note from the middle of the keyboard leaves hearing at both ends anyway. The engine holds what it sends an instrument to MIDI −60..Nyquist (`Engine::safePitch`), so no oscillator runs past half the rate whatever the bend; the instruments' own code is unchanged. MPE export picks the narrowest of 12, 48 and 96 semitones that holds the pattern's bends.
- **Chased notes start their voice; a sampler retriggers from the sample's start.** A note the playhead lands inside starts at that point with its expression as it is there; the instrument's own attack is its anti-click ramp (Flagship, Drums and the sampler start a voice as for any note-on). The sampler plays its sample from the beginning rather than from the elapsed time into it, as DAWs that chase notes usually do [Assumption, not surveyed]: an offset start needs a new note-event field through the component interface for one instrument, would start a recorded attack's middle with a click the attack can't hide, and a long pad sample starting fresh is rarely wrong. An offset start can come later as a sampler option.
- **A cut note keeps its curves where they were** (the repeat-at-the-clip's-end bug): the arrangement's copy of a cut note's expression carries the share of the written note it plays (`NoteExpression::from`, `span`), so no stored format changes; the playback structs only. With clips stored as MIDI (format v5, below) a clip still cuts notes at play time: the window is computed there, never stored.
- **Taps land in the drawn cell; no stacked notes** (#228): the tap cell is the grid between the lines drawn at the zoom, not a finger-sized coarser one; a note at the same start and pitch as one already there is never added (draw takes the existing one, a drop or a paste replaces it). [Assumption] stacking an exact duplicate is never wanted; a chord on other pitches is untouched.

## 2026-11-17: Clips stored as MIDI: the format, one converter, the migration (Claude, carrying out the owner's #216)

The owner's decision (2026-11-13, below) built on branch `midi-store`; the details in [app](app.md) ("Clips are stored as MIDI").
- **Format: a Standard MIDI File (format 0, 960 PPQ) with C.A.W events, not MIDI 2.0 UMP clip data.** Neither standard holds what pattern text does exactly: exact rational step times (13/3, 1/97), decimal velocities as written (0.8 is not k/127 nor k/65535), chance, a ratchet as one note, curve point shapes, vibrato settings, comments. Both would need an extension, so the base was chosen for reach: every DAW reads a .mid and MPE carries per-note bend, pressure and timbre; MIDI 2.0 clip files are rarely read, and the app already had a bounded SMF reader and writer. The extension is sequencer-specific meta events (FF 7F, the SMF's place for a sequencer's own data) and Text events (FF 01, the SMF's comments). Plain notes (on a 1/240-step grid, whole pitch, velocity k/127, no extras) need no C.A.W event at all: the MIDI is the clip. **Per-note bends up to ±96 semitones** (owner, #226): the record keeps the curve values as written, with no range of its own (pattern_doc's `maxBend` is the only limit), and the MPE layer picks the member bend range the widest bend needs (2, 12, 24, 48 or 96); a ±36 round trip and a ±96 MIDI-layer read are tested.
- **Manufacturer ID 7D:** [Fact, midi.org/sysexidtable, read 2026-11-17] the one-byte IDs 60H–7FH are "[Reserved for Other Uses]". [Assumption, from the MIDI 1.0 specification as known, not re-read] 7DH is the non-commercial (educational/research) ID, which products released to the public are not meant to use. C.A.W has no registered ID; its events are only ever inside its own clip files (never sent as SysEx), and other programs skip sequencer-specific events they don't know. **Open question for the owner:** register a manufacturer ID with the MIDI Association, or keep 7D.
- **One converter: Dart owns MIDI ↔ clip conversion** (`lib/model/clip_midi.dart` for storage; `midi_clips.dart`'s `patternNoteOfMidi`/`midiNoteOfPattern`, the same per-note rules for storage, .mid import and .mid export). Why: the exact model (rational steps, comments, note ids for undo) is the app's `PatternDoc`; the engine's `Pattern` holds doubles and no comments, so it can't own a lossless codec; the editors convert synchronously on the UI isolate and in plain Dart tests without the native library. The app's .mid exports (Export page, single and queued, now with the song's tempo map and signatures, `midiTempos`, ported from the engine's writer: a ramp as a tempo every 1/16 note) use it; the app's engine wrappers for .mid import and song export are deleted. **The engine keeps its converter** (`engine/src/midifile.cpp`) only for the `caw` CLI (headless tools have no Dart) and the MIDI 2.0 Clip File export, which the piano roll reaches by one call (`Engine.exportPattern(clip: true)`) with the clip's text. **The engine's project JSON still takes pattern text** (the app writes it from the MIDI): it is the live wire format, not storage, and keeping it needs no binding or C API version change.
- **Migration v4 → v5:** a v4 module's text converts on load. Proof: `test/clip_midi_test.dart` (every expression feature and 60 random patterns: old text → MIDI → text gives the same notes, header and comments, and the text from MIDI is a fixed point; corrupt and truncated input fails closed) and `test/clip_migration_test.dart` (the engine tests' patterns, the examples, templates and default project: same notes after migration, and with `CAW_LIB` bit-identical renders before and after). `Steps.toString` now writes an exact fraction where it rounded to 1/1000 (only times with denominators over 64 that no 10^9 divides), and a vibrato rate or fade-in without depth is written, so text written from a stored clip reads back the same. **Not exact:** a time written with more than 15 significant digits was already kept as the nearest fraction with a denominator up to 2^20 by the app's parser; it is stored as that.
- **Kept in memory only:** text that doesn't parse. The file holds the last version that parsed (else an empty pattern of the module's name). Undo restores clips from their MIDI, so the text view after an undo is the stored form (layout and chord lines as the editor writes them).
- **Size:** the bundled default project (4 patterns, 87 lines) is 16 904 bytes at v4 and 19 303 at v5 (its modules 4 965 → 7 205 bytes; base64 and the MPE curves of bent notes); the code-built demo project's modules go from 4 515 to 3 913 bytes (v4 saved each text twice, `source` and `run_source`).

## 2026-11-17: AAudio output usage GAME; automatic buffer size off by default (owner's request, #236)

**Decision:** every AAudio output opens with `AAUDIO_USAGE_GAME`, Android's low-latency guide's advice for Bluetooth, on every route (the engine can't tell the route before the stream opens; the volume stays media). The automatic buffer size (`caw_audio_tune_buffer`, Oboe's LatencyTuner way plus a shrink after a quiet minute) is built but off by default (`Settings.bufferTuning`, no Settings row) until it has run on a phone: Claude's choice, to keep 1.0.5 low-risk; turning it on, and its Settings row, are for the owner. The Bluetooth codec is not chosen (a privileged API). Facts and sources in [engine](engine.md).

## 2026-11-17: A new device's first callbacks stay out of the load meter; the performance hint for every stream mode (#234)

**Decision (Claude's, for the owner to confirm; the hint part is the owner's 1.0.5 call: "the ADPF performance hint only", no game category):**
- **Warm-up:** the callbacks of a device's first 0.5 s of frames after an open or an AAudio reroute enter no load average (the CPU meter, shedding's over-budget share, the mix pool's Auto). **Why:** the meter, which telemetry's `perf.worstCallbackPct` reads, moves about 9.9 × a stall's seconds, and a shared or Bluetooth stream starts by filling its buffer back to back on a core whose clock hasn't risen; the app closes an idle device and Play reopens it, so this landed while playing (600 and 800% on the owner's Bluetooth days, nothing shed). **[Assumption]** that first fill is the main source; the meter after the warm-up, and shedding, are unchanged.
- **The hint:** asked for the callback and the workers whatever the callback's policy, as before, but a refused session is asked again (1 s doubling to 30 s), the callback alone if the group is refused, every callback's own duration is reported (not the longest since the last report), and `hint_state` says why there is none. **Why:** the NDK and the system service ask only that the threads be the app's (sources in [engine](engine.md)); telemetry showed no session on the S22+. **Not claimed:** the 1.0.4 Benchmark's low clocks and 13,448 sustained xruns came while the app was behind another app (owner), so this change is not shown to fix them.
- **Benchmark:** a step the app leaves the foreground during runs again (`backgrounded`); a ramp the engine's own budget stops is capped, not an error; live parts say they are unprofiled (a short profiled pass would run the mix on one thread, unlike what plays). The local JSON grows additively; H2 is unchanged.

## 2026-11-17: Two Android builds (owner, #222)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-17):**
- **Two Android builds:**
  - **The GitHub build** has everything: third-party plug-ins and the self-updater.
  - **The Play Store build** follows Play's rule on downloaded code.

## 2026-11-17: Groundwork for the next minor release is laid silently during the 1.0.x batches (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-17):** "We should 100% be laying the groundwork for the 1.1.0 update, so we don't have a ton of work for ourselves, but make the changes silent."
- Each 1.0.x batch may carry hidden groundwork for the next minor release (which parts: the private plans).
- **Silent** means:
  - users see no change;
  - the work sits behind off-by-default internal flags, or is engine-only code with no UI;
  - the project format only gains fields when they are needed, and old versions are unaffected;
  - the changelog doesn't announce it.
- Each step has its own tests and a Benchmark check, so it costs nothing while it's off.

## 2026-11-17: Linked sample folders, the sample browser and automatic metadata (owner, #229)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-17, final):**
- **Linked folders** apply to the whole app, set in Files › Samples with **Link folder…**. Files are used in place, never copied. A missing file shows **Find it** (pick the file, or relink a folder by relative paths). The `.caw` export checklist lists linked files so they can be packaged.
- **A tap previews; a double tap adds** to a smart target, in this order: the selected audio track, at the playhead, snapped; else the selected drum pad or sampler; else a new audio track at the playhead. Dragging onto a track still works.
- **Preview:** a waveform on each row with scrubbing on the playing one; sync to tempo (loops stretched to the project tempo, starting in time with the song while it plays); auto-play next by arrow keys or a swipe; a preview volume; key and BPM on each row; an optional toggle, off by default, to preview through the selected track's effects. The audio callback rules hold.
- **Metadata,** in order of trust: (1) embedded tags (WAV LIST/INFO, acid, smpl, bext, ID3v2 TBPM/TKEY, FLAC Vorbis comments); (2) the file and folder names (BPM, key, type and instrument words, avoiding false hits such as "808"); (3) detection (`caw_asset_tempo` for BPM and a new key estimate, labelled as an estimate with a confidence). Every field becomes a sortable filter (BPM range, key with optional compatible keys, type, instrument, length, folder, tags, search), and where each value came from is stored.
- **Indexing** runs in the background, off the UI isolate, with a cache on disk; bounded and incremental, so a folder of tens of thousands of files doesn't freeze the UI.
- **Security:** linked files are untrusted: parsed with hard limits, no link followed out of the linked root, malformed headers fuzz-tested. On Android, `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE` with a persisted permission; no `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` or other broad storage permission. No new dependency (none was needed).
- **C API 33** (`caw_asset_key`, `caw_engine_preview_sample*`); batch 1.0.5's buffer tuner (#236) follows as 34.
- **When:** batch 1.0.5.

**How it is built** ([app](app.md), [engine](engine.md#the-sample-preview-and-key-estimate)):
- **Look (agent's choices within "follow the Files browser and the glass style"):** a list (not the grid): 56 dp rows with an 84 dp waveform, name over instrument · type · folder, then right-aligned tempo, key and length columns; estimates in italics with a "~" and their source and confidence in a tooltip; the selected row tinted blue, the playing name green; scrolling filter chips under the search box (each opening a glass bottom sheet); a glass preview strip at the bottom with previous, play, next, Sync, FX and a volume slider. Checked by `layout_fit_test` ("samples").
- **Android, `content://` to the engine:** the engine reads files by path, and a document tree gives URIs, not paths. Each file is opened read-only with `ContentResolver.openFileDescriptor(uri, "r")`, the descriptor detached (`ParcelFileDescriptor.detachFd`, "you are now responsible for closing the fd in native code") and handed to Dart, and Dart and the engine read it as `/proc/self/fd/<n>`; Dart closes it through `ParcelFileDescriptor.adoptFd(fd).close()`. Files the open project uses stay open for the session (at most 512 descriptors, the least recently used unpinned closed first); the indexer opens 64 at a time and closes them after reading the headers. Such a path is never saved: the project keeps the link and a placeholder path. **[Fact, developer.android.com "Access documents and other files from shared storage", read 2026-11-17]** the Storage Access Framework needs no system permission; `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE` (API 21+) grants access to the chosen directory and its subdirectories only; `takePersistableUriPermission` keeps the grant across restarts but not when the document is moved or deleted; on Android 11+ the picker refuses the internal storage root, an SD card's root and the Download folder. **[Fact, developer.android.com `ParcelFileDescriptor` reference, read 2026-11-17]** `detachFd` (API 12) and `adoptFd` (API 13) as quoted. **[Assumption]** reopening `/proc/self/fd/<n>` reads a document from a local provider (the device's storage or an SD card) as a file; a cloud provider that hands out a pipe is not seekable and may fail to load; to verify on the S22+.
- **Not built:** watching folders for changes (the issue mentions it; a refresh runs on open, on Read again and on pull-to-refresh, and only reads what changed); AIFF, OGG and other formats (WAV, FLAC and MP3 are listed, as the engine decodes them).

## 2026-11-14: Freeze and bounce: pre-fader, the frozen track sent as an audio clip, no C API bump (Claude, within the owner's 1.0.5 decisions, #209)

The owner's (2026-11-14): Freeze / Unfreeze and Bounce to audio in the track's hold menu, Bounce to audio in a clip selection's hold menu; a snowflake on a frozen track's header and its devices dimmed; editing a frozen track first asks "Unfreeze to edit?"; no header button. Claude's choices within them:
- **Pre-fader.** A freeze renders the instrument, per-note FX and effects (with their automation and modulation) before the fader, so volume, pan, sends, folder routing and mute/solo stay live, as asked; a bounce too, and the new audio track copies the fader, pan, sends and their lanes, so it sounds as the track did.
- **The frozen track is an audio clip, sent by the app.** `ProjectDoc.toEngineJson` sends a frozen layer as one fixed audio clip of its file from step 0 and leaves its instrument, per-note FX, effects and the lanes and links aimed at them out of the engine JSON. The engine never builds them, so they cost nothing, and it plays the file through the existing clip player: bit for bit the live track's sound (tests in [engine](engine.md#song-renders) and `app/test/freeze_test.dart`). Its pattern clips still go, for keys and modulators that hear its notes. The alternative, an engine-side `frozen` key that swaps the chain itself, would have duplicated the app's lane and link filtering in C++.
- **Engine additions without a C API bump:** the song render's `stem_tap: "pre"` (a new request key, refused by name by an engine without it) and an audio clip's `"lengthens_song": false` (a frozen file's tail past the song's end doesn't make the song longer; it plays when the song reaches there and in a render's tail). No C API change (the batch's 33 and 34 come from the sample library and the buffer tuner); nothing new crosses the FFI (the app uses `caw_render_song` through the worker pool as Export does).
- **Tail:** "the track's tail length" is measured, not declared: the render runs on until the track has been below −120 dBFS for half a second, at most 30 s (declared tails are often infinite, a reverb's or a delay's).
- **Project format:** `frozen`, `frozen_asset` and `frozen_context` on a layer, part of format v5 (merged with the MIDI storage change in batch 1.0.5, so no further bump; an older C.A.W refuses the file whole). `frozen_context` (the tempo map, signatures, seed and key) makes a freeze that no longer fits play live instead of out of time.
- **Unfreeze and undo:** the frozen file leaves the project at once but is deleted from disk only when the project next opens and nothing names it, so undo and redo work within the session.
- **Loops:** the frozen audio is the song played from its start, so at a loop range's or the song's wrap it restarts as the live track's first pass does; what a live instrument carries over a wrap (a held note, a reverb ringing into the loop's start) is not in it. **[Assumption]** close enough for a freeze, as in other DAWs; the owner may want otherwise.
- **Not built:** a Benchmark scenario for a frozen track (the scenarios are pinned by hash and reference render; the CPU check is in `render_stems_test.cpp` instead); bouncing audio clips from an audio clip's own menu. Comping and Consolidate came after the MIDI storage change (the entry above).

## 2026-11-14: Clip stretching runs Signalsmith Stretch live in the callback; two new dependencies (Claude, within the owner's 1.0.4 contract, #208)

**Dependencies (new, vendored header-only in `engine/third_party/`, each with its LICENSE file):**
- **Signalsmith Stretch** 1.3.2 (`signalsmith-stretch/signalsmith-stretch.h`), **MIT** ([Fact] its `LICENSE.txt`, "Copyright (c) 2022 Geraint Luff / Signalsmith Audio Ltd.", read 2026-11-14), github.com/Signalsmith-Audio/signalsmith-stretch commit 88061fd4eda56a2353c5d65c74aa3f4ccf76f9da (2026-10-22).
- **Signalsmith Linear** (only `stft.h` and `fft.h`, which is all the stretch needs: no `linear.h`, no platform backends), **MIT** ([Fact] its `LICENSE.txt`, "Copyright (c) 2025 Signalsmith Audio", read 2026-11-14), github.com/Signalsmith-Audio/linear commit 7bb1c8f36794d6980080ce817c91f8da8fe56a66 (2026-10-21). The optional `arm_math`, `xsimd`, Accelerate, IPP and PFFFT backends are left off (none is defined, so the plain C++ FFT is used).
- Read before vendoring: pure DSP (STFT, FFT, phase-vocoder maths); no I/O, threads, system calls or logging (the library's one `std::cerr` is in `linear.h`, not vendored). Only `src/clip_stretch.cpp` includes it (and `audio_stretch_test.cpp`, to pin its draws).
- **Local changes**, each marked "C.A.W local change" where it stands (the audio review and the first Arm64 and Windows CI runs, 2026-11-14): every `std::polar` in `fft.h` and `signalsmith-stretch.h` goes through a `SIGNALSMITH_POLAR` macro (upstream's `std::polar` by default), which `clip_stretch.cpp` points at the engine's deterministic sine, because libm's sin and cos differ between glibc, bionic and MSVC and they feed every FFT twiddle and the per-block rotations; and in `signalsmith-stretch.h` `reseed()`, which `HqStretcher::reset` calls, so every start replays the same random phases (its default constructor seeds from `std::random_device`; C.A.W uses the seeded one with `std::minstd_rand`, which the standard specifies fully); and `_impl::FixedUniform`, a fixed mapping from the raw engine output to a float in place of `std::uniform_real_distribution`, whose algorithm libstdc++, libc++ and MSVC each choose, so the draws are the same on every platform. Re-apply all of them when updating the library. `clip_stretch.cpp` and `time_stretch.cpp` are built with the strict float flags (as `tempo_map.cpp` and `sample_time.cpp` are): CI's first run of the references, recorded on Linux x64, failed on Linux Arm64 (fused multiply-adds) and Windows clang-cl (its libm) by 0.12 dB in one quiet window of the drums at 0.75; with both changes x86-64 gave the Windows figure, and the references were re-recorded from that build.

**Decision: stretch live, not render ahead.** Measured (Linux x64 VM, GCC 13 -O3, 2026-11-14): once `presetDefault` has run, `process()` and `seek()` made **0 allocations** through 4,000 64-frame calls at a 0.8 rate with pitch (a counting `operator new`; `outputSeek()` too), and the code takes no lock (read: buffers are `std::vector`s sized in `configure()` and only `resize`d within their capacity or `assign`ed equal sizes; its peak list is reserved for half the bins, and C.A.W's preparation runs it once on noise with a pitch shift so anything grown on first use has grown). `signalsmith_clip_voices_never_allocate_on_the_audio_thread` (caw_rt_tests) checks the engine's whole path: pre-rolled starts, a mid-clip join, a ramped map, pitch, reverse, loop, a seek and loop wraps. So the contract's preferred design holds and there is no render cache: no memory for rendered copies (a 4-minute stereo clip would be 92 MB), and a tempo or pitch change is heard at once.
- **Costs that shaped it:** a voice costs about 1.1% of this container's core (five times plain playback; numbers in [engine](engine.md#performance)); a whole spectrum at once took up to 423 µs of a 64-frame block, so the split computation is on (worst about 110 µs, one interval more latency, which the feed covers). `outputSeek()`, the library's way to start with valid output, took 1.2 ms per voice: too much for a callback when several clips start together, so it is not used.
- **Starting without a spike:** the stretcher's output is good 90 ms (its output latency at 48 kHz) after a start. A clip ahead of the playhead gets its voice 90 ms early, silent (its source before the clip's offset reads as silence), and before a loop wrap each clip sounding where the loop lands gets a spare voice the same way, which takes over at the wrap. A voice that joins mid-clip (a seek, play from inside a clip, a pitch change) plays WSOLA for those 90 ms; then Signalsmith's output is moved to the lag (±256 frames) that best matches WSOLA, the two crossfade over 512 frames with gains that keep the sum's power for their measured correlation, and the lag is taken back out of the feed over 0.5 s. The audio review measured the first design's linear crossfade dipping 5 to 8 dB for about 5 ms at every wrap and seek; now a sine holds within 1 dB and a chord within 1.5 dB. **[Assumption]** the short WSOLA bridge after a seek is not heard as a different sound; the owner may prefer a gap.
- **Memory and the cap:** about 0.9 MB a stretcher at 48 kHz. A track's timeline holds as many as its Signalsmith clips can play at once (their most overlapping, a step of pre-roll each) plus two (a voice fading out, the loop's spare), made or kept on the control thread at the engine's rate and freed with the timeline that drops them, on the control thread. A track plays at most 8 Signalsmith voices at once (**[Assumption]**; about 9% of this container's core, the S22+ to measure); more play WSOLA and move to Signalsmith when a stretcher frees, through the bridge.
- **Bypass:** ratio 1 with no pitch takes the old direct copy whatever the algorithm, so every API 31 project sounds the same to the bit (every existing reference and golden unchanged); `"stretch": "wsola"` stays the default and old clips keep WSOLA. The app writes `"signalsmith"` for new clips (its choice).
- **Following the tempo:** a clip that follows the tempo is placed by song step (`offset + (step − start) × 15 / source_bpm` seconds of source), read through the tempo map at each frame (each Signalsmith chunk fed from the map at its own frame), so it stays on the bars through a ramp and renders the same at every block size (1.0.3's musical clips played at the tempo where they started). With one tempo, the old formula, to the bit.
- **Tempo detection and slices:** onset-based, built here (spectral flux, autocorrelation for tempo, peak picking for transients; no new dependency), reading the asset in place in one streaming pass (2–3 MB for 600 s); described in [engine](engine.md#tempo-detection-and-slices).
- **C API 32**, the JSON keys and the calls: in the contract's "Engine notes" and `caw.h`. The project JSON keeps API 31's `stretch` (the algorithm's name), so the contract's `stretch` ratio is the key `stretch_ratio`; `follows_tempo` is the same as `"mode": "musical"`; loop points in source frames are `loop_start_frames`/`loop_end_frames` beside the seconds.
## 2026-11-14: Stretch, the sample editor and Slice to MIDI: the app side's choices (Claude, within the owner's 1.0.4 looks, #208, #90)

**Decision (Claude, 2026-11-14, building the owner's decisions of the day; the owner can change any of these):**
- **The notch** is a small triangle in the top-right corner, shown on every clip that plays at its own speed (not one that follows the tempo: its length is in bars, and the contract uses the ratio only when not following). It is filled once the clip is stretched. The fade-out square moves left of it so the corner is the notch's; the edges still trim.
- **The sample editor is the top of the Clip tab**, one scroll with the settings under it, rather than a tab or page of its own: a double tap already opened the Clip tab, and on a phone the waveform and the tools are what the panel shows first.
- **Trim** is "trim the silence off both ends" (−48 dB of the clip's peak), since the editor's edges already trim by hand. **Normalise** aims at a 0 dB peak (the clip's gain stops at +12 dB).
- **A tap on an audio file no clip plays** puts it on a new audio track at the song's start (one undo step) and opens that clip, so the editor always edits a clip ("the clip refers to the file plus its edits").
- **Slice to MIDI** puts up to 16 slices on bank A of a new drums track in Kit mode from C2 (each pad's Sample layer on its slice, the other layers off), more on the sampler (one-shot zones a key apart from C2, at most 64), on a new track right under the audio one. Reversed clips can't be sliced (the cuts would play forwards).
- **Pitch** is semitones −24..24 plus cents −50..50, the contract's ±2400 cents; a v3 clip beyond ±24 semitones (the inspector's buttons reached ±48) is held at ±24.
- **`gain_db` and `gain`:** the file keeps the contract's `gain_db` and, beside it, the exact linear gain, used while the two agree, because a dB value doesn't come back bit for bit and every undo would move a gain.
- **Keep pitch** means the clip's own keep-pitch algorithm: a WSOLA clip keeps WSOLA, new clips get Signalsmith Stretch from C API 32 (the engine notes: old clips keep their sound).
- **#90:** the lanes keep their scroll view; each lane builds the clips in a window around the view and places them relative to its origin, rather than a new viewport widget, so every gesture, drop and drag keeps working unchanged.

## 2026-11-13: The tempo map's engine side: one strict map, single tempo to the bit (Claude, within the owner's 1.0.3 contract, #207)

**Decision (Claude, 2026-11-13, building the owner's decisions of the day for batch 1.0.3):**
- **One map for every position.** `src/tempo_map.h` (`TempoMap`, `TimeSigMap`, `SongTime`) turns every beat or step into a sample: notes, layer passes, loops, automation, the metronome and count-in, the components' transport, audio clips, renders and recording. Its closed forms (60 / bpm s a beat; a linear-in-BPM ramp `60 / k × log1p(k d / bpm0)` and its `expm1` inverse) are built strict and out of line, as `sample_time.cpp` is, so timing stays exact on every platform.
- **Single tempo stays bit-identical.** Points that never change the tempo merge into one segment, whose maths is exactly the old `step × 60 × sr / (bpm × 4)`; where the old code used other forms (a layer pass's `roundRepeat`, notes relative to their pass, the metronome's beat offset, an audio clip's tempo check), a single-tempo map keeps the old expression and a map with tempo changes takes the general one. Every golden and reference is unchanged.
- **Swapped as a whole.** A map and its signatures travel as one `SongTime` through the command queue (built on the control thread, retired off the audio thread). While playing, any change involving a map keeps the beat; a change from one tempo to another keeps the sample, as before.
- **Metronome ticks are 1/den notes**, accented on each bar (6/8 clicks six eighths), on the first sample at or after the tick; the count-in uses the signature and tempo where playback starts. **[Assumption]** This is the usual DAW behaviour (REAPER's default); the owner can ask for dotted-beat grouping instead.
- **Audio clips:** a musical clip plays at the tempo where it starts; tempo-following audio stays 1.0.4's, as the contract says. A fixed clip keeps its seconds only if the app sends its `length_steps` worked out on the map (the engine takes steps).
- **MIDI files have no ramps:** export writes a ramp as a tempo event every 1/16 note, each holding the tempo that keeps its stretch's exact length; import reads every tempo and meter event as stepped points.
- **The audio review's fixes (2026-11-14):** the ramp maths uses the engine's own `log1p`/`expm1` (bit-identical everywhere, not libm); a live map change keeps a playhead inside the loop range; a note's expression spans its real length on the map; per-sample lanes read the chunk's steps (one segment lookup a chunk); the position is the audio thread's (a sequence lock); recording takes the Engine's map and the map holds while it records; MIDI export refuses tempos and lengths the format can't hold and leaves out changes after the pattern's end; the count-in counts whole bars of the signature (`caw_engine_play_count_in_bars`, which the app sends). The plug-in benchmark keeps the cheapest of three runs per curve point.
- **C API 31 additions the contract assumed:** there was no `caw_engine_set_bpm` or `caw_engine_position` before; both are new (set_bpm replaces the map with one point; position reports sample, beat, seconds, tempo, bar). The project JSON's map is the main route; the live setters hold until the next project update.

## 2026-11-13: Tempo lane, signature flags and the section bar: the app side's choices (Claude, within the owner's #207 looks)

**Decision (Claude, 2026-11-13, for the owner to confirm; the looks themselves are the owner's):**
- **Sections are the old regions.** A region (`MarkerDoc` with an end) is a section: one list in memory, saved apart as v3's `sections` and `markers`. Overlapping v2 regions are kept apart on load (a later one starts where the one before ends; with no room it becomes a marker), so nothing is lost.
- **Heights:** section bar 24 / 22 px (phone / other), ruler 28 / 24, tempo lane 40 / 32. Section names are white on the section's colour; markers are a pole and a small name flag; signature flags are yellow n/d tags in the ruler's lower half; the tempo is green (line, fill and labels). A section's name starts after the flags of markers at its start.
- **Gestures beyond the owner's list:** a tempo point drags up and down at once with a finger (one axis, so it wins over scrolling) and moves in time by hold and move; a tap on the lane adds a point, a double tap deletes one (the automation lanes' rules). A section drop snaps to the nearest bar line (Shift: any step) and is refused onto another section. Copy puts the copy in the first free place after the section.
- **Contents of a section** are the clips and audio clips that start inside it, the automation points inside it, and its tempo and signature changes; where it lands, its tempo and signature apply from its start and what follows keeps its own, and automation points already there give way. Delete clears that span without closing the gap.
- **The section menu** is exactly the owner's four (rename, colour, copy, delete); the old region menu's Loop this region, Select its clips and Duplicate with clips went (double tap loops; Copy is the duplicate).
- **MIDI import** with Adopt takes the file's whole tempo map and signatures; MIDI tempos are steps, so a map exported from a ramp needs steps too (the engine side's choice).

## 2026-11-13: Clips are stored as MIDI; Windows engine on Clang (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-13):**
- **MIDI storage:** "It should store the MIDI. The text is only for creating MIDI through a text editor… MIDI is a lot better format than just the text." Projects will store MIDI clips as MIDI, with pattern text as an authoring view only (#216). This replaces the choice made on the owner's behalf in #158 (pattern text as storage).
- **Windows engine:** built with Clang (clang-cl) instead of MSVC (#199). **Built (2026-11-13, branch `b1-extras`, Claude's choices):** Visual Studio's ClangCL toolset (MSVC ABI, so the DLL loads beside the MSVC-built Flutter runner); the app builds the engine as its own CMake build (`ExternalProject` in `app/windows/CMakeLists.txt`, since a Visual Studio build has one toolset; the runner and plug-ins stay MSVC; `CAW_ENGINE_CLANG_CL=OFF` goes back to MSVC). The engine takes the Linux/Android flags through `/clang:` (`-O3`, `-ffp-contract=fast -fno-math-errno -fno-trapping-math -fno-signed-zeros -fassociative-math`); strict files negate each one, since `/fp:precise` alone does not. x86-64 baseline kept (no AVX2). **[Fact, CI logs 2026-11-13]** the `windows-2025` runner has Visual Studio 18 2026 with Clang 22.1.3 for ClangCL (CMake 4.4.5), not the Visual Studio 2022 its published image readme listed. Every engine test passes with it (Engine run [43505893182](https://github.com/Kmandevr/C.A.W/actions/runs/43505893182)); on one runner it is 12–17% faster than MSVC for songs and 44% for the heavy Flagship ([engine performance](engine-performance.md#a5-cpu-features)).
- **Changelog:** a changelog from 1.0.0, in the app and on GitHub, starting with 1.0.2.

## 2026-11-13: Benchmark 2, engine side: features, engine parts, the plug-in benchmark's checks (Claude, within the owner's #197)

**Built:** C API 30 to the contract of #197 (the app builds against it): [engine](engine.md#the-benchmark), [SDK](native-module-sdk.md#benchmark-your-component).

**Choices (Claude; the owner set what to cover, its look and "Checks + sections", the rest was left to what is best for the app):**
- **Feature scenarios beside the ten**, not in caw_bench's suite (its CI tables stay comparable) and not in H2 (the relay knows ten ids). Their cheap sources are the engine's internal test tone, so a feature's own cost shows rather than an instrument's. `sends_many` sends each of 4 buses twice (pre and post) from 4 tracks: 32 sends with few buses, since a track has at most 8 sends and empty buses alone would make routing, not sends, the largest part.
- **Parts are exclusive and add up**, one current at a time, switched by scopes (a null check each when not profiling). A folder bus claims the mix graph's sum of its children into its input (routing: that is what a folder does); summing into the master and each track's gain stay mix. The audio-clip player and the sampler are `samples` whole, the master chain `master`: so `devices` is instruments and effects on tracks and buses. Resolving a component's values (lanes, ramps, project modulation) is `automation`. A live run, not profiled, gives `device_io` and the rest as `other`.
- **The plug-in benchmark's request** names a built-in id, or a library with the user's trust, and only Trusted opens one (refused before loading otherwise). It times a fresh instance per measurement at 48 kHz in 64-frame blocks, flush-to-zero on as the Engine runs it.
- **Thresholds** (all on the device that runs it; a check whose costs stay under 0.5 % of a block passes as too cheap to matter, except allocations and subnormal output): allocations fail at any; denormals warn over 15 % slower unflushed or any subnormal output, fail over 50 %; cost per voice warn over 1.5 % of a block, fail over 6 % (16 voices fill the block); block size warn when a frame costs over 50 % more at 32 than at 512 frames, fail over 150 %; sample rate warn over 2.4 times the 48 kHz cost at 96 kHz (2 is linear), fail over 3.2; parameter changes graded by the extra as a share of a block (warn over 2 %, fail over 10 %), so many parameters aren't failed for their number; silence warns over 25 % of the working cost unless it sleeps. **[Assumption]** these suit a phone's budget; to check against the owner's first plug-ins.
- **Allocations are counted only where the host can count them** (the test build's global `operator new`): a loaded library's own allocator and `malloc` can't be hooked portably in the app, so there the check warns as not measured instead of passing.
- **Sections** come through a host extension (`caw.profile.v1`) the SDK's `create` asks for, recorded only on the bench's thread into 32 fixed slots, so a section left in shipped code costs a load and a branch. They are exclusive (a nested section's time comes off its parent's: the shares add up), after the audio review.
- **After the security and audio reviews (2026-11-13):** trust is read when a plug-in is listed and again when its run starts (`NativeComponentManager.benchTrusted`: the current sources' choice, and a packaged build's own), and the binding sends Trusted only then; blocking or forgetting drops what the manager had loaded for it. The bench runs on its own thread, watched: a block past max(1 s, 100 times its budget) fails the job and that thread is abandoned, never joined, and a second bench fails fast instead of waiting. It still runs Trusted plug-ins in-process (a crash in one closes the app), said in the app and the docs, until the Isolated runner (#212).

## 2026-11-13: Low latency takes AAudio's own callback sizes; the Benchmark measures the app's figure (Claude, from the owner's S22+ run)

**Decision:** where AAudio is given no callback size (Low latency, Auto while recording, any size on Android 11 and older), the output takes the stream's own sizes (miniaudio's `noFixedSizedCallback`) instead of asking for the burst and reopening. **Why:** miniaudio's fixed size fell back to the stream's whole capacity (about 1950 frames on the S22+) rendered inside one burst's callback, so Low latency dropped out under 100 % load; AAudio's documentation recommends its own sizes for the lowest latency, a reopen costs a gap and on Android 11 would not reach the stream at all, and `DeviceRender` renders any size (tested). The Benchmark's timed runs now run the mix pool on Auto after an untimed warm-up, and the live runs ask for the playing buffer and Low latency, so its numbers are what the app gets ([engine](engine.md#the-benchmark)). H2 and the C API (29) are unchanged; the new numbers stay in the local JSON.

## 2026-11-13: Flagship's fold: a fold mode, a hybrid of ADAA and 2x, and a float drive (owner, #197)

**Decision (owner, GitHub issue #197, comment of 2026-11-13):** three changes to speed up the Flagship, all approved:
- **A fold mode per oscillator** (`oscN_fold_mode`, appended after the FM ratios): Per copy (the default, and what every project and preset saved without it plays) or Whole stack. **Design (Claude):** Whole stack sums the unison copies at their pan gains before the level and folds each side's sum once (once in all when every copy is centred), then applies the level, because a side hears only its own copies; Osc FM hears the mean of the two sides' folds. The level comes after the fold in both modes, so it never changes the fold.
- **The fold: a hybrid of ADAA at 1x and the 2x path (owner, 2026-11-13).** First (approved the same day) first-order antiderivative anti-aliasing (ADAA) at the sample rate replaced the fold's 2x oversampling in both modes and at every quality, written as sin(k m) × sinc(k d / 2) (exactly the ADAA quotient for the sine, so the ill-conditioned v ≈ p needs no threshold). Measured: at A6 with full drive its aliases were only 39 dB down against 63 dB for the 2x path (6 dB better than no anti-aliasing), though an octave lower, or at 0.6 of the drive, 67 and 74 dB (2x: 62 and 73). **Decision (owner, 2026-11-13): build a hybrid**, the old 2x path for high notes and ADAA below them, so high notes stay clean and most of the speed stays. **Design (Claude; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a6-flagship-fold-and-drive-197)):** (1) The crossover, from a sweep of A4..A7 a semitone apart at five drives in both modes against the 2x paths before and after: a note goes 2x when f × (drive + 2) > 10 kHz, f its highest frequency (the note, the oscillator's tuning, its stack's top copy and detune, and the bend range up) and the drive the most it may reach (8 when a slot moves the warp amount). ADAA keeps the aliases below −60 dB, the 2x path's own worst over the range, up to about 14 kHz on that scale per copy and 12 kHz for a whole 7-copy stack; 10 kHz leaves 3.5 semitones of margin or more. Below the crossover ADAA is at times several dB worse than 2x at a given note (B4–C5 at full drive: −66 against −79 dB), always below −60. (2) No clicks: the path is chosen when the voice starts from silence and held until it falls silent (as Eco's FM path is), not crossfaded: no work while a note plays, nothing to warm up (a crossfade would need the decimators run ahead of the switch), and a bend or glide can never switch it. The cost: a note bent or glided far above its start, or whose drive a knob raises later, keeps ADAA and aliases more; the bend range in the choice covers the usual bends. (3) Eco folds every note with ADAA. (4) The 2x path is the one from before #197 rewritten for both modes (Whole stack sums the copies at twice the rate and folds each side's sum), reading the table in fixed point as ADAA does and folding with the same fine sine, so a note sounds the same either side of the crossover but for the anti-aliasing; a centred stack runs one decimator for both sides. ADAA also delays the fold half a sample and softens its top octave (about −2 dB at 10 kHz).
- **A float drive:** the voice filters' drive runs in float four samples at a time (`Saturator::processApproxFloat`, SSE or NEON through GCC/Clang vectors) instead of double pairs; within 1e-6 of the double form (−138 dBFS on the heavy patch), no reference re-recorded for it.

The re-recorded references are `stress.cawref`'s `flagship_heavy` and `flagship_heavy16` only (the fold sound change; `flagship_heavy` again for the hybrid, whose top three voices fold 2x); the numbers are in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a6-flagship-fold-and-drive-197).

## 2026-11-13: The polish release is 1.0.1-alpha.1; the app stays alpha until the owner says otherwise (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-13):** the polish pass ships as **1.0.1-alpha.1**. "It'll end up being a full release if it's stable. But again the app is still an alpha … until I say it's going to go back to 1.0 and as an official release not just alpha." So versions keep the `-alpha.N` suffix (the beta telemetry channel, the Open alpha notice) until the owner calls the official release.

## 2026-11-13: The Benchmark (beta plan C) built; how it measures (Claude, within the owner's look)

**Built:** section C, to the owner's look of 2026-11-12 (below) and the architect's plan: [engine](engine.md#the-benchmark), [app](app.md).

**Choices (Claude; the owner said to do what is best for the app where the plan left it open):**
- **"Through the native worker pool" (C5) means a `CawWorkerPool` job:** `caw_worker_pool_submit_stress` returns a `CawJob` (`TaskKind::Stress`) with the status, cancel, wait and release every job has, plus `caw_stress_progress`, `caw_stress_history`, `caw_stress_result` and `caw_stress_costs`. C API **29** (27 while built; audio-integration's 28 merged first: 24, 26, 27 and 28 are its).
- **Shared code:** caw_bench's builders moved verbatim to `engine/src/stress_scenarios.cpp` in caw_core, so the app's and CI's projects are the same; a hash test of each project and reference renders of 1 s each guard them.
- **Timed like CI:** 256-frame callbacks at Normal quality on the run's own engine, overload shedding off on it (one flag checked where a layer is shed; changing it resets bt-clicks' shedding, so shed layers come back and the load history is forgotten).
- **A ramp step holds** when its p99 callback is within the budget (at most 1 in 100 over); the counts rise to the first step that doesn't hold, then halve the gap below it, never above it, which keeps C4's "stop at the first over-budget step" and gives exact limits.
- **Tiers:** a tier's worst p99 at most 70 % of the budget is comfortable, at most 100 % tight, else over budget. **[Assumption]** 30 % headroom covers the UI, other apps and heat; worth checking on the S22+.
- **Dropouts in H2:** a live run's (AAudio's XRun count, else its callbacks over budget) when that scenario ran live, else the timed callbacks over budget.
- **`topCosts`:** components' shares averaged over the profiled songs, built-in ids only (the host's share and C++ components left out, rather than sent as "other").
- **The profiler is per Engine**, carried to the chains in their `ChainTime` (no thread-local storage on the audio path: Android's emulated TLS can allocate; audio review, 2026-11-13), replacing the global pointer whose `slot()` was unsynchronised. A parallel mix uses its pool only when `wantsMixPool()` and the call has no profile (the one it read at its start, so a change mid-callback never reaches workers).
- **The live run keeps the device** (audio review, 2026-11-13): while its meter is installed, another engine's `caw_audio_start` is refused (safer than noticing too late), and a run whose callbacks stop for a second fails.
- **Memory:** an Extreme scenario whose measured need (peak RSS growth on Linux x64 plus a third) passes the engine's work-memory budget is skipped and says so.
- **Share** is Android's share sheet with the summary and the JSON (desktop: the clipboard); Save is the save picker.

## 2026-11-13: The audio branches merged: the capture bridge, the device FIFO and the mix pool (C API 28)

**Decision (the coordinator's merge order and rules, 2026-11-13; Claude's choices within them marked):** `audio-resilience`, then `bt-clicks`, then `speed-parallel`, every feature kept.
- **One output path:** the output device is always playback-only, so the block FIFO (`DeviceRender`) is used whether the input is open or not; the duplex loop is gone. The input bridge is read inside the render, once per block (`DeviceRender::render`'s input callable), so the input follows the frames rendered. **Claude's choices, after the audio review of the merge (2026-11-13):** the bridge's starting fill and slack cover the output callback as miniaudio calls it (the larger of the backend's period and `intermediaryBufferCap`) plus the FIFO's most (63 frames), so a callback that renders a block more than it plays doesn't run it dry; the drift loop samples the fill once a callback; the figures that align recordings take the FIFO's mean hold, `(64 − gcd(period, 64)) / 2`, on the output side, while the input side's fill, the FIFO's most included, is queued input: each delay counted once.
- **Shedding and the pool:** `reportLoad` takes the pool's times first, before every early return; Auto decides on the load per frame rendered; a shedding reset clears Auto's averages and its over/under times. **Claude's choices, after the audio review of the merge:** a callback counts toward shedding when its load per frame rendered passes 0.85 or its raw load reaches its played deadline (1); shedding holds off for 0.7 s (`kMixSettleSeconds` = `kMixWaitSeconds` + 0.2 s) after Auto turns a pool on and after a shedding reset with it on, and Auto adds no worker meanwhile; an overload while the callback waits on the workers turns Auto off before any layer is shed; a new live device starts Auto on one thread; the performance hint gets the callback's real time against the frames it played (the device's deadline), not per frame rendered.
- **Worst case no worse than serial:** measured after the merge ([engine performance](engine-performance.md#5-parallel-mixer), "After the audio merge"): where Auto uses the pool the worst case is lower than serial; where it doesn't, it runs the serial path (the same instructions, +0.002%). Auto stays the default.
- **Auto's tail guard (built 2026-11-13 at the coordinator's request after the contended bench; Claude's choice of measure and numbers, for the owner to confirm):** Auto also falls back to one thread on the tail, not only on the mean wait: the share of callback time that missed its played deadline (raw load 1 or more), a 2 s bias-corrected average kept apart for pooled and serial callbacks, pooled above serial by more than 2 percentage points for 1 s, turns Auto off for `kMixRetrySeconds`. **Why:** stability is the owner's first rule; on a busy machine a preempted worker made the pool's worst case and p99 exceed serial while its mean wait stayed low. The share of late callbacks rather than a decaying max: it measures how often a deadline is missed, and one stray spike doesn't trip it. The deadline rather than 0.85: with 0.85 the guard tripped in a quiet bench run (the pooled p99 sat near 0.85) and fell back to one thread, where the 32-track song is at 80% of the budget and shed a layer. The quiet bench keeps Auto on for the 32-track song.
- **C API 28:** 24 is speed-parallel's `caw_audio_threads` (numbered while built beside 25), 26 `caw_audio_set_background`, 27 `caw_engine_shedding_stats` (26 on its branch), 28 the merge; the benchmark branch's 27 becomes 29 when it is merged on top.
- **Bench:** `caw_bench SECONDS 500 --mode auto` runs the pool on Auto as a device does (each callback's time through `reportLoad`).

## 2026-11-13: Small updates (owner, 2026-11-12: "yes on small updates")

**Decision (owner):** build smaller update downloads, rebuilt on the device into the full APK (Android still installs a whole, signed APK) and only the changed files on Windows.

**Claude's choices (details and measured sizes in [app](app.md), "Updates"):**
- **APK: rebuilt from the installed APK's exact bytes, per zip entry.** Unchanged entries (and their local headers) are copied from the old APK, changed entries' stored bytes, the signing block and the central directory are data, so the result is byte-identical by construction and checked against the published SHA-256 before it installs. **[Fact]** (2026-11-12) the rebuilt 57 MB APK of 641fe41 equals the published one byte for byte (Dart's applier, from 9f92e8f).
