- **No binary diff inside the APK's compressed entries.** The APK stores native libraries deflated (`useLegacyPackaging = true`, decided earlier for download size), and a diff of the uncompressed libapp.so (1.57 MB by bsdiff instead of 4.9 MB) would need the device to re-deflate it to the identical bytes. **[Fact]** (2026-11-12) Python's zlib 1.3 at level 6 reproduces the CI entry exactly, but Dart's bundled zlib does not (5,010,662 bytes instead of 4,889,573), and Android's own zlib is not guaranteed to either, so this is not cheap or reliable. **Decision (owner, 2026-11-13):** keep the native libraries compressed so full downloads stay small; small Android updates stay at about 4.7 MB rather than the 1.6 MB uncompressed storage would allow.
- **bsdiff for Windows files (and any stored APK entry).** CI runs Colin Percival's bsdiff 4.3 from Ubuntu's package (`apt-get install bsdiff`): **[Fact]** BSD-2-Clause (Debian's copyright file for bsdiff 4.3-23, read 2026-11-12; one Debian patch to `bspatch`, which CI does not use, has LGPL-2+ gnulib macros). It is a CI tool only, never shipped; its bzip2 blocks are decompressed with the runner's `bzip2` and re-packed with deflate, and the app has its own bspatch-format reader in Dart (`patch_format.dart`), so no new app dependency. **Refused:** the Python `bsdiff4` package, whose core is under the "BSD Protection License" (not a standard permissive licence; read 2026-11-12).
- **The CI builder is plain Node** (`scripts/update-delta.js`, no npm packages, as `scripts/` requires) and applies every patch before publishing it; a patch that does not rebuild its target, or is not smaller, is not published (WARN), and the release still goes out.
- **Trust chain:** the release notes (and GitHub's asset digests) give the manifest's SHA-256; the manifest gives the patch's and the target's; the rebuilt APK must equal the release's published SHA-256. Secret-free: the jobs keep their own `contents: write` and `github.token`; assets go up before the notes are rewritten, and `latest-main` deletes a patch asset its new build has none of.
- **New app dependency `path` 1.9.1** (pub.dev, the Dart team; **[Fact]** BSD-3-Clause per the package's LICENSE, read 2026-11-13; it was already in the lock file through Flutter): `canonicalize` and `isWithin` for the Windows clean-up's "inside the app folder" check.
- **Hardening after the security review (2026-11-13, nothing exploitable found):** the clean-up only deletes set-aside files whose canonical path is within the app folder; an interrupted swap writes a progress line per file (path, and whether the file is new) before touching it, and recovery undoes only those (new files deleted, replaced ones restored), so a stale `.caw-old` from an earlier update is never restored; patch and stage paths refuse Windows device names (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM1–9, LPT1–9, with or without an extension) and anything under `.caw-update/`, and a patch naming one file twice in different case is refused.
- **Windows swap at the next start, then a relaunch:** the app can't replace files it is running from, so the stage waits beside the app (same drive, so moves are renames) and is swapped in first thing at the next start, before the window shows; the new build then starts in a fresh process, given the same command-line arguments. Files the new build no longer has are left in place (harmless).

## 2026-11-12: Updates: the check, the prompt, channels, other versions and automatic updates (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-12, in order given):**
- **The check:** GitHub Releases now, the source pluggable for a later AutoStacc distribution; never at startup; at most once a day; silent on any failure; nothing about the user in the request.
- **Never forced:** a prompt with **Update**, **Not now** and **Don't ask again** (per user, not per version); after startup, never over the startup screen, never while playing or recording. Settings › About › Updates has **Update now**, the notes, Copy download link, Check now and the automatic-check switch.
- **Update installs:** Android downloads the APK and hands it to the system installer; Windows downloads its asset and opens it or its folder; Linux or no asset copies the link. Downloads only from github.com and GitHub's asset storage over https, at most about 300 MB, stale downloads deleted, nothing run without the user's press.
- **Other versions:** "Install another version…" lists the channel's releases; going back is honest about each platform (Android needs an uninstall, which deletes data; the app never uninstalls itself), warns that newer projects may not open in an older version, and offers backups first; an older C.A.W refuses a newer project safely.
- **Channels:** Stable (default; the public line, `-alpha.N` and later full releases), Beta (`-beta.N` too), Every change (the rolling `latest-main` build of each Main push, opt-in, marked untested). Pushes to Main never make a stable release; the owner tags builds. Selected by tag suffix; semver order, alpha < beta < full for one number. The public line starts at v1.0.0-alpha.1, shown as "Open alpha"; the startup screen and About say "Open alpha: expect rough edges and unexpected issues."
- **Automatic updates:** off by default. On Stable: Off, Patches only, Minor and patches, or All, including major, against the installed version; a bigger step still shows through the prompt and Settings. On Beta and Every change: one switch for the channel's newest. Never while playing, recording or rendering; the project saved first; never a downgrade; as silent as each platform allows (Android 12+ PackageInstaller with `USER_ACTION_NOT_REQUIRED`), else the system prompt once; Windows downloads and notes it at the next start; Linux notifies only.

**[Assumption] (Claude, until the owner says otherwise):** during the open alpha a new alpha number (1.0.0-alpha.1 to -alpha.2), and an alpha to the full release of the same number, is a patch-level step for automatic updates.

**Built (2026-11-12; Claude's choices, details in [app](app.md), "Updates"):**
- `lib/model/updates/` (semver, channels and release parsing, the capped fetcher with the host allow-list, `UpdateService`), `lib/platform/update_installer.dart` (Android channel `caw/updates`, Windows Explorer, none), `UpdateInstaller.kt` (a PackageInstaller session for every in-app install, so later ones can be silent; no FileProvider needed), the prompt host above the shell, Settings › About › Updates and Install another version.
- **Allowed hosts:** github.com, objects.githubusercontent.com and also **release-assets.githubusercontent.com**: on 2026-11-12 github.com's release download URLs answered 302 to that host, so without it no download would work. Every redirect hop is checked; the first URL is pinned to `https://github.com/Kmandevr/C.A.W/releases/download/`.
- **After the security review (2026-11-12):** the Android install status goes to a non-exported manifest receiver through an explicit PendingIntent with a random token (the dynamic receiver was exported below API 33), and the installer's confirmation opens only when it resolves to a system app holding INSTALL_PACKAGES; Windows offers only the zip (no `.msi`/`-setup.exe` kind: an installer path needs a signed installer first); downloads stop after 30 s idle or 30 min; every download is checked against its SHA-256 (GitHub's asset `digest`, else CI's `SHA256 <asset>: <hex>` notes line; unverified older releases: allowed on Android, which checks the signature, and said plainly on Windows); CI uploads `latest-main`'s assets before it rewrites the notes.
- **Android:** `INTERNET`, `REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES` and `UPDATE_PACKAGES_WITHOUT_USER_ACTION` in the manifest. A silent install waits until the app is in the background (Android stops the app it replaces and does not restart it); the APK's own signature and versionCode checks are Android's. Not yet run on a device: CI builds it.
- **Windows:** a small update stages the changed files and swaps them in at the next start (decision of 2026-11-13 above); otherwise the release's zip is downloaded and shown in Explorer with "Downloaded to <folder>"; there is no installer to run. Auto-updates download in the background and say so at the next start.
- **Every change in CI:** app.yml's `every-change` job, after the Android and Windows builds of a push to Main, moves the `latest-main` tag and replaces the release's assets (`caw-main-android.apk`, `caw-main-windows.zip`) and notes (with `Version:` and `Build:` lines), as a pre-release with `--latest=false`, one run at a time (concurrency group `every-change-release`), with the job's own `contents: write` and no secret. Every build gets `--dart-define=CAW_BUILD=<the first 12 characters of the commit>` (one define for the update check and telemetry's `app.build`; the notes' `Build:` line uses the same 12).
- **Newer project files:** `ProjectDoc.fromJson` refuses a `caw_app_project` above `fileVersion` (`NewerProjectFormat`); `ProjectStorage.load` passes it on instead of calling the file damaged, and the app opens the default project with a line saying why, leaving the file as it is.
- **No new Dart package:** dart:io's HttpClient and the platform channel; no url_launcher (Copy download link instead).

## 2026-11-12: The music video's second round: export, layout, text, images, transitions, camera, the beat and speed (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-12, answers of the day):**
- **Export:** 24 fps and a quality or file-size choice (small, balanced or high, the bitrate shown); range as it is; transitions, processing and effects "on the beat" in the export.
- **Layout:** the sheet and editor in clear sections or tabs (Shots/Edit, Look/Effects, Text & media, Export), the preview kept in sight, a pinned compact export bar with Render, in the app's design language and clearly better on a phone.
- **Text and titles:** overlays per shot (title, artist, free text) with size, place, a palette colour and in/out animations (fade, slide, pop, typewriter) that can sync to the beat; the Title card becomes one preset.
- **Images:** the user's image (cover art, logo, watermark) as an overlay or a background, per shot or for the whole video, picked with the system picker, copied into the project's assets, decoded with size limits.
- **Transitions:** crossfade, slide, zoom, flash, wipe, glitch, their length in beats or bars, per cut (cut marker → picker); auto-cut picks transitions to match the style.
- **Camera moves:** keyframed pan and zoom per shot (start and end framing, eased); the auto camera stays the default.
- **Effects on the beat:** presets per effect ("pulse on every beat", "flash on the downbeat") and beat markers on the lane.
- **Speed** ("really slow to build the video"): profile first with numbers, fix what it shows; render in the background while the user works with a progress chip and a done notice, only while the app is open (the mediaPlayback foreground service approved for playback on another branch is not extended here); a quick draft render; before and after in seconds per rendered second.
- **Storage:** everything saves with the project; the format's version goes up, older projects read, parsing bounded.

**Built (Claude, 2026-11-12; choices to confirm, [App](app.md) "Music video" has the behaviour, [performance](performance.md#music-video-render-2026-11-12) the numbers):**
- **Quality:** Small, Balanced and High are 0.05, 0.1 and 0.2 bit per pixel per frame (1–40, 2–80 and 4–80 Mbit/s); Balanced is what every video used, so a saved video renders as it did. **Draft:** 360 on the short side, 15 fps, Small, saved with `_draft` in its name.
- **"On the beat in the export"** read as: the export draws each frame at its song time, so transitions timed in beats or bars, the effects' beat presets and beat-synced text land on the beat in the file exactly as in the preview; the Export tab says what is on the beat. [Assumption] No separate export-wide beat switch was added; open (owner): whether one was wanted.
- **Layout:** the preview (about a quarter of the height on a phone) and the lane stay on top, four tabs under them (Shots, Look, Text & media, Export; the words without icons under 420 dp), the export bar pinned at the bottom with Draft and Render. The full-screen page is the same panel on the same video (from 900 dp the preview and lane on the left, the tabs on the right). The director's settings moved into the Shots tab as "Auto director".
- **The Title card stays its own switch** at the top of Text & media, the first of the text presets (Title card, Title, Artist, Lower third, Text): drawn as before, so earlier videos' frames are unchanged (the pinned frame hashes pass). Title and Artist overlays read the title card's fields.
- **Text on the beat** (the default for a new overlay): it comes in on the first beat of its span, a popped text pulses on every beat, a typewriter types a letter a sixteenth.
- **Images** go into `video-assets/<project>/` beside the projects (deleted with the project), saved by file name only (`img<n>.<ext>`, checked against a pattern), so a downloaded project can't point the app at other files. Ids are never reused (one past the highest `img<n>` among the assets and the folder's files), and a file whose asset is gone (removed, or undone away) is deleted from that folder when the sheet closes, not at once, so an undo can bring it back. Limits: 24 MB a file, 8,192 px a side and 40 megapixels (read from the header before decoding), 16 megapixels for PNG, GIF and BMP ([Assumption] from Skia's SkCodec: only JPEG and WebP decode straight at a reduced size, so the others are whole in memory for a moment), kept at most 2,048 px on the long side. Only the images the video's layers use are decoded, within 128 MB together (each at its share; re-decoded when more or fewer are in use). [Assumption] Project packages (zip) don't carry them yet; open (owner): whether they should.
- **Transitions:** Push stays beside the six new ones (saved videos use it). Lengths offered: half a beat, 1, 2 and 3 beats, a bar, two bars. Flash is one burst at the cut; Flash and Glitch carry a note for photosensitive viewers. Auto-cut's matching (`styledTransition`) is on by default, also for auto-cut settings saved before it (its sheet's settings save with the video).
- **Camera moves** frame the whole shot (zoom 1–3×, the place kept inside the picture) with four eases and five presets; with one, the views keep their own width (no opening zoom) and still follow the song.
- **Beat presets** set the drive, pulse length and amount; an Off-beat drive was added for "hit the off-beat". The lane ticks every beat and dots each beat effect's hits.
- **Speed** (profile first; [performance](performance.md#music-video-render-2026-11-12)): the render keeps a profile; frames rasterise up to three ahead of the encoder (about 64 MB at most); Android frames go through the codec's input Surface (an EGL texture draw, the GPU converts RGBA to YUV, each frame stamped at exactly its frame time) over a binary channel into two reused buffers, with a row-at-a-time CPU path where the Surface fails; the encoder no longer waits 10 ms a frame for output mid-stream; laid-out text is kept between frames. Not done: more than one raster thread (Flutter rasterises on one), and caching whole static layers as pictures (the views move every frame; the profile shows rasterising, not recording, is the cost).
- **Background render:** one at a time (`MusicVideoJobs`), a chip over the app with progress, Cancel, then Save or why it failed; it continues only while the app is open, with no foreground service.
- **Storage:** `music_video` `version: 2` (texts, image assets and layers, camera moves, the new transitions and drive, 24 fps, quality); version 1 reads as it was; bounded: 32 texts and 16 images a shot or video, 32 assets.
- **No new dependency and no engine C API change** (`file_picker` was already in use).

## 2026-11-12: Audio keeps playing when the microphone is busy or the app is behind (owner)

The owner heard audio break on the Galaxy S22+ when another app used the microphone and when C.A.W was out of focus, worse the longer it played; with input off it was fine in front (confirmed on the phone). Cause ([Fact], miniaudio 0.11.25 source, read 2026-11-12): once input was on, the device ran duplex, and on AAudio duplex playback calls the engine only with frames capture delivered (`ma_device__handle_duplex_callback_playback`); a reroute (`ma_device_reinit__aaudio`) closes both streams and after failed retries stops the device while `caw_audio_status` still said running; and Android freezes a cached background app with no foreground service.

- **Two devices, joined by a lock-free bridge:** a playback-only device and a capture-only device (`engine/src/input_bridge.h`); a missing input is silence, and the output never depends on the microphone ([engine](engine.md#audio-input-and-recording)).
- **The microphone only while it's needed:** an armed track, a monitoring track (`on`), a recording or the latency ping; released behind the app unless recording ([app](app.md)).
- **Real running status:** `"running"` from `ma_device_get_state`; a resume or the app's status check reopens a device the system stopped.
- **"Microphone in use by another app"** where the input meters are, while playback goes on and the input is retried quietly.
- **An Android foreground service of type `mediaPlayback`** (owner-approved), plus `microphone` while recording: it runs while playing or recording, its notification says *C.A.W is playing* or *recording* with Play/Pause and Stop wired to the transport, and it stops when playback stops. Not exported; explicit `FLAG_IMMUTABLE` PendingIntents; POST_NOTIFICATIONS asked on Android 13+ the first time something plays. [Fact] (developer.android.com, read 2026-11-12: foreground service types; restrictions on starting from the background; launching a foreground service; notification runtime permission; PendingIntent): `mediaPlayback` needs FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK and no runtime permission; `microphone` needs FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE and RECORD_AUDIO, and on Android 14+ cannot be created while the app is in the background; apps targeting Android 12+ cannot start a foreground service from the background; a foreground service runs without POST_NOTIFICATIONS, its notice then only in the Task Manager; Android 12+ requires FLAG_IMMUTABLE or FLAG_MUTABLE. Renders are not covered (they run in front). No new dependency (the framework's `Notification.Builder`).
- **After the audio review (Claude, 2026-11-12; C API 26):** the bridge follows clock drift with a fractional read rate (a PI loop, at most 2000 ppm) rather than skipping or refilling, so normal running has no splice or gap and only a real discontinuity is crossfaded; no input retry during a take (the gap is silence in place, and told); device opens outside the device lock; reroute jobs drained before uninit; a larger AAudio output buffer behind the app, restored in front ([engine](engine.md#audio-input-and-recording)).
- **Battery: a Settings row and a one-time hint (owner, 2026-11-12):** the test build played perfectly behind the app once C.A.W's battery was set to Unrestricted, so Settings › Audio shows whether it is restricted with an Open button to Android's page for C.A.W, and the first time playback goes behind the app while restricted a hint suggests Unrestricted (Open settings, Don't show again, saved). Not the `ACTION_REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` system dialog (the owner did not choose it). Hidden on Windows and Linux. [Fact] (developer.android.com, read 2026-11-12): `PowerManager.isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations(packageName)` (API 23) says whether the app is on the power allowlist; `Settings.ACTION_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATION_SETTINGS` (API 23) shows the list of apps that may ignore battery optimizations; `ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` (API 9, `package:` URI) shows the app's details, and a matching activity may not exist. [Assumption] on Samsung One UI the app's details page is one tap (Battery) from the per-app Unrestricted choice, closer than the all-apps list.

## 2026-11-12: Shed layers come back on their own

**Decision (the engine lead, asked for by the coordinator after the owner's report):** layers the engine mutes because the device ran out of time return by themselves once the load has stayed low, one at a time with a fade, where they fit; a short stall never sheds; a device open and the app going to or from the background start afresh; offline renders never shed. The "Out of headroom" note and Unmute stay (since 1.1.2 in the Performance sheet behind the CPU readout, not a banner; [app](app.md)). **Why:** a throttled stretch (the background, a Bluetooth reroute) shed layers one by one and nothing brought them back but Unmute, which the owner could not see in the background: the song thinned out until it stopped while the CPU meter looked normal. Thresholds and their reasons: [engine](engine.md) ("Overload, and coming back"). **Open (owner):** how the app should say that layers came back on their own (a quiet note was asked for; no UI was built without the owner's look).

## 2026-11-12: The engine renders in fixed blocks at the device (owner's idea)

**Decision (owner's idea, with the engine lead's agreement):** the engine always renders in fixed-size blocks, whatever size the device's callback is. The callback takes frames from a small FIFO preallocated at device open, which renders whole engine blocks when it runs short, so Bluetooth and background callbacks of 441, 960, 1920 or random frames never reach the engine and its behaviour is the same on every route and in export. **Built** (`engine/src/block_fifo.h`, the playback callback in `engine/platform/audio_miniaudio.cpp`): 64-frame blocks (`kBlock`, the engine's own chunk); a callback that is whole blocks passes straight through; otherwise at most 63 frames wait, and the reported output latency adds them; the FIFO is new at each open and keeps what it holds across a Play, count-in or Stop (the engine has already rendered those frames; dropping them clicked, found in the audio review); a small period that isn't whole blocks (under 256 frames) bypasses it. With the separate capture device (audio-resilience, merged 2026-11-13) the output is always playback-only, so the FIFO is used with the input open too, the input bridge read for each block rendered. The engine itself stays correct for any size (offline renders and other hosts call it with any size): a second line of defence, not a replacement. Details: [engine](engine.md) ("Fixed engine blocks at the device").

## 2026-11-12: No shared buses; work the REAPER way (owner)

**Decision (owner):** "I don't love the use of busses. I like REAPER's way of working." The shared reverb-bus idea (A7) is dropped. Routing stays REAPER-style: tracks, folders and sends between tracks, with no separate bus concept added. Speed for heavy Flagship patches comes from the engine itself.

## 2026-11-12: Files › Projects: select mode and folders (owner)

**Request (owner):** an easy way to mass-delete projects and files, and better-organised project files: a real integration rather than a list, with metadata (the version a project was built with, dates and so on) and folders to sort projects into.

**Look (owner's choice, "Select mode + folders"):** in Files › Projects, a Select button or a long press turns on checkboxes and a bottom bar ("N selected", Delete, Move to folder, Export, select all / none); Delete asks once and is undoable; mass delete also works for the other files Files lists; folders can be created, renamed, deleted (when empty, or asking to move the contents up), nested and dragged into; old projects show at the top level; each card shows name, last edited and created dates, the app version it was last saved with, track count, length and tempo; a sort menu (name, last edited, created, size) and a search box above the list. Metadata is read cheaply (an index or a small read, off the UI isolate).

**Choices (Claude, within the owner's look):**
- **Folders in an index file** (`projects/library.json`), not directories: project ids and file paths never change, and old projects need no migration (no entry: top level).
- **Undoable delete through a trash** (`projects/trash/<batch>/`), not restore points: the project and everything that belongs to it move in one rename each; Undo moves them back; the batch is deleted when the Undo message closes, or at the next start. Files' other items undo through project undo (one step), kept presets and set-aside WAVs.
- **New project fields** `created` and `saved_with` (`{version, build}`); old projects read as *unknown* rather than guessing from file times. `appBuild` (pubspec's `+N`) joins `appVersion`.
- **Last edited** is the file's modified time, which now changes only when the content does (saving identical text is skipped).
- **Export** of several projects saves each as a plain `.caw` (the project JSON) into one picked folder; the single-project Export project… keeps packaging samples.
- **Long press** on a project card starts select mode, as chosen; on other Files items it still opens the item's menu (whose new Select entry starts select mode), so their existing gestures and keyboard Delete stay as they were.

## 2026-11-12: Open alpha and beta: what the names mean (owner)

**Decision (owner):**
- **Alpha** means the app itself is unofficially released. The public line is the **open alpha**: v1.0.0-alpha.1, then -alpha.2 and so on. Anyone who downloads the app is an alpha tester.
- **Beta** means unofficially released content: tagged `-beta.N` builds of changes not yet in the public alpha. Anyone on the Beta channel is beta-testing the alpha.
- **Every change** is an opt-in channel with each Main build.
- **Releases:** pushes to Main never make a release. The owner tags a build once it is stable.

**Telemetry (owner confirmed):** everyone using the open alpha is a tester, so the alpha builds use the telemetry contract's `beta` channel: on by default, with the first-run notice. The `release` channel (off until the user opts in) is kept for a later official release.

**Automatic updates (owner):** an opt-in setting, off by default ("people don't like change on things like DAWs"), that installs new alpha or beta releases of the chosen channel in the background without the user seeing it, as far as each platform allows. It can be turned off at any time.

**Compatibility (owner):** from the official 1.0 release on, backwards compatibility with future updates is a priority: projects, settings and saved files from 1.0 must keep opening in later versions.

## 2026-11-12: Editing cues and one set of gesture rules for Arrange (owner)

**Decision (owner, through the coordinating session):**
- **Piano roll cursor** (look: like the arranger): the roll shows Arrange's edit cursor line and playhead line, across the whole roll.
- **Piano roll ruler:** a tap or click on the ruler moves the playback cursor there, snapped, and playback jumps there if playing; a drag along it draws a loop (snapped) and turns it on, with a handle at each end, exactly as Arrange's loop. **Choice (Claude):** this is the song's loop range, the same one as Arrange's: the roll's existing "loop bar" is the clip's loop length (one end, starting at the source), not a range that can be drawn, so it stays as it was and the ruler's loop is the project's.
- **Loop collapse:** in Arrange and the roll, a loop handle dragged so the loop has no length turns the loop off and removes its markers, on release; undo restores it.
- **Drag guides** (look: two thin lines): while clips are moved in Arrange, two thin full-height lines in the clip's colour at its start and end, through every track and the ruler, and the start as bar.beat in a small tag on the ruler; they fade in and out; a multi-clip drag uses the selection's overall start and end. **Choice (Claude):** a trim of either edge shows them too, at where the clip will start and end; audio clips show them as MIDI clips do.
- **Gestures for track headers and clips** (gesture changes only, the look unchanged): a single tap only selects; a double tap runs the quick action; a hold without moving opens the menu (the existing long-press menus); a hold that moves past the touch slop moves the item (a clip in time and across tracks; a header reorders the track, folders included) and closes the menu if it had opened; a finger that moves before the hold fires never gets the menu. With a mouse: click selects, double-click is the quick action, right-click the menu, drag moves. **Choice (Claude):** a clip's quick action opens its editor (a MIDI or drum pattern clip's notes in the focus view's Clip tab, the piano roll; an audio clip's Clip tab); a header's opens the focus view on its FX tab (the instrument and effects), which the double tap already did; on the focus view's pinned track a tap still returns to all tracks.

- **Notes in clips when zoomed out** (owner's choice "Thin note lines", replacing the density bars the owner disliked): notes stay at their real pitch as 1–2 px lines, the pitch range fitted to the clip's height with a minimum gap so lines never merge, a tick per hit on drum clips, and no switch between zoom levels. **Choice (Claude):** far out, where a lane's clips are blocks, the block becomes a light tint with the note lines over it, from the same cached pictures.

**Why the held menu is not a route:** a pushed route cancels every pointer under it (Flutter's `Navigator`), so the held finger could not go on to move the item; a menu opened by a hold is drawn in the overlay instead and closes on Esc, Back, a tap outside or a choice as before.

## 2026-11-12: Beta readiness: speed, smoothness, a stress test, telemetry and feedback (owner)

**Decision (owner, through the owner's other session; confirmed in this session):** the full direction is in the [beta readiness plan](beta-readiness-plan.md) and issue #187.
- **Engine speed beyond phases 1-5:** audio-thread scheduling with platform hints (A1), Flagship voices side by side (A2), the first callback after Play (A3), per-callback loops bounded to active slots (A4, #133), CPU-feature dispatch (A5), and a pool sized from real core types (A6). A shared reverb bus (A7) is a proposal for the owner only.
- **Frames:** the UI runs at the display's full rate, up to 240 Hz, and asks for no frames when idle.
- **Stress test:** a built-in test on Android, Windows and Linux.
- **Startup:** a startup screen in C.A.W's own style.
- **Telemetry:** anonymous, on by default in beta builds and off in release builds, and all of it published in `Kmandevr/caw-telemetry`.
- **Feedback:** a form whose reports are posted as public issues.
- **Split of work:** this repository builds sections A-G. The owner's other session builds caw-telemetry, the relay and the privacy and terms texts. Section H is the shared contract, and only the owner changes it.
- **Process:** every look (B settings, C, D, E, F) is asked of the owner before it is built. Anything that sends data off the device gets a security review.
- **Looks (owner's answers, 2026-11-12):**
  - **Startup (D):** a "waveform reveal". The crow draws itself from a moving audio waveform line. Then the real loading steps appear under it as a small list that ticks off each step.
  - **Stress test (C):** both a full-screen Benchmark screen and a card in Settings › Performance that opens it, with Quick and Full runs. Results show tier badges, the limits found, a ranked "what's holding it back" list, per-scenario rows and Share/Save JSON. While it runs, a live load graph shows.
  - **Privacy and feedback (E, F):** simple sheets.
    - First run is one scrollable page: a plain summary, links to the full texts, and Continue.
    - Feedback is a bottom sheet with type chips, a text box, attachment toggles that expand to show exactly what is attached, an optional email field, and Send.
  - **Frame-rate caps (B2):** Lightweight caps at 60 Hz and Minimal at 30 Hz while moving. Full and Balanced follow the Frame rate setting.
- **Built, D startup screen (2026-11-12; Claude's choices, details in [app](app.md), "Startup screen"):** the waveform reveal on a fixed #07070B background that the Android launch theme (no icon on Android 12+'s splash: the crow draws itself in Flutter), the Windows and the Linux window share; four real steps from `AppState.init`; the screen leaves the moment loading is done, cutting the reveal short with a 160 ms fade over the already usable app; still logo for Reduced, Off and Minimal. Startup numbers in [performance](performance.md#startup-screen-2026-11-12-beta-readiness-d2).
- **Section H updated (owner, 2026-11-12, through the other session):** the data repository is Kmandevr/C.A.W-Telemetry, the relay `https://caw-telemetry.kmandevr.workers.dev` with app key `caw-beta-1` (CI's defaults), feedback goes to Issues there, `errors[].where` is a relative C.A.W location, and the relay's format rules and daily limits are fixed; the list is in the [plan's updates](beta-readiness-plan.md#updates-to-section-h-2026-11-12-owner).
- **Stress tests are sent every time (owner, 2026-11-12):** each stress-test run goes out at once as its own report while telemetry is on, with whatever fields apply. One of the relay's 10 reports a day is kept for the daily report; past that, the newest result rides on the next daily report. Off: nothing is sent or kept.
- **One flow across both repositories (owner, 2026-11-12):** `PLAN.md` in Kmandevr/C.A.W-Telemetry is the contract's other copy; a contract change goes into both in the same session, with the owner's approval. Cross-repository references are written in full (Kmandevr/C.A.W-Telemetry#N, Kmandevr/C.A.W#N) so GitHub links them both ways, and a feedback issue that becomes app work gets a C.A.W issue linking back to it. Order of changes: the relay accepts a new field before the app sends it, and the app stops sending a field before the relay drops it.
- **Contract addition `app.build` (owner, 2026-11-12; the relay accepts it):** reports carry `app.build` and feedback a top-level `build`, both optional, 7–12 lowercase hex: the short commit the build came from (CI's `CAW_BUILD`, the first 12 characters of the commit). Local builds leave it out; `app.version` stays the real version from pubspec.
- **Contract decisions passed on by the relay session (owner, 2026-11-12):** section H takes PLAN.md's stricter rules (`maxClockMHz` at most 4 values, whole-number percentages, `topCosts` at most 10); reports are one daily plus one per Benchmark run, at most 10 a day; unknown values are `null` or left out, never 0; `/v1/delete` accepts any receipt and key and simply doesn't count unknown or deleted ones; release-channel builds use the app key `caw-release-1` beside `caw-beta-1`; reports carry an optional top-level `"kind": "daily" | "stress"`; Privacy stays at Settings › About and beta › Privacy, the name the published PRIVACY.md, TERMS.md and README use; user-facing text names the repository Kmandevr/C.A.W-Telemetry (caw-telemetry is only the Worker's name).
- **Device and peripherals (owner, 2026-11-12, a contract addition the relay accepts; all optional):** `device.platform` also "ios" and "macos"; `arch`, `osName`, `osVersion` as major or major.minor[.build], `manufacturer` and `model` (maker-defined only), `formFactor`, `screen`, `audio.output` and up to 16 `peripherals` (kind, connection, and for USB the maker's product string and ids). Never a name the user can set, a serial number, a hardware address, a port path or a full build string; the app enforces it, since the relay can't tell where a string came from.
- **Less to link a device by (security review of be0e745, 2026-11-12; the owner's rule "nothing that identifies a user"):** peripherals send no `model` (a USB product string can be set by a DIY board's owner) and no `inUse`, at most 8, sorted by kind; `screen` is pixels to the nearest 10 with no `dpi`; `model` comes only from the platform's maker data (Build.MODEL, DMI product_name). All are optional fields, so this is within the contract.
- **Names (owner, 2026-11-12):** the public line is "Open alpha" from v1.0.0-alpha.1; "beta" means tagged builds of unreleased changes, so the first-run page says "this early version". The `channel` field stays beta or release, and which one the public alpha uses is open with the owner. The UI calls the stress test **Benchmark**; "stress" stays the JSON word.
- **Built, telemetry and feedback (E, F, G1, G3; Claude's choices, details in [app](app.md#privacy-telemetry-and-feedback)):** no new package (dart:io `HttpClient`, the app's own SHA-256); Privacy under Settings › About and beta, so the existing Settings goldens stay as they are; "Open in repo" and the dashboard link copy the link, since the app has no way to open a browser; a build without a relay URL sends nothing.
- **After the security and code reviews (2026-11-12, Claude's choices):** nothing is collected or sent before the first-run page is accepted; Android backups keep projects and settings but exclude `telemetry.json` (backup rules, not `allowBackup="false"`, since projects in app storage are worth backing up); the release manifest asks for INTERNET (no plugin adds it); CI builds the release channel only for a final version published as a GitHub Release, so a pre-release such as 1.0.0-beta.1 is a beta build; `Retry-After` is capped at 24 hours; redirects are never followed; relay calls run one at a time.

## 2026-11-12: Frame rate: Auto by default, caps the screen has (owner)

**Decision (owner):** the app uses high-refresh screens (60, 120, up to 240 Hz where the screen has them), with a **Frame rate** setting (Settings › Performance; the app has no Display page). **Auto** (the default) runs at the screen's highest rate while anything moves and drops to its lowest when the screen is still, to save battery. Fixed caps are 60 / 90 / 120 / 144 / 240 Hz, shown only where the screen supports them. Playback follows the cap: one rate everywhere, playhead, meters and the music video preview. The OS has the last word (battery saver, its peak-rate setting): the app only asks.

**Why the platform channel:** no new dependency; Android's own window request (`preferredDisplayModeId`, `preferredRefreshRate`) is enough, behind `DisplayRateDevice`. Windows draws at the monitor's rate and offers Auto only. Details in [app](app.md) (Frame rate).

## 2026-11-12: The music video editor's open questions answered (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-12):** "do what you think best, but 1 for sure yes."
- **Full screen: yes (owner's decision).** The editor also opens full screen, as its own closable page like the full-screen piano roll; Back or Esc closes it. On phones it uses the whole screen with the preview on top and the lane and list below; desktop gets more room.

**Claude's choices with the owner's go-ahead (built 2026-11-12):**
- **Full screen, as built:** the toolbar's full-screen button (or F11, `video.fullScreen`) pushes a closable page (`closablePage`, so Esc and the mouse's Back button close it, as Back does). A phone stacks the preview (up to two fifths of the height) over the lane and list; from 900 dp wide the preview and lane sit on the left and the shot list on the right. It edits the same video as the sheet (its undo steps are its own).
- **An empty stretch of the lane** keeps showing the arrangement flythrough.
- **Effects stay a per-shot stack;** each effect has a target, the whole shot (the default) or one panel of the layout. An effect aimed at a panel the layout no longer has applies to the whole shot.
- **Strobe:** the first time Strobe is added the editor warns about photosensitive epilepsy (Cancel or Add Strobe); Settings remembers it was shown (`strobe_warned`). Strobe is marked ⚠ in the list's rows, its effect card and the add menu.
- **Falling notes as a default shot** for new videos only, when the song has notes: a project without a saved video starts with every shot on (Falling notes too, if it has notes); saved setups keep their shots, and the defaults for a setup without a shot list stay the four earlier shots.
- **The editor's keys join the action catalogue** in their own keyboard context, `video` (Music video editor): `video.add` A, `video.split` S, `video.duplicate` Ctrl+D, `video.delete` Delete or Backspace, `video.undo` Ctrl+Z, `video.redo` Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y, `video.nudgeLeft` ←, `video.nudgeRight` →, `video.previous` [, `video.following` ], `video.autocut` Ctrl+Shift+A, `video.fullScreen` F11; Settings › Keys rebinds them ([actions](actions.md#keyboard-focus)).

## 2026-11-12: The music video becomes an editor (owner, #182)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-12, answers to the design questions; extends "Arrangement music video" below).**
- **Cuts in two forms.** A shot lane under the song's bars: each shot a block to drag and trim, snapping to beats or bars from the project's tempo and steps per bar; cut markers that move; add, split, delete and duplicate. And a shot list with each shot's start, length, view and layout, effects and transition. Auto-cut fills the lane and you edit from there; re-running it asks before it replaces edits, or fills only the empty space.
- **Auto-cut triggers (all four), each with its sensitivity or settings:** every N bars or beats; bursts of note starts; tracks entering or going quiet; loudness jumps (drops, build-ups) from the existing meter data or an offline analysis. Heavy work runs off the UI isolate.
- **Layouts:** split presets you can resize (full, top/bottom, side by side, one big view plus a strip, 2 × 2), a view per panel, draggable dividers, a layout per shot, changes between shots cut or animate. The owner's example: the arrangement takes most of the frame with the mixers along the bottom, nothing changing out of sight.
- **Effects:** a per-shot stack like the audio FX stack (glow, shake, zoom punch, tint, blur, vignette, chromatic shift, strobe), each with an amount and settings, able to follow a track's loudness or the beat, the same in the preview and the render.
- **Look:** each shot or panel can change how its view looks: palette, note style, grid, glow, background.
- **Pitch bends** show in the note views (arrangement blocks, piano roll, falling notes), in the preview and the render.
- **A Falling notes view** like piano-song videos: a keyboard along the bottom, notes falling onto it as long as they last, the key lighting in the track's colour and the note glowing or bursting as it plays; a view for any shot or panel, with the effects; the keyboard fits the song's notes or shows all 88 keys; colours from the tracks with a per-track on/off; fall speed in bars visible; the theme's look, copying no existing app's art.
- **Storage:** shots, layouts, effects and looks save with the project, versioned, and older projects load.
- **Bug #183:** some MIDI notes were missing in the arrangement shot; find the cause, starting with a failing test.

**Built (Claude, 2026-11-12; choices to confirm, [App](app.md) "Music video" has the behaviour):**
- **#183's cause:** the video's scene unrolled a looping clip's notes from its source offset only (`at >= offset` each repeat), so after the loop wrapped the notes before the offset never showed; a clip trimmed on the left (which moves the offset) lost them in every repeat. Notes already sounding where the window starts (tails) were dropped and a clip that doesn't loop showed notes past its pattern's end. The scene now unrolls through `clipNotes` (`arrange_edit.dart`), the rule the arranger draws and edits with and the engine's `compileArrangement` plays.
- **Where it lives:** a Shot lane section in the Music video sheet under the preview (lane, toolbar, snap, zoom, then the list; the chosen row opens the shot's editor), and full screen (above). An empty lane keeps the auto director, so videos made before cut and draw as they did (the test's frame hashes are unchanged). An empty stretch between shots shows the arrangement flythrough.
- **Snap:** Step (a sixteenth), Beat or Bar; the lane never lets shots overlap (a drag stops at the neighbours; Duplicate moves the later shots inside the range along, cutting short or dropping what it pushes past the range's end; shots already past the end stay as they are). Shots wholly outside the range (a shorter song, a loop or selection range) keep their place: the list says so and its Start and Length steppers are off. An edit that changes nothing leaves no undo step, and nothing edits while auto-cut runs.
- **Auto-cut:** Replace replaces only what lies in the video's range (a shot across its edge keeps its part outside), Fill empty space fills only the range's gaps; a failure shows on one line. The triggers' cuts are merged with a minimum gap (2 beats by default) where the stronger trigger keeps its place (loudness, then tracks, bursts, interval); views come from the director's weighting with the setup's shots, style and seed. Loudness reads the notes by default (no render needed) or, chosen, the rendered audio: the range rendered offline by the engine's worker pool and its RMS per beat. Burst: 12 to 3 note starts in a beat after a thinner beat; loudness: a 12 to 3 dB jump between the bars either side of a bar line; tracks: a track sounding after (or silent for) a set number of bars.
- **Effects** apply to the whole shot (all its panels) or, chosen, one panel (owner's go-ahead, above), each redrawing the picture in order; drives are Steady, Beat, Bar (a pulse with a set length), Mix loudness and Track loudness (the render then asks the engine for stems). Strobe held steady flashes every eighth note. Looks are per panel; the default look draws exactly as before.
- **Falling notes** is a sixth shot kind, on for new videos of songs with notes (above) and off for setups made before; its keyboard spans the shown tracks' notes over the whole song (bends included, at least two octaves, white keys at the ends), so it doesn't jump at cuts.
- **Bends** draw as a line through the note's time, offset by its bend curve and vibrato (`NoteExpression.pitchAt`); notes without one draw as before. The project holds bends only as note expressions (no separate pitch-bend lane exists).
- **Storage:** `music_video` in the project JSON, `version: 1`: the setup, the shots, auto-cut's settings and the snap. A project without it (every earlier one) opens as a new video; the reader is bounded (4,096 shots, 16 effects a shot, unknown names to defaults, overlapping shots dropped, numbers clamped) and a newer version is read for what this one knows.
- **Keys (desktop, while the editor has focus):** A add, S split, Delete or Backspace, Ctrl+D duplicate, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y, ← → nudge by the snap, [ ] the previous or next shot; they are catalogue actions (above).
- **No new dependency and no engine C API change.**

## 2026-11-12: Perceptually identical, not bit-identical; speed first (owner)

**Decision (owner):** engine output must be **perceptually identical and consistent**, not bit-identical, across platforms, live playback against export, and repeated runs. This replaces every earlier promise of identical bits (the 2026-11-03 "Exports are always bit-identical" and golden-hash entries, and the 2026-11-10 "perceptually the same" entry, rewritten below to point here).

- **Still exact:** note, clip and automation timing to the sample; every seeded random choice (stutter, randomise, round robins, seeded noise); discrete behaviour (triggers, step choices).
- **Tests:** golden hashes are replaced by tolerance tests against the current renders, which stay as the references. Stable paths: the null residual against the reference stays below −120 dBFS peak. Chaotic paths (feedback near self-oscillation, self-resonant filters, free-running or unison phase): loudness over time within 0.1 dB and per-band spectrum within a stated tolerance. Discrete: identical note events, triggers and seeded choices. Real bugs (wrong gain, clicks, broken filters) must still fail, shown by a deliberately broken case.
- **Built (2026-11-12; Claude's choices within this decision, details in [engine](engine.md#tests), "Reference renders"):** the 141 former golden renders are references in `engine/tests/references/` (23.7 MB, every render's whole waveform), each sample rounded to 2^-21 (−132.4 dBFS), so a render within −122.4 dBFS of the recorded one always passes the −120 dBFS null. A case is chaotic when a probe (an FMA build, a fast-math build, or its input moved one float step) brings it within 6 dB of −120 dBFS: four high-gain non-linear effect settings; the other 137 are stable. Chaotic: bands (and below 20 Hz, for DC) within 0.5 dB, levels more than 60 dB down held at that floor, onsets within 2 frames.
- **Allowed for speed, where the tolerance tests pass:** flush-to-zero, FMA and fast-math (keeping the NaN/inf guards that protect the mix), system or approximate maths instead of `caw/detmath.hpp` on per-sample paths, float instead of double on signal paths (double stays for phase accumulators and low-cutoff feedback filters), and a parallel mixer that sums in a fixed order. `detmath`'s bit operations stay wherever exact bits are behaviour (bitcrush, glitch masks).
- **Built, speed phase 2 "floating point" (2026-11-12; Claude's choices, [engine](engine.md), "Determinism"; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#2-floating-point)):** flush-to-zero on the audio thread (every `Engine::process` call, so live, exports and renders alike, and sample-pack renders; an RAII guard that clears only what it set). The engine's own code builds with `-ffp-contract=fast -fno-math-errno -fno-trapping-math -fno-signed-zeros -fassociative-math` (GCC and Clang): each measured faster on Arm64 or x86-64, and every reference passes unchanged. Fused multiply-adds alone gave −6.5% of the typical song on Android arm64 and the subsets another −1.2% (−7.7% in all, −13.8% on the heaviest Flagship); flush-to-zero alone is within noise, kept as a guard. Not taken: `-ffinite-math-only` (it removes the NaN/Inf guards; a test now feeds NaN and Inf from a track), `-freciprocal-math` (no gain), a higher x86-64 baseline for FMA (it would need runtime dispatch) and MSVC's `/fp:fast` (Windows isn't the speed target; its NaN handling is unchecked). `CAW::SDK` keeps the strict flags, so the Library still gives the same bits in a component on every platform; the Library's exact-bit tests run in their own strict executable (`caw_exact_tests`), and every program that links `caw_core` builds with the engine's flags (the graph routing tests guard their expected values instead). Sample positions (`src/sample_time.cpp`) stay strict in every build, so note, clip, loop and MIDI-export timing is the same on every platform.
- **Built, speed phase 3 "maths" (2026-11-12; Claude's choices; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#3-maths)):** a rational `lib::tanhApprox` (within 5e-9) for every per-sample tanh in the built-ins, `tanApprox` for Flagship's voice-filter coefficients, and control-rate exps, pows and sin/cos worked out only when their inputs move; `detmath` and every older Library function keep their bits, and every reference passes unchanged. The typical song gains −3.5% on Android arm64 (−4.5% Linux arm64), under 5%, kept: the owner keeps every gain. Not taken: `fastExp` in the modulation path (a block-split bit test holds it to `denormalizeParameter`'s bits).
- **Built, speed phase 4 "precision and layout" (2026-11-12; Claude's choices; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#4-precision-and-layout)):** the EQ, Delay Pro and the Saturator staged over the run with a stereo pair's sides as one two-wide double vector (`src/stereo_pair.h`), the Library's `Oversampler2x` over a block, and a send's sum into its destination in float (the same bits). Gains, feedback filters, phases and delay positions stay double: the send and track gains in float moved a downstream crusher's steps (−77 dBFS against −120), so they were kept. Every reference passes unchanged; the typical song gains −5.8% on Android arm64 (−7.2% Linux arm64).
- **Built, speed A2 "Flagship voices side by side" (2026-11-12; Claude's choices; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a2-flagship-voices-side-by-side)):** the fold warp's decimators run a block at a time with a voice's two sides in one loop, and voices whose filters are set alike run them side by side (`MultiLadder::processPairs`, `Svf::lowpassPairs`), four voices at a time on x86-64 and two on Arm64 because four measured no faster there (their state spills); each voice is still summed in the voices' order, so the output is the same within the tolerance tests and bit-identical on the builds checked (x86-64 and Arm64; under the engine's `-ffp-contract=fast -fassociative-math` that holds by code generation, not by construction: the paired and single paths may differ by about 1 ulp, which perceptual identity allows). A voice that ends after the pool's walk is dropped from its list at the period's end (`VoicePool::dropSilent`; audio review, 2026-11-12). Filter and decimator state stays double. Two unison-copy changes that measured no gain were not kept. Flagship heavy at 64 voices: −19.9% on Android arm64 (worst callback 78.9% → 67.2% of the budget), −13.1% on Linux arm64.
- **Built, speed phase 5a and 5b "serial groundwork for the parallel mixer" (2026-11-12; the architect's plan, [engine performance](engine-performance.md#5-parallel-mixer)):** per-node scratch, sends and keys pulled by their destinations in the old summing order, per-slot modulation output, retirements held per node until the graph has run, and the mix graph sorted into dependency levels and run level by level, still on the callback thread. The same bits as before (every reference's digest and 200 random routed mixes); the cost is within 0.2% (callgrind).
- **Built, speed phase 5d "audio-thread scheduling and core-aware pool sizing" (2026-11-12; the owner's plan A1 and A6 in `docs/beta-readiness-plan.md`, Claude's choices within it; facts in [engine](engine.md), "Audio threads", numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#5-parallel-mixer)):** all platform code behind `engine/src/thread_tuning.h` with one file per platform in `engine/platform/` (the architect's 5d plan), loaded at run time with a silent fallback. Choices: **(1)** a worker's priority is never above the callback's (a spinning worker above it could starve it on a shared core), so Linux gets `SCHED_FIFO` workers only under a real-time callback (one step lower) and otherwise none; Android workers get nice −19 (`THREAD_PRIORITY_URGENT_AUDIO`) under AAudio's `SCHED_FIFO` callback; Windows workers join MMCSS "Pro Audio" only when the callback did, and C.A.W now asks miniaudio to put its WASAPI thread there (`wasapi.usage`). **(2)** Workers keep to the fast cores (a mask) on a hybrid CPU; the callback is never pinned. **(3)** ADPF's calls run on the hint's own thread, the callback only stores its duration (the NDK reference does not say `reportActualWorkDuration` is safe on a real-time thread). **(4)** Pool size: the fast cores less the callback's, at most cores − 2 on 4 or fewer cores, at most 4; cores within 15% of the slowest count as little. **(5)** Auto weighs the callback's wait for workers against the workers' task time: one worker fewer while the wait is over 10% of the budget and longer than the workers worked (0.5 s), none means one thread for 10 s, one more back after 2 s of little wait; the serial switch leaves the wait out of the load. **(6)** rtkit (D-Bus) is not used on Linux: it needs libdbus, a new dependency (**[Proposal]**). No new dependency; C API 24.
- **Built, speed A3 "the first callback after Play" (2026-11-13; Claude's choices; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a3-the-first-callback-after-play)):** an instrument of built-ins is reset once when its chain is built (control thread; one with a loaded component is not, as the native ABI does not say reset may run off the audio thread) and `ComponentChain::reset` with nothing run since is a no-op; each component's values are resolved when built, and a block where only the play state changed keeps the values of a component no lane drives (a Play still clears knob overrides). The first callback after Play went from 2–8× the mean to 1.2–2.1× on the Bench, with no change to what is heard (every reference passes). Relies on the component contract that `reset` returns a component to the state `activate` left it in.
- **Built, speed A4 (#133 item 2 finished, 2026-11-13):** `startPlay` and the stopped monitoring scan walk the in-use slot list, and solo is worked out once per Play instead of once per slot. Scans left over the capacity run once per event (Stop's end, a tempo change, ClearOverload, a device's all-notes-off, the previews' release-all), not per callback.
- **Measured, not built: speed A5 "CPU features" (2026-11-13; numbers in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a5-cpu-features)):** x86-64 builds target SSE2 and Arm64 builds NEON with FMA (their baseline). The whole engine at x86-64-v3 is 15–29% faster with GCC but no faster with MSVC `/arch:AVX2` (±2%), and MSVC, the shipped x86-64 compiler, is up to 2.3× slower than GCC on the same CPU. No runtime dispatch is built: where it would ship it gains nothing, `target_clones` would miss the engine-wide gain and MSVC lacks it, and a second AVX2 copy in one binary risks the one-definition rule. The Windows engine is built with clang-cl and the engine's flags since 2026-11-13 (owner, #199, above). **[Open question for the owner]** whether to ship x86-64-v3 as a second DLL chosen at load or as the minimum Windows CPU. A Windows x64 target (clang-cl since 2026-11-13) is in the Bench workflow.
- **Process:** measure first (a CI bench on Linux and Android arm64 with a baseline in [engine performance](engine-performance.md)), then one pull request per phase (free wins, floating point, maths, precision and layout, parallel mixer), each with before/after numbers. Claude asks the owner before any audible change. Every measured gain is kept, however small, even under 1% in a rarely used path: the engine should run fast enough for the weakest device. The only reason to turn a gain down is that it makes the engine unstable (owner, 2026-11-12, replacing the earlier "ask when a phase gains less than 5%").

## 2026-11-12: Third-party native components in a serial lane (Claude, [Assumption])

- **[Assumption]** When the mixer runs nodes on several threads (speed phase 5c), a node holding a third-party native component (one the user loaded as Trusted) runs in a serial lane: on one thread, never at the same time as another such node, until the module format can declare a component parallel-safe. **[Fact, `engine/include/caw_component.h`, 2026-11-12]** the C component interface's thread rules speak of one audio thread (process and reset "run on the audio thread") and say nothing of two instances processing at once, so a library may keep shared state that is not safe across threads; those components are in-process, unsandboxed code. The built-ins are the engine's own code and can run in parallel. To check with the owner before 5c ships; the declaration in the module format is future work. Built with 5c (2026-11-12, branch `speed-parallel`): a node whose instrument, inserts, a chain fading out or per-note FX racks hold a loaded component (`ComponentChain::foreign`) runs on the callback thread, before the level's other nodes go to the pool, so no two such nodes ever run at once.

## 2026-11-12: Default project (owner, 2026-11-12)

- **Decision (owner):** "Here's the project I made, that will essentially be the default." The owner's "mid tempo" project (90 BPM, A minor; Track 3, Bass, Drums, Layer 4 on Flagship, the 808 kit and built-in effects) is what a first run, a deleted last project and the bundled project template open.
- **How:** the project.json from the owner's exported .caw package is bundled as `app/assets/projects/default.json`. Its unused C++ component and the package's device binary (.so) were left out: a bundled default must not carry native code (in-process, not sandboxed; see the trust decision) or depend on a compiler. Each copy gets fresh ids with references remapped, so two copies never collide (`lib/model/default_project.dart`). The bundled template keeps the id `builtin:first-loop`, now labelled *Mid tempo*, so a saved "New project starts from" setting still finds it.
- **Kept:** the code-built First loop stays as `AppState.demoProject()`, a test and benchmark fixture whose shape many tests depend on; the engine's First loop goldens and `caw_bench` scenario encode their own projects and do not change.

## 2026-11-11: one plug-in page per C++ component (owner's "the plug-in workflow is confusing"; Claude, a proposal for the owner's review)

**Problem:** a C++ component's Test suite, Export as plug-in and Make sample pack were three separate pages behind the editor's ⋯ menu, with build status only in the editor and nothing linking one step to the next.

**What established tools do (primary sources, read 2026-11-11):**
- **JUCE Projucer:** each project lists its *exporters* (one per IDE or build system), each carrying its own build configurations, Debug and Release by default; the generated IDE projects are rebuilt from the Projucer project on save, so settings live in one place ([JUCE tutorial "Manage your Projucer project"](https://docs.juce.com/master/tutorial_manage_projucer_project.html)). Lesson: one home per artefact, targets listed side by side.
- **iPlug2:** one plug-in codebase targets CLAP, VST2, VST3, AUv2, AUv3, AAX, WAM and standalone apps ([iPlug2 README](https://github.com/iPlug2/iPlug2)). Lesson: formats are targets of one source, not separate workflows.
- **Steinberg VST3 SDK:** the *validator* is a command-line host that checks a plug-in's VST3 conformity and suits build-server use; the *Plug-in Test Host* has a unit-tests window with Run All / Run Selected, errors in an Errors view and warnings and results in a Messages view ([VST3 portal: Validator](https://steinbergmedia.github.io/vst3_dev_portal/pages/What+is+the+VST+3+SDK/Validator.html), [Plug-in Test Host](https://steinbergmedia.github.io/vst3_dev_portal/pages/What+is+the+VST+3+SDK/Plug-in+Test+Host.html)). Lesson: validate before shipping, errors apart from warnings.
- **CLAP:** `clap-validator validate <plugin.clap>` runs an automatic test suite for common bugs, with `--only-failed` to show just failures ([clap-validator](https://github.com/free-audio/clap-validator)); `clap-host` is a reference host to load a plug-in and play it from the keyboard ([clap-host](https://github.com/free-audio/clap-host)). Lesson: a pass/fail summary first, the detail behind it.
- **DDMF Plugindoctor:** analysis modes (linear, harmonic, dynamics, performance…) behind tabs, two chains to compare or show the difference ([manual PDF](https://www.ddmf.eu/pdfmanuals/PlugindoctorManual.pdf)). Lesson: measurements grouped by question; compare A/B. (Our test suite already follows this.)
- **Xcode:** Archive, then the Organizer's **Validate App** (an automated check with feedback, before submitting) and **Distribute App** ([Apple: Distributing your app for beta testing and releases](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode/distributing-your-app-for-beta-testing-and-releases)). Lesson: one place per build, a validation step before export, and export offered as the next button.
- **Max for Live:** Freeze bundles a device with every file it uses so it works when shared; it takes effect on save ([Cycling '74: Freezing Max for Live devices](https://docs.cycling74.com/legacy/max8/vignettes/live_freezing)). **Kilohearts Snap Heap:** a preset built from Snapins works for anyone it is shared with ([Kilohearts: Snap Heap](https://kilohearts.com/products/snap_heap)). **Bitwig:** the Grid device as a whole is the preset, loaded from the browser ([Bitwig user guide: devices](https://www.bitwig.com/userguide/latest/introduction_to_devices)). Lesson: "make it a thing you can share" is one action from where you made it. **[Assumption]** Bitwig's exact save steps were not found in its current guide.

**What makes them clear:** one place per artefact; status at a glance (pass, fail, not run); validation before export; a next-step button; readable errors that lead to the code.

**Design (built, for the owner's review in the APK):** one **plug-in page** per C++ component (instrument or effect): Write → Build → Test → Export as a strip and a card per step. Each step has a status (DONE, CHECK for warnings, FAILED, NOT RUN, OUT OF DATE after an edit, RUNNING), its key result in one line and a button; the next step's button is the filled one and the bottom bar repeats it. Build runs in place and lists errors that jump to their line; Test opens the existing test suite and shows its verdict and thresholds; Export offers CLAP, VST3 and (instruments) Sample pack as targets side by side, opening the existing pages. Export warns, never blocks, when the latest build is untested, failed its tests or the code isn't built (the owner's "warn, don't block"). Reached from **Plug-in** beside ▶ Build (not only ⋯) and from the component's card menu on a track. Why: it follows the shared lessons above with the screens we have, rewriting none of their logic. **[Assumption]** The pipeline's record (test verdicts, exports) lasts for the app session and isn't saved in the project; the owner may want it saved. **Open (owner):** the look in the APK; whether the record should persist.

## 2026-11-11: Build tools leftovers (#162, #163, #164, #11; Claude, proposals for the owner)

- **Test-suite thresholds:** three switches, sent as the engine's `checks`: Headroom (both peaks ≤ −0.3 dBFS) and Not silent (both RMS > −70 dBFS) on by default, Real time (median block < 50 % of real time) off and only sent with CPU timing on. Why: the commonest ways a component is wrong (clipping, silence, too slow) with values a user doesn't have to type; the engine takes any path, so more can follow.
- **Instrument notes:** four fixed sequences (C4 1 s, C major chord, C4 at 32/64/96/127, C2–C6) instead of a note editor. Why: they cover pitch range, polyphony and velocity response on a phone with one tap.
- **Sample pack out of the app:** a stored .zip through the save picker, not a folder picker. Why: Android's picker hands back a document, not a writable folder path, and one file is easier to share.
- **VST3 in the export page:** one format per package (CLAP or VST3), not both. Why: it keeps the page and the generated workflow simple; a second export makes the other format. Folders from Steinberg's "Plug-in Locations" page (read 2026-11-11).
- **Learned CCs in a .mid export:** a lane on a knob with a learned CC uses that CC and channel, values on the knob's travel; a CC learned by two lanes goes to the first. The "mod-wheel-mapped target" is read as a knob with CC1 learned; a mod-matrix route from the mod wheel is not turned into CC1, since the lane holds the knob's values, not the wheel's (open question for the owner).

## 2026-11-11: the master loudness meter measures only while read (Claude, performance pass)

The live master loudness meter (BS.1770-5 K-weighting and the 4x true-peak filter, every sample) cost about 6 % of a three-track project's audio work, and nothing in the app reads it (`caw_engine_loudness` has no caller in `app/lib`; [performance](performance.md#song-scenarios-2026-11-11)).

- **Decision (Claude, a small assumption for the app; the owner may reverse it):** the Engine's master meter measures from the start until 30 s pass with no read, then stops; the next read starts it over as a reset does (`LoudnessMeter::sleepWhenUnread`, `engine/src/meters.*`). Its momentary and short-term values are then those of the last 30 s it measured, and the integrated value counts only the time something was reading. Export loudness uses its own meter and is unchanged; a `LoudnessMeter` made outside the Engine measures always.
- **No sound changed:** every other speed-up of the pass gives the same bits (no golden render was re-recorded), so it needs no perceptual check.

## 2026-11-11: the .caw project file and its package format (#159; Claude, choices to confirm)

Carries out the owner's packaging bullets of "MIDI clip loops, clip automation and project packaging" (below); [App](app.md) has the flows.

**Decision (Claude, for the owner to confirm):**
- **One `.caw` file, two forms.** With nothing ticked besides the project itself it is the project JSON (as `.cawproj` was). Otherwise it is a **zip** holding `manifest.json`, `project.json`, `assets/` (audio and samples), `components/<component id>/<ABI>/` (C++ builds), `presets/*.cawpreset`, `theme.json` (`{"name", "theme": the .theme file text}`); `plugins/` is reserved and stays empty. The reader tells them apart by the zip signature. Import also takes `.cawproj` and `.json`.
- **The manifest** (`"format": "caw.package"`, version 1) lists every dependency the export found, packaged or not: `kind`, `name`, `used_by`, `size`, `packaged`, `path` in the archive, `sha256`, `reference` (what the project JSON calls it), and per kind `component`, `abi` and `source_fingerprint` (builds), `preset_kind`, `embedded` (C++ sources and convolver impulses, which live in the project JSON) and `licence` (plug-ins: "unknown: not packaged"). A packaged file's references in `project.json` become `package:assets/<name>`, so no device path of a packaged file travels with it; a file left out keeps its path and its hash, so Locate… can check a copy.
- **No new dependency:** the zip reader and writer are our own (`app/lib/model/zip_archive.dart`, stored and deflated entries through `dart:io`'s raw deflate, no zip64, no encryption) rather than the `archive` package ([Fact] MIT, its `LICENSE` in archive 3.6.1 on pub.dev, read 2026-11-11): the reader is small enough to check whole, and every limit is ours.
- **Untrusted on the way in:** every archive name must be relative, `/`-separated and plain (no `..`, `.`-leading parts, drive letters, backslashes, control characters or links; zip-slip), and names are never used as output paths: files are written under names made here, inside the project's own folders. Limits: 20,000 entries, 4 GiB in all, 1 GiB per audio file, 256 MiB per build, 64 MiB of project JSON, 4 MiB of manifest, presets at their own 4 MiB, 1 MiB of theme; a deflated entry stops at the size it declared (zip bombs), and size and CRC are checked. A packaged file whose hash differs from its manifest is left out and named. Only files the manifest lists in their kind's folder are read, each at most once (a manifest naming one twice, or listing more files than the archive holds, is refused), and the 4 GiB counts what is actually unpacked, so an import stops and removes its files once past it. Paths in a project are untrusted too: only plain local absolute paths are looked at (no network shares, device paths or URLs; they become missing files when a project opens), and an export packages or hashes only the project's own files; Locate… checks the copy it made, not the file picked.
- **Builds through the trust store:** a build is unpacked only for this device's ABI and for sources it was built from; it asks Trust or Block once per build fingerprint (`library:<sha256>` in `native-trust.json`, beside the sources' fingerprints), and it loads only while that Trust stands (`NativeComponentManager` checks again at each prepare); the sources' own trust is still asked as before. A blocked or unanswered build is deleted and the sources build here.
- **Defaults in the checklist:** audio and samples start ticked; builds, presets and the theme start unticked (device-specific or the user's own library); presets offered are those for a device the project uses (a track doesn't record which preset it came from); the theme is offered when the one in force is a custom one.
- **On import:** presets join the user's under a free name; the theme waits for **Add theme** (`Settings.addTheme`, which doesn't choose it); a file that is still missing shows the banner.
- **Open (owner):** whether the export should also offer to package recordings that no clip uses (they are listed under Files today and packaged when ticked); whether a plug-in's licence should be confirmable in the checklist once plug-in hosting exists.

## 2026-11-11: the .caw language is deleted from the engine (#160, WP-D)

Carries out the owner's decision of 2026-11-10 ("C++ replaces the .caw language"; old projects are dropped).

- **Deleted:** the compiler and VM (`compile.*`, `vm.*`, `builtins.*`, `synth.*`, `tables.*`, `program.h`, `ops.h`), the built-in libraries (`engine/lib/`), the function reference generator (`docs.cpp`, and with it `docs/library.md` and `docs/agent-guide.md`), the .caw example templates and their golden hashes, `language_test`, `variables_test`, `synth_test`, `library_test` and `golden_test`, the CLI's `check`, `templates`, `docs` and single-module `render`, and the C API's `caw_check`, `caw_library`, `caw_template_*`, `caw_engine_set_param`, `caw_engine_set_global` and `caw_worker_pool_submit_check`. The engine CI no longer checks or renders examples. `docs/synth.md` went with the patch compiler.
- **Kept:** pattern text (the note-clip format, #158) and the per-pattern result the pattern tab reads; the two example patterns (`examples/acid-line.caw`, `chords.caw`), because the app bundles them and its test reads them.
- **A track is its instrument and inserts:** `Engine::Chain` is gone (Install only makes a slot a track); one mix-graph node per track; no voices, seams, code swaps, swap timing, `.caw` params, globals, exports or imports (this ends #78 and #133's export budget). A layer without an instrument plays nothing. `LayerSpec.chain`, the project's `globals`, `voices`, `swap`, `seam_crossfade` and key fields are not read.
- **C API 21** (18 is audio, 19 modulation, 20 is not used, and per-note FX, merged after, is 22). A module that is not a pattern reports an error on line 1.
- **Tests play C++:** every engine test that used a .caw source as a signal plays `org.caw.test.osc` (`engine/tests/test_signal.h`) or a built-in. The mixer goldens moved onto it and were re-recorded on purpose; the Library parity tests keep frozen hashes of the ports' output, recorded while they still matched the .caw built-ins. Every other golden is unchanged.
- **Bug fixed on the way:** a track's instrument installed while nothing sounds counted as still ringing at the first Play, which mixed in 5 ms of a phantom tail (a free-running source started at twice its level); a new instrument now starts idle.
- **Open, for the owner:** an instrument track keeps running after a Stop until its output is quiet (or 30 s), and a never-quiet effect there now ends at that cap without the 20 ms fade buses get; a bus whose capped tail has faded comes back while a child track still rings out. The app still sends `voices`, `swap`, `seam_crossfade` and the key (Settings' light-mode voice cap and swap timing now change nothing): removing those settings is UI work.

## 2026-11-11: Latency compensation for Bluetooth and other outputs (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-11).** Compensate for output latency on Bluetooth speakers and headphones, and on any output with noticeable delay (USB, HDMI, casting).

The owner added (2026-11-11): inputs too (Bluetooth headsets' and USB microphones, a per-input offset, the ping split into input and output, takes placed by input + output), and meters, note highlights and the playhead in step with what is heard (a timeline of meter reads read at the heard time).

**Built (Claude, branch `latency-comp`; the look of the page and badge for the owner's review):**
- **Measure:** the engine reports each side's latency (C API 23, `caw_audio_latency`; [engine](engine.md#audio-input-and-recording)): AAudio's timestamps where it has them, else the queued buffers. The routed device comes from Android's `AudioManager` (`AudioRoute.kt`) and its device callback; on Windows from the endpoint's bus and form factor (WASAPI property store), else its name. Each device keeps an offset on top of the platform's figure, preset by its kind, typed in, tapped along (median of eight taps) or, for an input, split from the loopback ping.
- **Apply:** `AppState.playheadStep` is the heard position (so the playhead, follow, live MIDI and automation writing move with it); meters read `audibleMeters`/`audibleLayerMeters` everywhere (the arranger playhead and master meter, the mixer strips, track meters, track cards, the transport position and the music video preview; CPU, overload and `playing` stay on `meters`); MIDI takes are placed at the heard step, audio takes by output + input latency; the count-in countdown waits the output latency. The metronome and count-in clicks are rendered with the music, so they are heard with the same delay and need nothing.
- **Show:** `LatencyBadge` (+N ms, the tip on tap) while the output adds more than 50 ms.
- **Facts read 2026-11-11:** [Fact] the NDK's AAudio reference: `AAudioStream_getTimestamp` "returns the time at which a particular frame was played on a speaker or headset, or was recorded on a microphone"; `AAudioStream_get*()` may be called from the data callback (not done here: the engine reads it on the caller's thread); device types `AAUDIO_DEVICE_BLUETOOTH_A2DP`, `BLE_HEADSET`, `BLE_SPEAKER`, `BLE_BROADCAST` ([NDK audio reference](https://developer.android.com/ndk/reference/group/audio)). **[Fact, read 2026-11-12]** `AudioManager.getDevices` and `registerAudioDeviceCallback` were added in API level 23 and `getAudioDevicesForAttributes` in 33 ([AudioManager](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/media/AudioManager)); the `AudioDeviceInfo` TYPE_* values the app maps (A2DP 8, SCO 7, BLE headset 26, BLE speaker 27, BLE broadcast 30, BLE hearing aid 33 (API 37), USB headset 22, HDMI 9, eARC 29 and the rest) match the reference ([AudioDeviceInfo](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/media/AudioDeviceInfo)). [Fact, read 2026-11-12] Windows' `PKEY_AudioEndpoint_FormFactor` holds an `EndpointFormFactor` (Headphones, Headset, SPDIF, DigitalAudioDisplayDevice for HDMI or DisplayPort, …) ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/mmdeviceapi/ne-mmdeviceapi-endpointformfactor)); **[Assumption]** a Bluetooth endpoint's bus name is `BTHENUM` (classic) or `BTHLE…` (LE). **[Assumption]** whether A2DP's link delay is inside AAudio's timestamps is undocumented; a published phone test measured average Bluetooth latency from about 244 ms to 484 ms by handset ([Android Authority](https://androidauthority.com/smartphone-bluetooth-latency-973198/)), so presets are typical figures (Bluetooth 200 ms, LE 100 ms, a Bluetooth microphone 150 ms) meant to be calibrated. **[Assumption]** a tap along includes the finger's own touch delay (tens of ms on phones, not measured).
- **Open (owner):** the page's and badge's look in the APK, and where the badge sits (the transport row belongs to another branch: it places `LatencyBadge`); whether compensation should default off for wired outputs.

## 2026-11-11: Every change animates (owner)

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-11):** "As a rule of thumb, if anything changes it should animate to its changed state." That covers moves, size changes, things appearing and disappearing, and panels and pages opening and closing, both in and out.
